# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code when working in this repository.

# CardTastic (Solitaire until 2026-11-11)

Eleven classic solitaire games in one ad-free, self-contained app (Klondike, Spider, FreeCell, Pyramid, Yukon, Golf, TriPeaks, Canfield, Scorpion, Forty Thieves, Aces Up, in `src/games/`), and since 2026-11-04 games for two players: RatSlap (Egyptian Ratscrew), then Slapjack, Six-Card Golf and Trash, then War and Speed, then Gin Rummy, the first whose hands are hidden (pass and play), Four Crowns, the family's rummy for up to four, and Crazy Eights (see "RatSlap", "Slapjack, Six-Card Golf and Trash", "War and Speed", "Gin Rummy", "Four Crowns", "Rule choices and suit marks" and "Crazy Eights" at the end), built with Expo. Part of the `@rific`/`@tastic`/InfiniteToken app ecosystem - depends on `@rific/auto-paper`, `@rific/feedback-press`, `@rific/focus-chain`, `@rific/gestures`, `@rific/splash-gate`, `@rific/toaster`, plus `@tastic/profile` (+ its `@tastic/core`/`@tastic/hud` peers) for player identity (a color + short tag, shared across the fleet via an iOS App Group). Since 2026-11-03 it is a `@tastic/shell` pack like DuelTastic and PuckTastic: the root, Home, the stats screen, Profiles, the store and the player are the shell's, and the eleven play screens and everything those screens show are Solitaire's (see "Solitaire on @tastic/shell" at the end). It was renamed CardTastic on 2026-11-05 (the label under the icon, Home's title, and the App Store name, "CardTastic: Solitaire & More" since "CardTastic" alone was taken there), and on 2026-11-11 the repo, the folder, the scheme, the Android package, the web path and the Vercel project followed; the iOS bundle ID, the EAS slug and the `solitaire:` storage prefix keep Solitaire (see "The rename finished" at the end). The new build, listing and art are still to come (TODO.md's rename section). Much of this file still says Solitaire for the app: it was written before the rename. Unlike the fleet's physics-driven games, there's still no board-physics or split-screen engine here, just tap/drag card-game logic. A selected player's color/tag drives the cosmetic colors (the card back, the felt and the Paper theme, `src/components/theme.ts`, see the root-felt note below), every setting follows the player (see "Settings follow the player"), and stats are kept both device-wide and per player (`StatsState.profiles`, keyed by `Profile.id`, or `GUEST_PLAYER_ID` for guest since 2026-10-22, and filled for whichever player is active when a deal resolves, see `src/stores/gameStats.tsx`).

**Expo has changed.** Read the exact versioned docs at https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v58.0.0/ before writing any code - don't rely on general Expo knowledge, this app is on SDK 58 specifically (since 2026-10-16, see "Expo SDK 58").

## Commands

```bash
npm run lint          # expo lint .
npm run fix            # expo lint . --fix
npm test               # Jest
npm run typecheck      # tsc
npm run verify         # lint + test + typecheck
npm run doctor         # expo install --fix && expo-doctor
npm start              # Expo dev server
npm run client          # Expo dev server (dev client build)
npm run build:web       # verify, then the web export for games.infinitetoken.com/cardtastic/
```

Always run `npm run lint` before finishing any task. This is an app (`"private": true`, no publish scripts) - `verify` doesn't include a build step.

**Builds and OTA updates:** the `build` and `update` scripts are plain `eas build` and `eas update`, with profiles in `eas.json` (`development`, `preview`, `production`). Pass the profile through, e.g. `npm run build -- --profile preview` or `npm run update -- --branch production --environment production --message "..."`. Nothing in them runs `verify`, so run `npm run verify` first. An OTA update also bumps `otaVersion` in `src/constants/release.ts` (the version label players see), committed before publishing. They used to be `@rific/updater`'s `rific-updater-build`/`rific-updater-update` bins, which @rific/updater 0.7.0 removed; the package stays for its OTA update hook. `build:web` is the one exception: it chains `npm run verify &&` before the web export (see "Web on games.infinitetoken.com" below).

## Tooling

Onboarded onto the shared `@infinitetoken` config packages (`eslint-config`, `jest-config`, `tsconfig`) - previously hand-rolled its own `eslint-config-expo`-based config, `jest-expo`-preset-direct config, and `expo/tsconfig.base`-extending tsconfig (the same pattern documented in [Swirlio](../Swirlio/.claude/CLAUDE.md)'s and [BoxHockey](../BoxHockey/.claude/CLAUDE.md)'s CLAUDE.md).

`npx expo install --fix` + `npm update` genuinely found real staleness here - more than any other app surveyed so far: `@expo/metro-runtime`, `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage` (a full major version, `3.1.1` → `2.2.0` - the installed version was actually *ahead* of what SDK 57 expects), `expo-linking`, `expo-router`, `react-native`, `react-native-gesture-handler` (`^3.1.0` → `~2.32.0`, also a major-version rollback), `react-native-reanimated`, `react-native-safe-area-context`, `react-native-worklets`, `@types/jest`, and `jest-expo` were all misaligned with the installed Expo SDK, in both directions. **`npm update` alone wasn't enough to reach a stable clean state - a genuine wrinkle not seen in the other 7 migrated apps.** After the first `expo install --fix` + `npm update` pass, `npm outdated` showed `Current === Wanted` for everything, but `npx expo install --check` had newly drifted on two packages `npm update` had bumped past Expo's compatible range per their own loose semver (`react-dom` `^19.2.3` → resolved `19.2.8`; `react-native-screens` `^4.26.2` → resolved `4.27.0`) - `npm update` respects a package's declared semver range, which doesn't know about Expo's own SDK-compatibility matrix. Fixed by running `expo install --fix` a second time (pins both back to their SDK-57-compatible versions); confirmed clean afterward on both fronts: `npx expo install --check` reports up to date, `npm outdated` shows `Current === Wanted` for every dependency (remaining `Latest` values are all major bumps outside declared ranges, a deliberate-upgrade decision, not routine maintenance).

- `eslint.config.cjs` - `@infinitetoken/eslint-config/expo`, no local override. Confirmed directly, not just assumed: `@infinitetoken/eslint-config`'s own CLAUDE.md documents auditing all 23 real Expo apps in the fleet before this preset was built, and found Solitaire's old hand-rolled config differed from the other 20 byte-identical apps only by semicolons - a formatting difference, not a functional one. The plain one-liner is exactly correct.
- `tsconfig.json` - `extends: "@infinitetoken/tsconfig/expo"`, keeps only the path-valued local bits (`paths`, `include`) - no `exclude` needed (`.claude/worktrees/` doesn't exist here, confirmed via `ls` before deciding), and no `@shopify/react-native-skia` redirect (this app doesn't depend on that package at all - grepped `package.json` and `src/` to confirm, not assumed from precedent). Since 2026-10-29 `paths` starts with `"@/pack": ["./src/pack"]`, and it has to stay FIRST: `@infinitetoken/jest-config` sorts the aliases by regex length, `^@/pack$` ties with the catch-all `^@/(.*)$` (which maps to the repo root), and a tie keeps the tsconfig's order, so declared after `@/*` it never matched in Jest.
- `jest.config.cjs` - `@infinitetoken/jest-config/expo`, no options at all. The old `jest.config.ts` had `testPathIgnorePatterns: ['/node_modules/', '/.expo/']` (rather than the fleet-typical `.claude/worktrees/` entry) - dropped without replacement, since the shared preset's default `roots: ['<rootDir>/src']` already scopes Jest's crawl away from `.expo/` (which lives at repo root) regardless of what's in `testPathIgnorePatterns`.

**`jest.config.ts` became `jest.config.cjs`, `tsconfig.json`'s `types` array was removed (now defaulted by the shared preset), and `prettier.config.js` was deleted in favor of `"prettier": "@infinitetoken/eslint-config/prettier"` in `package.json`** - all for the same reasons documented in BoxHockey's/Swirlio's CLAUDE.md (`ts-node`/`tsconfig.jest.json` gotchas, the prettier-config duplicate finding, the fleet-wide `types` drift investigation). This app never had a `metro.config.js` at all, so there was nothing to check/delete there either.

**Native/Expo module mocks moved from inline `jest.mock()` calls in `jest.setup.cjs` into individual `src/__mocks__/*.ts` files**: `react-native-reanimated`, `react-native-worklets`, `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage`, `react-native-gesture-handler`, `react-native-safe-area-context`, `expo-router`. Each ported verbatim from this app's own pre-migration `jest.setup.ts` - several are genuinely narrower than their same-named counterparts elsewhere in the fleet (e.g. this app's `react-native-gesture-handler` mock only covers `State`/`Gesture.Pan`/`Gesture.Simultaneous`/`GestureDetector`/`GestureHandlerRootView`, no `PanGestureHandler`/`Pinch`/`Race`/`Sequence`; its `react-native-reanimated` mock has no `useAnimatedScrollHandler`/`measure`/`scrollTo`/`Extrapolation` - this app doesn't use those APIs), not drift to converge away.

**`jest.setup.cjs` ended up narrower than the fleet's usual shape, on purpose.** This app's own pre-migration `jest.setup.ts` never had `unhandledRejection`/`uncaughtException` process handlers or the try/catch `NativeAnimatedHelper` automock that most other migrated apps carry forward - so none were invented here (per the migration rule: don't add setup-file content, same as not inventing a mock, that wasn't already there). `jest.setup.cjs` now holds exactly what this app genuinely had: the `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` flag and the RAF/cancelAnimationFrame polyfills, with TS type annotations stripped (plain `.cjs`, loaded via `require()`, no transform).

**Migrating onto `typescript-eslint`'s `recommended` ruleset (via the shared preset) escalated `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` to an error for the first time, surfacing 9 real findings across 7 component files** - every one the identical `style?: any;` passthrough-prop pattern (a component forwarding its `style` prop into a `View`/`Pressable`/`Svg`/`Animated.View`), never previously caught because the old hand-rolled config never composed on `typescript-eslint.configs.recommended` at all. Fixed by typing each as `StyleProp<ViewStyle>` (imported from `react-native`) instead of `any`: `src/components/PileView.tsx` (`PileViewProps.style`, `DraggableTableauCardProps.cardStyle`), `src/components/PlayingCard.tsx` (`PlayingCardProps.style`, `EmptySlot`'s inline `style`, `FoundationSlot`'s inline `style`), `src/components/StockWaste.tsx` (`StockWasteProps.style`), `src/components/cards/SuitPip.tsx` (`SuitPipProps.style`), `src/components/dragdrop/DraggableCard.tsx` (`DraggableCardProps.style`), `src/components/dragdrop/DropZone.tsx` (`DropZoneProps.style`).

**A large pre-existing `prettier/prettier` warning backlog was left untouched - verified pre-existing, not migration-introduced, by directly re-running lint under the old hand-rolled config for comparison** (3081 warnings under the old config vs. 3068 after the full migration + fixes, both 0 errors; same prettier options either way - `jsxSingleQuote`/`printWidth: 1000`/`semi: false`/`singleQuote`/`trailingComma: 'none'`, byte-identical between the old standalone `prettier.config.js` and the new shared `@infinitetoken/eslint-config/prettier`). The app's source consistently uses semicolons and trailing commas despite `semi: false`/`trailingComma: 'none'` being configured all along - this codebase appears to have never actually been run through `--fix`/a formatter, in any prior state. Not fixed here (out of scope for a tooling-config swap, same as BoxHockey leaving its own pre-existing backlog); `npx eslint . --fix` would resolve the bulk of it in one pass whenever that's wanted.

**Migrating onto `@infinitetoken/tsconfig/expo` turned on `noUnusedLocals`/`noUnusedParameters` for the first time**, surfacing:
- 6 dead `import React from 'react'` default imports (named imports still needed, `React` itself never referenced) - `src/app/settings.tsx`, `src/components/dragdrop/DraggableCard.tsx`, `src/components/PileView.tsx`, `src/games/freecell/FreeCellScreen.tsx`, `src/games/klondike/KlondikeScreen.tsx`, `src/games/pyramid/PyramidScreen.tsx`, `src/games/spider/SpiderScreen.tsx` - removed, keeping the named imports.
- One dead named import, `src/games/pyramid/PyramidScreen.tsx:17` (`flatIndex` imported from `./rules`, never referenced) - removed from the import list.
- One dead local test helper, `src/__tests__/games/freecell/rules.test.ts:151` (`stateWithRoom()`, a `FreeCellState` factory function declared but never called - every test in that `describe` block builds its own inline state object instead) - removed entirely.
- 3 unused *middle* callback parameters (`indexFromTop` in a `(card, indexFromTop, origin) => ...` `onPressCard` handler, with `origin` still used after it - `src/games/freecell/FreeCellScreen.tsx:270`, `src/games/klondike/KlondikeScreen.tsx:278`, `src/games/spider/SpiderScreen.tsx:270`). Unlike AirHockey's `buildGoalPosts` case, these can't just be dropped from the signature (a later positional parameter is still used) - prefixed with `_` instead (`_indexFromTop`), which both ESLint's `^_`-ignore convention and TypeScript's own `noUnusedParameters` treat as "deliberately unused, keeping the position."

`@infinitetoken/eslint-config` (`^0.2.0`), `@infinitetoken/jest-config` (`^0.2.3`), and `@infinitetoken/tsconfig` (`^0.4.1`) are all on real published versions - never yalc-linked for this app. `eslint-plugin-package-json` and `ts-node` were removed from `devDependencies` (both redundant under the shared presets).

**Skia and `@tastic/split-screen` are gone (2026-10-18): every hud import is `'@tastic/hud/chrome'` (or `'@tastic/hud/guide'`), never the bare `'@tastic/hud'`.** hud's main entry is one barrel that also re-exports the per-seat lobby and the Skia gauge (`TriggerGaugeHost`), so any import from it made Metro and `tsc` resolve `@shopify/react-native-skia` and `@tastic/split-screen`, whichever names were used. Solitaire never drew anything with Skia, yet it had to install both, plus a `"postinstall": "setup-skia-web"` script and the `public/canvaskit.wasm` it copied for the web target. `@tastic/profile` 0.16.0 imports hud only through its light `./chrome` entry, and Solitaire's own 13 files moved there the same day (every name they use is exported by it), so nothing installed reaches hud's main entry any more and all four went. `eslint.config.cjs` bans the bare specifier (`no-restricted-imports`), and `src/__tests__/hudImports.test.ts` checks every quoted hud specifier under `src/`, which also catches a `jest.mock()` or `jest.requireActual()` the lint rule can't see: a test that wraps a hud export mocks `'@tastic/hud/chrome'`, the specifier the source imports. The hud/profile import cycle went too (`./chrome` never imports profile), so no test imports one package first or wraps a mock in a Proxy any more. **Dropping Skia removes a native module, so it changes the fingerprint and needs a new native build, not an OTA** (the same build `@tastic/profile` 0.16.0 already needs, since profile is native too). Verified with `npm run verify` and a `WEB_BASE_URL=/solitaire` web export, which bundles with no Skia, CanvasKit or split-screen code and no `.wasm` (Home, Klondike, Settings and Achievements load from it with no console errors). Not run on a device or simulator.

**`FeedbackPressProvider` needs an explicit `paper={RNPaper}` prop (`import * as RNPaper from 'react-native-paper'`) or every Paper-mapped wrapper it injects - critically `@tastic/hud`'s/`@tastic/profile`'s internal `TouchableRipple` usage (the color-swatch trigger, profile rows, the "New Profile" row) - silently falls back to a plain `Pressable` that drops the `style` prop entirely.** Before this was wired, the Player section's color-swatch trigger in `ProfilesManager`/`InlineColorPicker` rendered with no background fill and a tiny, wrongly-sized hit box (its `backgroundColor`/`width`/`height`/`borderRadius` inline style silently discarded) - confirmed by reading `@rific/feedback-press/src/components/paper/TouchableRipple.tsx`'s fallback branch directly. Fixed the same way BoxHockey's `src/components/Feedback.tsx` does it: pass the already-imported `react-native-paper` module straight into `<FeedbackPressProvider paper={RNPaper} ...>` (`src/app/_layout.tsx` then; the shell's `FeedbackBridge` does it since 2026-11-02). This was a real, previously-latent gap - Solitaire's own `Pressable` usage elsewhere never needed it, since nothing before `@tastic/profile` used any of `@rific/feedback-press`'s Paper-flavored wrappers.

**On the web target specifically, `@tastic/profile`'s `ProfilesManager` create/edit flow doesn't reliably commit via the Name field's Enter key, and its documented "commit on unmount" fallback doesn't fire either** - confirmed live: typing a name and pressing Return left the row still open/editable, and navigating away via `router.back()` (Settings → Profiles → back) didn't commit it, because React Navigation's web implementation keeps the previous screen mounted (hidden, not unmounted) across a stack pop, unlike native. Only a hard browser navigation (a real page reload) actually unmounts the screen and would trigger that fallback. This is a gap in the third-party package's web behavior, not in Solitaire's own integration code - `onCreate`/`onSave`/`onDelete` are wired exactly per the package's documented prop shape. Unconfirmed whether this also affects native (where screens genuinely unmount on pop) - worth a real device check before relying on the unmount fallback there. Worked around during verification by seeding `AsyncStorage`'s web-backing `localStorage` key (`solitaire.profiles.v1`) directly rather than fighting the picker UI.

## How-to-play guide (2026-09-24)

A first-run, replayable "How to Play" flow, built on `@tastic/hud/guide` (the light-peer subpath of `@tastic/hud`; see that package's own CLAUDE.md, "The guide subpath"). Same shape as LightCycles, Snake, AirHockey, BoxHockey and Pong. **The code word is `guide` everywhere, never "onboarding"** (the fleet's word for a round countdown).

- **Content:** `src/constants/guideSteps.tsx`: `getGuideSteps({ touch })`, three cards, pure and hook-free. `touch` is `useIsTouchPrimaryDevice()` and only swaps the verb (tap vs click). **Per-game rules are deliberately NOT here**: `RulesDialog` (`constants/gameRules.ts`, plus its shared Scoring paragraph) already covers each of the 11 games; the guide teaches the suite-wide things a first-timer can't work out alone, and every sentence has to be true in all 11 games at the default settings:
  - **Tap to Play** (Click to Play on desktop): "Tap a card for its best move. Pyramid pairs take two taps." Ten games run the game's `autoMoveHint` on a tap (each screen's `handleCardTap`, Spider's `handlePressCard`; the tableau pick is `engine/autoMove.ts`'s `pickBestTableauTarget`). Pyramid's tap only SELECTS (`handleSelectPyramid`; a tap on the partner removes the pair, a King goes on one tap), so the first sentence would be false there without the scope. **Dragging is deliberately not taught:** a drag can't make every move. The discard pile has no `DropZone` (`games/shared/DiscardCell.tsx`), so an Aces Up discard (its main move; the card still lifts because `hasLegalMove` counts discards) and a Pyramid King both snap back when dragged there (`AcesUpScreen`'s header and `handleDrop`, `PyramidScreen`'s `attemptDrop`). Making the discard a drop zone in those two screens would make a drag line true again.
  - **Lower Is Better**: "Score is moves plus seconds. The first move starts the clock." `engine/score.ts`'s `computeScore` (every game's readout, through `GameChrome`); `useGameSession`'s `registerMove` starts the clock (a stock draw counts); `statsEngine.ts` keeps the LOWER best score. The clock half is otherwise only in the Rules sheet's Scoring paragraph.
  - **Get a Hint**: "Tap the light bulb in any game to see a move you can make." `GameChrome`'s `lightbulb-on-outline` Hint button, in both layouts; `engine/hint.ts`'s `findHint` rings a legal move, or the stock when a draw is the move, and each screen's `handleHint` toasts when there is none. Home has no light bulb, so this doesn't describe what's behind the card. The info button is left out for exactly that reason: every Home tile shows it (the shell's Home, each game's `Rules`).
  - Art: `src/components/GuideArt.tsx` (`GuideCardPlay`, whose `pointer` is `'finger'` on touch and `'mouse'` on desktop, also picking the screen-reader verb; the pressed seven is outlined in round dots (the Hint picture's ring shape, traced by `roundedRectDots`: React Native's dotted border is square-dotted on Android and the web), with the click cursor in its lower right on desktop. It used to be a fingertip circle centred on the card, which cut across the big pip (it sits below the card's middle; Jay, 2026-10-17); `GuideScore`; `GuideHint`, the Hint button plus a ringed, glowing seven and the eight it goes on; only the card to move is ringed, as in a game). Plain Views plus Paper Icons/Text, colored from `useAutoPaperTheme()`: no Skia/SVG, and deliberately NOT the in-game `PlayingCard`/pile components or `@tastic/cards` (the mini cards are Views). None of hud's shared art (swipe, key caps, split phone) fits a card game.
- **Copy rules (Jay, 2026-09-24):** Title Case titles; one-word buttons (hud's defaults Skip / Back / Next / Ready, no `finishLabel`); bodies of 90 characters or less; no questions, no filler, no vague nouns, no em dashes; every sentence true for everyone who can see it (all 11 games, default settings); don't describe what's already visible behind the dialog; never tell a desktop player to tap or a touch player to click. **Plus a Solitaire-only rule: every body fits in TWO lines** of DejaVu Serif Bold (the app-wide Paper font, `ThemeProvider`'s `fontFamily`) at bodyMedium 14/20 on a 264pt page (a 360dp phone; `guideSteps.test.tsx` caps bodies at 62 characters as a proxy). A third line makes the turned card too tall, see Wiring. Measure a new body against the real TTF (`node_modules/dejavu-fonts-ttf/ttf/DejaVuSerif-Bold.ttf`, e.g. with PIL's `getlength` plus 0.25 letter spacing per character), since widths vary per letter. **Considered and left out:** dragging (see Tap to Play), the info button (visible on every Home tile behind the card), tap-the-readout-to-hide-the-footer (portrait only: false in the wide layout and in a desktop window), the shake on a card with no move (in Pyramid a shake means a failed pair), hint cycling (not true when the hint is a stock draw or the only move), Undo's hold-to-repeat (never checked with a mouse on web), auto-save (it shows itself the first time you come back).
- **Wiring:** the pack's `guide` (`components/packRoot.tsx`' `packGuide`: `GUIDE_VERSION`, the seen version from Solitaire's own `guide` key, `getGuideSteps`), shown by `@tastic/shell`'s `GuideHost` (one `GuideProvider`), which the shell's RootLayout mounts around the stack inside PersistGate and the auto-paper/Paper provider (since 2026-11-02; Solitaire's own `components/GuideHost.tsx` before). **The card is the one dialog here that is NOT under an in-frame Portal.Host:** `GuideProvider` renders it beside its children, so its Paper `Portal` lands in the nearest host above the shell's `GuideHost`, which is `FeedbackPressProvider`'s own `Portal.Host` (the shell's `FeedbackBridge` passes `paper={RNPaper}`), outside every frame. It takes no rotation (hud 0.34 dropped the prop; the OS turns the screen). The shell's Home shows it by itself on a first launch (`autoShowPack`, at most once a session), held back whenever the launch is about to drop into a saved game, so the guide can never open over a live deal (until 2026-11-02 Solitaire's own Home did, through hud's `useAutoShowGuide`). Settings row: the shell's `SettingsDialog`'s `onShowHowToPlay`, which only the shell's Home passes (`openPack`), **never `GameChrome`**: the score counts elapsed seconds, so a guide opened mid-deal would cost the player score.
- **Turned card height:** the fleet's rotation is fake (the OS window stays portrait), so a quarter-turned card's layout HEIGHT has to fit the window's short edge. hud handles that for every game now: `getGuideCardSize` swaps the axes for +/-90, widens the card, caps its height, shrinks the art under 480pt, and each page scrolls if it still doesn't fit (found in this app's review, fixed upstream 2026-09-24). Keeping every body to two lines meant that scroll was never needed at the default text size; since the card's "How to Play" header (2026-10-15, see Dialogs) a page fits a 402pt-tall window exactly and scrolls a little on a 375 or 360pt-wide phone held sideways. Not yet checked on a real device held sideways.
- **Persistence:** `src/redux/guideSlice.ts` (a `createGuideSlice` binding) mounted as `guide` among Solitaire's own keys (`redux/packReducers.ts`; its own key: Reset Stats never touches it). `GUIDE_VERSION` (`src/constants/guide.ts`) is what an existing player is stamped with. Players who already have a root store (otaVersion 4+, 464e3e1) are grandfathered by the slice itself. **Players last on otaVersion 3 (25532f0, 2026-09-12) or earlier have NO root store** (it arrived in 6dbe6e5), so the store's migrate (`pack.ts`' `storeConfig.migrate`) runs `createReturningPlayerMigrate(AsyncStorage, LEGACY_STORAGE_KEYS)` first, inside the shell reshape (`redux/shellMigrate.ts`, which leaves its stamp alone): the 20 literal keys 25532f0 really wrote (the 11 `solitaire.game-in-progress.v1.<game>` saves, `solitaire.stats`/`.achievements` via `@tastic/achievements` 0.1.1's namespace, settings, theme, last-active-game, profiles, guest color, sound, vibrate; per-key source in guideSlice.ts), read from that commit's source, not today's constants. otaVersion 1 (5f3078f) and 2 (f065784) wrote the same set. `solitaire.stats.v1.<gameId>` is NOT listed: those builds only ever read it. **Early-write rule:** nothing may write one of those keys before PersistGate opens, or a fresh install is taken for a returning player. Today `solitaire.settings.v1`, `solitaire.theme-settings.v1` and `solitaire.last-active-game.v1` belong to providers mounted OUTSIDE PersistGate (`SettingsProvider`, auto-paper's `ThemeProvider`, `LastActiveGameProvider`), but none writes on mount (auto-paper's `onChange` skips the first render, see `@rific/core`'s `createSettingsContext`; the other two write only from their setters) and every setter call at boot (`ProfileThemeSync`, the shell's `OrientationBridge`, the game screens) comes from inside PersistGate. Only finish/skip writes; a replay never does. Bump `GUIDE_VERSION` only for a real controls/scoring change: every player sees it again.
- **Not built:** any in-game entry point (deliberate, see Wiring), and per-game first-visit tips (RulesDialog is the per-game layer).
- **Tested:** `guideSteps.test.tsx` (three illustrated cards per platform, Title Case, bodies <= 90 characters and <= 62 as the two-line proxy, no questions or dashes, touch vs desktop differ only by the verb, the Pyramid scope, no drag promise, the exact score copy, the hint card naming the light bulb and not the info button, each card's art component and pointer, the art's screen-reader labels, the score picture matching `computeScore`), `guideSlice.test.ts` (this app's binding; `LEGACY_STORAGE_KEYS` namespaced, unique, all 11 saved-deal keys, no `stats.v1` keys; the migrate over each key gives `GUIDE_VERSION` and no keys or a failed read gives 0, also end to end through real redux-persist, including a fresh install's second launch after the theme key was written; the real `persistConfig` has the migrate, imported under fake timers because store.ts's persistStore leaves redux-persist's 5s failsafe timer), `components/gameChromeHowToPlay.test.tsx` (never in a game) and `pack.test.ts` (the pack's guide); the redirect guard and the Settings row are the shell's, tested in the package (Solitaire's `homeGuide` and `settingsHowToPlay` tests went with its Home on 2026-11-02). **Not verified live:** nothing here was run on a simulator, device or web build: the card's look (mini-card ink in dark mode, art sizes on a 320pt phone), the transform-rotated card under a fake landscape hold (the 358pt height is an estimate from hud's styles and the real font's widths, not a measurement; check it on a 375pt iOS simulator and a 360dp Android emulator held sideways, at the default and a larger text size), the Settings row, and the auto-show on a genuinely fresh install.
- **Packages:** the guide ships in the published `@tastic/hud` 0.13.0 (`./guide`, with `createReturningPlayerMigrate`). Solitaire has no yalc links any more (2026-09-25: `.yalc/` and `yalc.lock` removed, `@tastic/cards`/`hud`/`profile` installed from npm). If you yalc-link a package again for local work, never `yalc push` from it: it rewrites every project registered in yalc's store.

## Testing

- Framework: Jest (`@infinitetoken/jest-config/expo`, `jest-expo` preset)
- Tests live in `src/__tests__/`, mirroring the source subfolder structure for the games (`games/freecell`, `games/klondike`, `games/pyramid`, `games/spider`) plus a top-level `engine.test.ts`
- Native/Expo module mocks live in `src/__mocks__/`, one file per module (plus one scoped subpath), picked up automatically (no `jest.mock()` call needed) - see Tooling above
- Most suites are pure game-logic (`engine`, each game's `rules.ts`), but component-rendering tests exist too (`src/__tests__/components/`: e.g. `solitaireStats`, `dealLayer`, `dragGhostLayer`, the per-game `*Screen*` suites). They use the shared mocks in `src/__mocks__/`; a test that needs a particular window mocks `react-native/Libraries/Utilities/useWindowDimensions` (`winCascadeLayer`, `winCascadeHandoff`). Until 2026-11-06 they mocked `@tastic/core`'s `useRotation`/`useRotatedWindowDimensions` instead (see "The OS turns").
- `jest.setup.cjs` holds only the `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` flag (see Tooling above for why it's narrower than the fleet's usual shape). The RAF/cancelAnimationFrame polyfills it used to carry were removed on 2026-10-03 as no-ops: `@react-native/jest-preset`'s own setup (pulled in by `jest-expo`, and run before this file) already defines `requestAnimationFrame`/`cancelAnimationFrame`, so the `typeof ... === 'undefined'` guards never fired.
- `stores/profiles.test.tsx` pins `usePlayer` over the shell's `ProfilesProvider`, `profileSelection` and `seatColors` (2026-10-29): the self-heal in both places the roster is replaced (the shell's `pruneToRoster` after the initial reconcile and after `onRemoteChange`) drops a profile a sibling app deleted to guest, a roster replaced with the same profiles re-renders no `usePlayer` reader, and the color picks. Until 2026-10-29 Solitaire had its own single-seat `activeProfile` and did that self-heal inline.
- `store.test.ts` runs the real store (`pack.ts`) through real redux-persist: the keys, the saved boards kept out of the root entry, a 1.2.8 save reshaped in place (the old player keys and the lock gone), and a second launch changing nothing. Until 2026-11-06 it also loaded what the move left behind with 1.2.8's own store (`src/testUtils/release128Store.ts`, deleted with the rollback keys). It mocks AsyncStorage with an in-memory one of its own: `src/__mocks__/@react-native-async-storage/async-storage.ts` (a stand-in that stores nothing) wins over the official mock `@infinitetoken/jest-config` maps the specifier to. A `jest.mock` factory's result is shared by every `jest.isolateModules` registry once any module has required it, so the mock reads whichever disk the latest launch set, at call time.

## Architecture

```
src/
  app/          - expo-router routes (index: the shell's Home, play/[game]: its PlayRoute, stats, profiles)
  components/   - shared UI (PlayingCard, PileView, StockWaste, theme, card/drag-drop primitives)
    cards/      - card-face rendering (CardBack, CardFace, CourtFigure, SuitPip)
    dragdrop/   - drag-and-drop primitives (registries, ghost layer, draggable card/drop zone) -
                  supports exactly one flying card/group at a time (drag, tap-to-auto-move)
    dealing/    - the initial-deal animation engine (DealProvider/useDealAnimator in DealLayer.tsx) -
                  a deliberately separate module from dragdrop/, since a deal needs N independently
                  flying cards at once, which the single-ghost drag/drop layer can't do. Wired into
                  all four games, each with its own flight origin: Klondike/Pyramid measure a real
                  stock-pile/triangle view ref; Spider (stock is a fan of face-down packets, no
                  single view to measure) measures a real registered stock card via CardRegistry
                  instead; FreeCell (no stock UI at all) repurposes its far-right freecell as a
                  decorative deck placeholder shown only while dealing (see FreeCellScreen.tsx's
                  dealOriginRef) - freecells are never touched by the initial deal, so nothing real
                  is ever hidden under it. FreeCell also flies its cards in face-up (unlike Klondike's
                  forced face-down flight), matching how FreeCell actually deals in real life.
  engine/       - shared card/deck/pile primitives, RNG, auto-move
  games/        - one subfolder per game (freecell, klondike, pyramid, spider), each with its own
                  rules.ts + <Game>Screen.tsx; games/shared/ holds hooks/components extracted once
                  2+ games needed them (useGameScreenBasics, undoTypes, withGameProviders,
                  useDeferredDeal, useFoundationWinCheck, useDerivedWinCheck, FoundationCell,
                  WinBanner). Every game also has its own dealSequence.ts, layered on top of
                  rules.ts's unrelated
                  internal deal-slicing order - kept as separate per-game copies rather than a
                  shared import (see Spider's own dealSequence.ts for the explicit rationale), to
                  keep each game's logic self-contained. Klondike/Spider/FreeCell's actually
                  reorder column-major storage into round-robin visiting order; Pyramid's is a much
                  thinner pass-through (its own flat index order already IS the natural apex-first,
                  row-by-row deal order - nothing to reorder), kept anyway as the same named,
                  separately-testable seam the other three have, decoupling the deal's visual order
                  from rules.ts's storage order even though the two happen to coincide today.
  hooks/        - useSettings (the one way to read/write the persisted settings)
  types/        - Settings (the persisted settings' shape)
  pack.ts       - the @tastic/shell pack: the games, the hero, the root pieces, the stats options
  redux/        - the store (store.ts, built by the shell from storeSource) and Solitaire's own slices
  stores/       - hooks and module APIs over Redux state (game in progress, stats, the player);
                  nothing here touches AsyncStorage directly anymore
  testUtils/    - test-only helpers that must live outside __tests__ (jest runs every file there)
  utils/        - commaString, safeBack (navigation)
  __tests__/    - test suites (engine.test.ts, games/<game>/rules.test.ts)
  __mocks__/    - manual Jest mocks for native/Expo modules
```

**The deal animation's destination measurements raced the React commit they depended on, and silently dropped ~90% of the board when it lost.** `CardRegistry.measure(cardId)` (`components/dragdrop/CardRegistry.ts`) only resolves once that card's own registration `useEffect` has run - but the deal's initial implementation called `measure()` for all 28 destination cards *synchronously, right after* `setState(newTableau)`, before React had even committed that render, let alone run the new cards' registration effects. Confirmed live: this raced the *previous* deal's still-registered (but about-to-be-replaced) cards against the *new* deal's not-yet-registered ones, and mostly matched neither - 25 of 28 destinations came back `null` on one measured run, silently excluding those cards from `entries` and leaving them hidden forever (a card whose destination is `null` never gets added to `deal.start()`'s entries, so it never fires `onCardLanded`, so its id never leaves `dealHiddenIds`). The fix: never call `CardRegistry.measure()` synchronously after the `setState` that mounts the new cards. Instead, stash a "deal is pending" token and do the actual measurement from a `useEffect` keyed on `[state]` - React fires effects bottom-up per commit (children's before parents'), so by the time a *parent* component's `[state]`-effect runs, every child `PileView`/`DraggableTableauCard` for that same commit has already run its own registration effect. See `KlondikeScreen.tsx`'s `pendingDealTokenRef` for the pattern; `PyramidScreen.tsx`'s equivalent doesn't need it for the *destination* side, since its destinations are computed analytically (`cardPosition()`) rather than via `CardRegistry.measure()` - it only measures two container refs (the stock pile, the triangle container), which aren't subject to this per-card mount race. It still needs the same `[state]`-deferral for the *origin* side, though: those two container refs are themselves behind Pyramid's `if (!state) return <GameLoading />` branch, so on the very first mount they aren't attached yet at the moment `startNewGame` calls `setState` - calling `runDeal` synchronously right after that (as it originally did) measured two still-null refs and silently skipped the deal (cards just appeared with no animation, every time you navigated to Pyramid fresh). Fixed by giving Pyramid its own `pendingDealTokenRef` + `[state]`-effect, same shape as Klondike/FreeCell/Spider - confirmed all four now defer their initial `runDeal` this way.

**A second, unrelated bug found in the same session: reused React keys across separate deals silently stalled a handful of cards forever.** All 52 cards' ids are shuffle-independent (`${suit}-${rank}-${deckIndex}`, see `engine/deck.ts`) - a fresh deal reuses the exact same ids as the one before it, just redistributed. If `deal.start()` is ever called again while a previous deal's ghosts are still mounted (shouldn't happen in normal play, since the skip-overlay covers the "New Game" button during a deal too, but is easy to trigger via rapid/automated interaction), React reconciles by id and reuses the *same* `DealGhostCard` instance for a repeated id rather than unmounting and remounting it - meaning its `useSharedValue` position and its "already notified" guard ref never reset, since both only initialize on a genuine first mount. Fixed by folding a monotonic generation counter into each ghost's React `key` (`DealLayer.tsx`'s `generation`), forcing a real unmount+mount - and therefore a full state reset - on every `deal.start()` call regardless of id overlap with the deal before it. The same generation is also stamped on every slot (`deal`) and checked in every callback (`generationRef`, bumped synchronously in `start()`, which also `cancel()`s the old slots), because the old slots stay mounted - timers live - until the commit AFTER `start()`: a timer firing in that gap used to report a stale card into the new deal.

**Pyramid's pairing/King removal lands cards in a real discard pile (`PyramidState.discard`, `rules.ts`) instead of just deleting them.** A solo King flies as a single card via the shared `ghost` (`DragGhostLayer`/`useDragGhost`), same as every other single-card flight in this app. A pair instead flies as **two cards travelling independently at the same time**, each from its own real board position straight to the discard pile (`PyramidScreen.tsx`'s `startPairFlight`/`PairFlightGhosts`/`FlyingPairCard`) - not merged into one stack, and not one card visiting the other before either moves. The single shared `ghost` can only ever animate one origin/delta for its whole group (see `DragGhostLayer.tsx`), so two cards converging from different starting points needed their own small overlay instead - the same "N independent simultaneous ghosts" idiom `DealLayer`/`WinCascadeLayer` already use for the same reason (see `DealLayer.tsx`'s doc comment), just with each ghost getting its own independent origin/destination instead of a shared one. Each card's flight is its own `withSpring` at `FLIGHT_SPRING`, the one time every flight in this app takes, so the two cards of a pair leave together and land together. For a drag-completed pair, "first" is still sitting mid-drag in the shared `ghost` at drop time - its live position is read directly off `ghost.originX/Y.value + dx/dy.value` before handing off to its own independent flight and retiring that ghost (`ghost.hide()`). Two earlier shapes of this flight were tried and rejected first: originally "first" visibly flew onto "second" before the (already-merged) pair continued on to discard, as two distinct legs; replacing that with a single flight of the two cards already merged into one stack (skipping the first leg, but still one shared ghost) was also rejected - both read as one object moving, when the actual ask was always two cards animating independently to the same destination at the same time. The discard pile itself (`PyramidScreen.tsx`'s `DiscardCell`) measures its position like `stockViewRef`/`triangleViewRef` (a plain view ref, `measureView()`) rather than `FoundationCell`'s per-card `CardRegistry` registration - there's only ever one discard pile, not a slot whose occupant identity keeps changing. **Undoing a pairing/King removal now animates too, flying each card independently from the discard pile back to its own original spot** (`PyramidScreen.tsx`'s `undoRemoval`, `RemovedCardRef`) - the mirror image of the forward removal flight: one shared origin (the discard pile, which doesn't move) fanning out to each card's own destination, reusing the same `PairFlightCardSpec`/`PairFlightGhosts`/`startPairFlight` machinery (generalized so each card's spec now carries its own `to` as well as its own `from` - the forward flight shares one `to` across cards with independent `from`s, the undo flight shares one `from` with independent `to`s). `UndoEntry` gained a `removedCards: RemovedCardRef[] | null` field (the PileRef each removed card came from) alongside the pre-existing `move: MoveInfo | null` for stock draws - the two are mutually exclusive. **Every undo resolves ALL its endpoints BEFORE it commits, and mounts its ghosts in the same synchronous stretch as the revert** - a pyramid-cell destination is pure math (`cardPosition` + the measured `triangleViewRef`), a waste-bound card's is the always-mounted waste container (`wasteViewRef`; there is one waste slot, so it is the same place the card's own registration would say), and the discard/stock are plain containers too. The old shape (commit the revert, then a `[state]`-effect measured the destinations and only then started the flight) left the restored cards visible NOWHERE for a frame or two (the discard already showing its older top, the cards hidden, no ghost yet) before the ghosts popped in. Undo of a draw and of a recycle measure both endpoints first and do `ghost.show` + `beginFlying` + `setStockHidden(true)` + `commitState` + `flyTo` in one stretch; undoing a draw also freezes the stock slot (`stockHidden`) until the returning ghost lands, instead of showing the returned card at once. The restored card(s) have to be hidden (`pyramidHiddenIds` for a pyramid destination, reusing `flying`/`wasteHiddenCount` for a waste one) in that same commit - React batches same-tick `setState` calls - to avoid a frame where the restored card flashes fully visible before its ghost covers it (same reasoning `startNewGame`'s `setPyramidHiddenIds(dealtIds)` already relies on for the initial deal). A waste-bound card's `flying` entry ends with ITS card's landing (the pair ledger's `flying.cardId`), not with the whole pair, so a landed Ace is not hidden again while the Queen is still on its way. `dealHiddenIds`/`isDealHidden` were renamed to `pyramidHiddenIds`/`isHidden` to reflect that the same hidden-until-landed mechanism now covers two different reasons a pyramid cell's card might be flying in, not just the initial deal. A card whose destination cannot be measured (a rare edge case) simply has no ghost and is not hidden, rather than left stuck invisible forever. **Fast play:** pair flights are independent - `pairFlights` is a list with a per-flight ledger keyed by gen (a landing settles only its own flight; a single slot let the next pair replace the last one, so its ghosts vanished mid-air and its `activeFlightCount` claim never settled), and the discard shows the topmost card not in `discardInAir` under the ghosts (`DiscardCell`'s additive `settledCard`, the `FoundationCell` idiom). Every action claims its flight synchronously (`claimFlight`, generation-stamped) and **re-validates against `stateRef.current` after its measurement awaits** (`replayMove`, then one synchronous `commitRemoval`) - committing the pre-await `result` overwrote whatever committed meanwhile and let a double-tapped King be removed twice. Pinned by `pyramidScreenQuickPlay.test.tsx`, whose mocked measurements have a latency so taps really overlap. **Overlapping draws and the waste:** the waste slot's frozen copy of the pre-flight top goes stale once draw flights overlap (each landed ghost vanished onto the card from before the whole stream), so `wasteInAir` (the waste's `discardInAir`) tracks the drawn cards still flying and the screen passes `StockWaste` the additive `settledWasteTop` (the topmost waste card not in it; omitted - `undefined` - keeps the frozen copy, which every other game and every non-draw flight still uses). **A card is never acted on while its own draw ghost is airborne:** a tap/drop on it (or a pair completed with it) waits - `whenLanded`, re-checked at every landing, bounded by `DEFER_MAX_WAIT_MS` - so the card visibly arrives, THEN flies on (measuring the hidden real card would start a second ghost from a slot the drawn card has not reached, shown twice); a draw waits out a recycle's group ghost still flying into the frozen-empty stock, and a recycle waits out draw ghosts still landing (it would pick the airborne card up twice) - plain draws still overlap freely. The check comes AFTER the measurement awaits and does not yield when nothing is in the way, so an earlier tap's commit is never interleaved with a later one's. **Every such wait is a LOOP, not a single check** (`while (!gate() && budget.left() > 0) await whenLanded(gate, budget.left())`, inline at all three call sites - `attemptMove`, `attemptDrop`, `handleDrawStock`): being resumed is not proof the gate still holds, because a landing resolves EVERY waiter whose predicate held at that instant and their continuations then run one after another - the first (a recycle) re-freezes the stock for the next, which used to draw from the freshly recycled stock while the recycle's group ghost was still flying into it (stock [5], waste [2, 3], three quick stock taps). The loop must stay inline (a helper looping inside an async function hands its answer back across a microtask boundary in which a sibling can change it again), and its deadline is ONE budget per action (`startDeferBudget`), not a fresh `DEFER_MAX_WAIT_MS` per resume. **`commitRemoval` drops a selection that names a card it removes** (`setSelected((sel) => removed.some(refsEqual) ? null : sel)`): an action clears the selection when it starts and may then park in the wait above, and a tap in that window selects the waste as `{waste, 0}` - "whatever is on top" - which, once the deferred pair had removed that card, pointed at the card that was UNDER it (highlighted, and paired by the next tap: a move the player never chose). A selection of an unrelated card made during the wait is kept. **New Game/restore retires every shared-ghost flight still in the air** (`trackFlight`/`hideAirborneFlights`: a King's, draws' - queued ones included -, recycles', undos'), after bumping `dealTokenRef` so their completions are generation-guarded; dropping `pendingFlightRef` alone left queued ghosts mounted for good.

**Pyramid's win is a SWEEP DEAL, then one chain from one pile.** rules.ts still folds whatever is left in the stock/waste into the discard ON the winning move (a won game's discard is all 52 cards, waste bottom-to-top then stock bottom-to-top - the order everything else depends on), so `won` stays game state exactly as before (clock, stat, save, recorded the instant it happens). But nothing flew for that fold, so the leftover cards now fly: the winning removal's `commitRemoval` calls `beginWinSweep`, which claims a flight (`claimFlight`, while the removal's own claim is still held, so `settled` never blips true) and marks every leftover as in the air on the discard (`discardInAir`, so the pile's top is always a card that really landed). Once nothing but the sweep's claim is outstanding (`activeFlightCount` down to 1 - the last removal has landed), `runSweep` deals the leftovers through the existing deal machinery (`useDealAnimator`: same stagger `dealStaggerMs`, spring, throttled sound/haptics and skip-tap - no flight layer of its own), one card per swept card from its own pile (the waste slot for the waste's, the stock slot for the stock's; a pile that cannot be measured deals from the other one, only with no origin at all does a card simply land) to the discard, in the discard array's order, face-up (the deal layer cannot turn a card over in flight, and every swept card is face-up already). The board keeps showing the pre-fold piles (`winHoldView`, kept in `sweepUi.view`, keyed to the won state's identity) and empties each pile when its last card takes off - driven by the deal layer's own take-off signal, `DealAnimator.start`'s additive optional `onCardDealt(cardId)` (once per card, at the instant its ghost is drawn over the spot it leaves; reported for every not-yet-taken-off card when a skip lands the deal at once; silenced by a cancel/restart exactly like `onCardLanded`), `SWEEP_PILE_BLANK_LAG_MS` after the pile's LAST card, on the run's timers so `cancelSweep` clears it. A timer armed from the scheduled delays right before `deal.start` (what this used to be) runs on the wrong clock: the deal's own clock only starts once its ghost pool has rendered and mounted (several frames on a phone) and a card's real take-off is that clock + its re-based delay + the render of its launch, so the timer ran ahead and the pile sat empty until the ghost popped in (worst for a pile whose last card leaves at delay 0). Pinned by `pyramidScreenWin.test.tsx`'s mount-latency cases and `dealLayer.test.tsx`'s `onCardDealt` block. The deal's `onAllLanded` (or a skip, which lands everything at once) settles the claim exactly once; a New Game mid-sweep, or the reveal's fallback timer, ends it via `cancelSweep` (an empty `deal.start([])` is the deal layer's only cancel). Timeline: last removal lands -> sweep (up to ~1.1s for 24 cards) -> 400ms hold -> reveal, so `useWinReveal` gained an additive `maxWaitMs` and Pyramid passes 4s (nominal ~1.9s worst case against the default 2.5s was too thin for a phone's JS stalls). The reveal then launches ONE chain of all 52 discard cards (head = the card on top) from the discard container; the discard blanks only in the commit its ghosts mount (`cascadeHandoff`, now just the discard). **The chain keeps the usual 42ms between cards, so all 52 string out across the bounces behind the leader** (2026-10-17, Jay: "That's what the whole effect is for. So long as they all follow the leader and have enough spacing to not blur into a black streak"). From September it was squeezed into a 13-card chain's half second (`chainSegmentDelayMs`/`CHAIN_MAX_SPAN_MS`, ~9.8ms per card at 52, so it stayed within one bounce), and the stacked shadows of cards that close together read as a black streak down the chain's side; both are gone. `ChainSpec.segmentDelayMs` and `isGhostVisible`'s delay parameter remain, used only by tests. Pinned by `pyramidScreenWin.test.tsx` (a deal harness that follows the real deal's timeline; its 52-card case checks the chain has no `segmentDelayMs`). Don't split a pile into several chains or squeeze it again without asking Jay.

**Golf and TriPeaks sweep their leftover stock onto the waste before the cascade, too** (2026-10-17, Jay: "I'd like the remainder cards to be added to the stack before doing the win animation"). Both win with the tableau cleared, often with cards still in the stock, and the cascade carried only the waste, so those cards just sat in the stock under the celebration. `games/shared/useWinStockSweep.ts` deals them, top card first (as a draw takes them), face up, with the deal layer (`dealStaggerMs`, the deal spring, sound and skip-tap), from the stock slot onto the waste slot once the winning card has landed (`othersSettled`: no flight and no initial deal in the air). Unlike Pyramid, the rules are untouched: the won state keeps its stock, and the hook only changes what the board shows (`stockTopCard`: the real stock until its last card has taken off plus `SWEEP_PILE_BLANK_LAG_MS`; `waste`: the real waste plus each swept card as it lands) and what the cascade carries (`sweptWaste`, one chain at the usual spacing, as in Pyramid). `active` is derived in render from the won state's identity and holds `settled` false from the winning commit, so the reveal never blips; `revealMaxWaitMs` is `SWEEP_WIN_REVEAL_MAX_WAIT_MS` only for a win with stock left. New Game, restore, an undo of the winning move and `runWinCascade` (the reveal's fallback firing first) call `cancel()`, which takes the ghosts out of the air with an empty `deal.start([])`. Both constants moved there from PyramidScreen. Pinned by the `a win with cards still in the stock` describes in `golfScreenWin`/`tripeaksScreenWin` (a deal stand-in that keeps its own schedule). Seen in headless Chrome on the web build (390x844): Golf and TriPeaks deal the stock about 33ms apart, the stock blanks just after its last card leaves, and a 52-card Golf chain strings out behind its leader with no dark streak. Not seen on a device.

**The chains of a multi-pile cascade leave one after another, spread over the first chain's first fall** (2026-10-17, Jay asked for them one after another, the first one's bounce landing as the last one falls). They used to leave a fixed 220ms apart (`CHAIN_ENTRANCE_STAGGER_MS`, removed), which read as one burst; Aces Up's four Aces left together. `WinCascadeProvider.startChains` now times them itself: chain i of n leaves at `chainEntranceDelayMs(i, n, paths[0].vHalfPeriodMs)` = i/(n-1) of the first chain's launch fall (900-1330ms, random per win), so the last chain starts falling just as the first lands on the floor, in the order the screen lists them (the foundations left to right, Spider's completed-run slots, Aces Up's piles). Spider's 8 runs end up ~130-190ms apart (closer than the old 220), 4 foundations ~300-440ms. `ChainSpec.entranceDelayMs` is now an optional override no screen passes (the motion tests use it for exact timings). Pinned by `winCascadeMotion.test.ts`'s `chainEntranceDelayMs` and `winCascadeLayer.test.tsx`'s `chains leave one after another`. Seen in headless Chrome on the web build: Klondike's four Kings left at 1055, 1372, 1689 and 2005ms after the tap, and the first one hit the floor at 1988ms. Not seen on a device.

**Landscape is the OS's since 2026-11-06** (see "The OS turns" at the end): the window itself turns, and every layout below reads the real window (`useWindowDimensions()`) and its real safe-area insets. Until then the OS was portrait-locked and the app drew the turn itself: `@tastic/core`'s `OrientationProvider` read the accelerometer, and every screen drew inside a turned frame (`FakeLandscapeView`, in `withGameProviders` for the games; hud's `RotatedFrame` for the shell's screens and the root toaster). The bullets below that only existed for that drawn turn are cut to a line of history each. What still holds:
- **History: the drawn turn's frame kept ONE identical element tree at every angle.** `FakeLandscapeView` through 0.8.0 returned a different tree at 0deg vs +/-90deg, so every turn remounted the whole game screen (~700-800ms of JS per turn on the simulator). The games' frame is a plain `View` now (`withGameProviders`, 2026-11-06), and an OS turn is just a resize, which remounts nothing.
- **The "wide" layout (`components/gameLayout.ts`) is what makes landscape playable.** Any window wider than tall (`isWide`: a phone held sideways, an iPad in landscape, a desktop browser) drops the vertical chrome bands (portrait reserves 56pt top + 98pt bottom, which would eat 154 of a ~402pt-tall landscape screen): `GameChrome` puts the footer controls (Undo/New Game/Hint/Rules) in a column in the RIGHT side gutter (where the thumb is) and the moves/score/time readout in the LEFT one, hides the title (rules stay reachable from the info button, which only the wide column has), and the corner buttons stay put. Hiding the footer is portrait-only (there the footer floats over the board; in landscape it sits in a gutter and hiding it would give the board nothing back). The two gutters are PER-SIDE (`leftGutter`/`rightGutter`, each = its chrome column + its OWN safe-area inset): sizing both from the larger inset left ~44pt of dead space between the footer buttons and the board on the small-inset side of a phone held sideways. `useGameLayout()` gives every screen its `contentWidth` (net of the gutters) and `availableHeight`. Portrait is byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **The portrait footer is New Game | Undo | Hint, and the moves/score/time readout shows and hides it** (2026-10-14, Jay: the buttons were too small, Undo was furthest from the thumb, and the footer "never seems to be open when I want it"). Each button is an 88x52 target (`FOOTER_BUTTON_WIDTH`/`HEIGHT`, 8pt apart): its 24pt icon over a caption in the scoreboard's label style (NEW GAME, UNDO, HINT; 2026-10-16, Jay: his kids had to ask where to start a new game, and the icon alone is a fan of cards). Captions went on the buttons, not into the guide: the guide shows once, on Home, and kids tap through it, while the caption is there when they look for the button. **Every label is under its thing** (the same day, Jay: MOVES over its number and NEW GAME under its icon read as inconsistent): the scoreboard is number over label too, in both layouts, and sits 10pt higher (`MOVES_TIMER_BOTTOM` 66) so the gap between the two rows is clearly wider than the gap inside each pair and MOVES doesn't read as the New Game icon's name. The board gave up that 10pt (`GAME_CONTENT_BOTTOM_PADDING` 88 -> 98), so a portrait column squeezes and the height-fitted portrait boards (Aces Up, Pyramid's cap) size from 10pt less height than the figures quoted elsewhere in this file. The chevron sits over the numbers in both states and only turns. **A portrait button's touch area reaches 20pt above its pill** (`FOOTER_TOUCH_REACH`: over the gap and the MOVES/SCORE/TIME line, up to the numbers; Jay kept tucking the controls away when he meant Hint). The footer is drawn after the scoreboard, so it wins that line while it is up; tucked away it takes no touches and the whole scoreboard is the toggle again. The reach is part of each button's own box, not a hitSlop, because iOS and Android never hand a child a touch outside its parent's bounds. Checked with `elementFromPoint` on the web build (the TIME label and the strip above Hint press Hint; the numbers and chevron toggle); not on a device. Each button is `GameChrome`'s `FooterButton` (feedback-press's `Pressable`, since a Paper IconButton has no room for a caption), drawing IconButton's pressed overlay on the pill (on Android too: a ripple would fill the whole touch area); a disabled one dims to 38% white (the theme's onSurfaceDisabled is dark in light mode, nearly invisible on the felt). The wide column uses the same buttons, 48pt wide (RULES is ~45pt; NEW GAME wraps onto two lines) and 4pt off the safe edge so the icons stay where the 40pt IconButtons drew them; it and the readout column are centred in the height below the corner buttons, so the taller column never runs under the cog on a 320pt-tall phone held sideways. No `overflow: 'hidden'` on a button: its rounded corners clipped RULES. Seen in headless Chrome at 402x874, 874x402 and 568x320; not on a device. Undo, the most used, sits in the middle (easy for either thumb); New Game, the destructive one, furthest out. Rules has no portrait button: the title opens it (the wide layout has no title, so its column keeps Undo/New Game/Hint/Rules). The felt does nothing on a tap any more: it used to toggle the footer, and a tap that just missed a card hid it unnoticed. The readout (a 240pt-wide `Pressable`, `Hide controls`/`Show controls`) is the toggle, and since it slides into the footer's spot when hidden, the same area brings it back. While hidden the readout is stacked above the footer (`movesTimerAboveFooter`): on the web a child's own pointer-events beat its parent's `pointerEvents='none'`, so the hidden buttons took the lowered readout's taps. Verified on the web build (402x874). **The buttons take no presses for 300ms after the readout brings them back** (`FOOTER_SHOW_GUARD_MS`, Android's double-tap window; `FooterButton`'s `inert`: disabled but not dimmed, so a screen reader hears them as disabled meanwhile). The readout shows them as the finger lifts and has slid off that spot ~35ms later, so the second tap of a double tap on the lowered readout pressed the button that had come up under it (2026-10-17: Undo undid a move). One tap never could: a press log in a Release build on the iOS 27 simulator showed each tap of the simulator tool as one touch, taken by the readout alone, and Undo pressed only by a SECOND tap there (0.45s later). With the guard a second tap 38ms after it pressed nothing, and one later than 300ms still presses the button, on purpose, since it comes up right under the finger. Not checked on a device or the web build. Pinned by `boardScroll.test.tsx`'s GameChrome block. The readout's and the buttons' press areas are widened by their slides (`SCOREBOARD_PRESS_RETENTION`, `FOOTER_PRESS_RETENTION`): see "Animated props skip the shadow tree on iOS" below for why.
- **In the wide layout the column games' cards are sized by the WIDTH, like portrait, and a long column scrolls** (2026-10-14, Jay: "I never use landscape, because the cards are all tiny and separated, I'd rather see them all big, and just have to scroll a bit sometimes"). Klondike, Canfield, Yukon, Spider, Scorpion, FreeCell, Forty Thieves and Aces Up take `cardLayoutForRow`'s width-derived size and gap in both layouts (capped at `CARD_WIDTH_MAX`, 96pt). On a phone held sideways (874x402) that is Klondike/Canfield/Yukon/Aces Up 96, FreeCell 84.6, Scorpion 75, Spider and Forty Thieves 67.3, against 40-57pt when the cards were shrunk to fit the ~365pt of height (and the leftover width was spread into wide gaps). The tableau box is still the height left on screen (`useTableauMaxHeight`), so a column first compresses to `MIN_FAN_SCALE` and then scrolls (see the board-fit bullet below); Yukon's and FreeCell's tallest starting columns already scroll at the deal on a phone held sideways. Those games' `boardModel.ts` files (and their tests) were deleted with the fit. **Still fitted to the height:** Golf (`useFittedCardWidth` + its `boardModel.ts`), Pyramid and TriPeaks (`geometry.ts`): their boards never grow, so a fit costs nothing later and width-sized cards would scroll every game. Aces Up keeps its PORTRAIT fit (`boardModel.ts` with `longColumn` 9, below). `columnBoardModel`/`fitCardWidthToHeight`/`useFittedGap` and the `longColumn` floor (`LONG_COLUMN_MIN_CARD_WIDTH`) stay for those. Scorpion's wide layout has no top row: the assisted stock packet is absolutely positioned one gap past column 7 (tableau centred on its own, so the width budget is 7+2 columns; `useTableauMaxHeight(..., { topRowCards: 0 })`), so there is no tableau jump when the stock is dealt/undone. The simulated column lengths behind the old `longColumn` sizing (90th percentile of each game's tallest column: Klondike 12 face-up, Canfield 11, FreeCell 13, Forty Thieves 10, Aces Up 9, Yukon 28, Scorpion 30, Spider 22) also showed the Hint ping-pong fixed on 2026-10-14 (see Hints below). Not checked on a device or the simulator in landscape.
- **Every board fits the screen; it scrolls ONLY when a column too long to squeeze any further runs past the bottom** (2026-10-13, Jay: "a sane minimum and scrolling a bit to the bottom card"). `useTableauMaxHeight` gives each column exactly the height left on screen, in both layouts (the per-game 300-460pt portrait floors are gone: they made every column box taller than an iPhone SE or a 640pt Android, so the board scrolled there from the first deal, with every column short). `PileView` lays each column out with `columnTops` (`components/columnTops.ts`, which FreeCell's analytic deal calls too), compressing a long column into that, face-down steps first, down to `MIN_FACE_DOWN_FAN_SCALE` (0.55), then face-up ones, never below `MIN_FACE_UP_FAN_SCALE` (0.95 of the natural steps; it was one 0.8 floor for both until 2026-10-22, see that section): past that the column runs taller than its `maxHeight` (the box grows to `totalHeight`) and the board scrolls to its last card. 0.8 is from Simulator screenshots of a 40-card Yukon column: the next card's edge and shadow take the bottom of the strip, so at 0.7-0.75 a covered Q read as an O and a J as an I. A Pro-size iPhone fits ~26 Yukon cards in a column before it scrolls. The wide layout sizes the column games' cards by the width (see the wide-layout bullet above), so there a long column scrolls sooner. Aces Up fits its cards to the height in PORTRAIT (`AcesUpScreen`, `longColumn` 9): its width-sized 4-pile cards (88pt on a 640pt Android) ran a pile past the bottom by its sixth card in most games; now 67-71pt there, 81pt on an iPhone SE, ~93pt on a tall phone (96pt until 2026-10-22's readable-rank fan floor; its 12pt `PILE_MIN_GAP` also shrinks a 390pt phone's cards, see that section). The fan's extent is the top of its LAST card plus one card (it used to add the top card's own step, so columns compressed a step early and left a gap). FreeCell's deal lays its ghosts out with `columnTops` itself (its hand-copied fan formula drifted once the floors were gone and would have landed dealt cards 12-27pt off in short portrait windows). `components/boardScroll.ts` (`useBoardScroll(movesCount)`, spread onto `GameChrome`'s ScrollView): scroll-enabled only while the content is more than 1pt taller than the viewport, `alwaysBounceVertical={false}` (React Native's default rubber-banded the whole board under every swipe on the felt, even when it fit), and a HOLD (`holdHeight`, the content's `minHeight`): from the moment the board starts moving (`onScrollBeginDrag`/`onMomentumScrollBegin`, or the first scroll event on the web) it keeps all the content it had, and once it rests (120ms after the last scroll event, never while a finger is down) offset + viewport, the offset clamped to the scroll range (iOS reports a rubber-band past the end as an offset past it, and a hold sized from that grew the content). So a long column losing cards (a move, an auto-play stream) never jumps the board under a card in flight or leaves it stuck past its end (iOS doesn't pull the offset back in: RN's TODO T190695447 in `RCTScrollViewComponentView.mm`; Android/web clamp at once, which jumps). The player scrolls back up; at rest at the top the hold lets go. A new deal while held (the move count drops back: New Game, the win/loss banners) REMOUNTS the ScrollView (`boardKey`), so the new board is at the top in the same commit that mounts it, before the deal measures its destinations (a `scrollTo` reaches the shadow tree a commit or more later, so the deal would aim at the old offset). A viewport height change while scrolled (the phone turned) clears the hold and scrolls to the top. Pyramid's portrait size is capped by `PYRAMID_BOARD_MODEL` too (its budget counted 36pt of margins against 40, so near-square portrait windows ran up to 4pt past the screen). Spider's portrait tableau row padded 24 against the budgeted 20, so its board was 4pt too tall on every phone; it pads 20 now. Verified on the iOS Simulator (iPhone 17, Metro-served dev build): a fitting board doesn't move under a felt drag; a 40-card column scrolls; a vertical drag starting on a card lifts the card rather than scrolling; moving a 25-card run off the long column while scrolled leaves the board in place; Not checked: Android, landscape, a real device. Pinned by `columnTops.test.ts`, `boardScroll.test.tsx` (each scroll case fails with its fix removed), `pileViewHold.test.tsx` (the box growing past `maxHeight`), `useTableauMaxHeight.test.tsx` and `pyramid/geometry.test.ts`.
- **A column that squeezes or spreads glides there, and a moving run keeps the spacing of the columns it leaves and lands on** (2026-10-13, Jay: "It's a little jarring when moving a card or set and the column immediately expand or contract"). Every tableau card's `Animated.View` carries `layout={FAN_GLIDE}` (`PileView.tsx`: Reanimated's `LinearTransition`, `FAN_GLIDE_MS` = `scaledMs(120)` = 192ms, ease-out): the LAYOUT moves at once, so CardRegistry, the drop zones and every flight still measure the card's final spot, and only the drawn view glides there natively. A card that mounts (a landing swaps a `FlyingCardSlot` for it) or is held where it left doesn't glide. The ghost of a multi-card run used to fan at the natural step whatever the columns did, so a run lifted off a compressed column stretched the instant it took off and snapped shorter as it landed on one. Now each laid-out tableau card (and each `FlyingCardSlot`) records its spot in the fan in `components/dragdrop/fanTops.ts` (module-level, owner-guarded like CardRegistry; a held card records nothing), and `DragGhostLayer` reads it: a run's ghost starts at the run's spacing in the column it leaves (`takeOffStep`, for `show()` and a drag's `showLive()`) and springs to the spacing of the column it lands on with the same spring as its flight (`flyTo`'s `landingStep`; each fanned card is a `FannedGhostCard` translated by `index * step` on the UI thread). A single card, an unfanned stack (`offsetStep: 0`, a recycle) and a run not laid out in a column keep the `offsetStep` they were given. Every multi-card ghost in the app is a tableau run, so no screen passes anything new. Verified on the iOS Simulator (iPhone 18 Pro Max, Metro-served dev build, a seeded 25-card Yukon column, frame-measured): the column spreads 66 -> 78 -> 81 -> 82px and squeezes back over ~190ms; a tapped 11-card run takes off at the squeezed 65.5px and eases to 82px over its flight, the undo eases back and lands matching the squeezed column; a dragged run keeps 65.5px under the finger and eases to 82px on the drop. Known edge: a card tapped or picked up mid-glide starts its ghost at its final layout spot, a few points from where it is drawn. Not checked: landscape (turning the phone may glide card positions too), Android, a real device. Pinned by `dragGhostLayer.test.tsx`'s `a run is fanned like the columns it leaves and lands on` (each half fails with its line removed) and `fanTops.test.ts`.
- **The game's dialogs render in the game screen's own Paper `Portal.Host`** (`withGameProviders`, OUTSIDE the deal, drag, cascade and hint providers), so they paint above every ghost overlay and DealLayer's skip-tap layer. History: until 2026-11-06 the host also had to sit inside the drawn turn's frame (Paper's default host is at the app root, where a dialog laid out against the portrait window ran off a sideways screen and the Settings sheet lost its first row), and every dialog was told `rotation={0}`. hud's dialogs default to 0, and nothing here passes a `rotation` now. The update dialog is the shell's (its RootLayout's `UpdateDialog`, in the app-root host).
- **The board's column is `GameContentGutter`** (`components/GameContentGutter.tsx`; `RotatedContentGutter`, which sized from the drawn turn's width, until 2026-11-06). hud's `ContentGutter` is NOT a drop-in replacement: it caps against the whole window and splits the leftover equally, and its `leftGutter`/`rightGutter` props are decorations drawn inside that leftover, not reserved widths - so the wide layout's per-side chrome columns would overlap the board. Keep `GameContentGutter` unless hud grows per-side reserved widths.
- **Every ghost overlay uses measured window points as they are** (`measureInWindow`, Reanimated `measure()`, `e.absoluteX/Y`). `DragGhostLayer`, `DealLayer`, `HintGlideLayer`, `WinCascadeLayer`, Spider's `SequenceFlightGhosts` and Pyramid's `PairFlightGhosts` each draw in an absoluteFill View at the game screen's top level, and the game screen starts at the window's origin (the stack draws no header: `useThemedRootBackground`'s `headerShown: false`; a header on the web build once put every dealt card that far below its spot). A new overlay must sit at the screen's top level too, or measure its own origin and subtract it. History: until 2026-11-06 every point went through the drawn turn's frame (`overlayFrame.ts`' `useOverlayFrame`/`toLocal`, `localOffsetToScreenPoint`, `rectOffsetToScreenPoint`, `wasteFanScreenShift`, all deleted with their tests); Pyramid's and TriPeaks' analytic cell points (`pyramidCellScreenPoint`, `peaksCardScreenPoint`), FreeCell's dealt card (`rect.y + top`) and the draw-3 fan (the top card's x less the fan step) are plain sums again. A dragged card trails the pointer by the pan's activation distance (PileView's `minDistance(8)`: the drag counts from where the pan started), along the drag. Seen on the web build at 402x874 and 874x402 (2026-11-06, headless Chrome over CDP, every animation frame sampled): every ghost overlay at (0, 0, window); deal ghosts in Klondike, FreeCell, Pyramid, TriPeaks, Canfield and Spider on an exact line to their card spots (never more than 0.03px to the side); a Klondike tap move taking off from its card and landing within 0.2px, the same move dragged landing on the same spot, and the win cascade taking over the four Kings exactly where they stood and bouncing inside the window. Not checked on a device.
- **The win cascade follows a resize while it bounces** (the phone turned, a browser window resized; 2026-10-15, Jay: "the win animation doesn't update bounds on orientation change"). Each chain's path is still built once, at the win, in that window's bounce box (`cascadeBounds`: the walls, the apex at the header, the floor), but `CascadeChains` keeps the box as it is NOW in a shared value (`bounds`, updated from a layout effect on the window size and header) and every ghost's frame callback moves its position into it (`fitXToBounds`/`fitYToBounds`: the path's walls onto the new walls, its apex and floor onto the new ones, the rest in proportion). The motion is linear interpolation between the path's own points, so that is exactly the bounce a chain built in the new window would make: same timing and phase, nothing restarts, and turning back gives the untouched cascade to the bit. Before, the paths kept the portrait box after a turn: on a 402x874 phone held sideways the cards fell to y 850 of a 402pt-tall window and never got past x 390 of 874. Ghosts still parked at the turn (the first second or so) go to the same proportional spot, not to where their pile now stands, and keep their card size. Verified on the web build by resizing 402x874 -> 874x402 mid-cascade (cards spanned x 12-389/y 56-845, then x 13-862/y 56-378); an OS turn on a device was not checked. Pinned by `winCascadeLayer.test.tsx`'s `the phone turned while the cascade runs` block and `winCascadeMotion.test.ts`.
- **Home is `@tastic/shell`'s `HomeScreen` since 2026-11-02 (`app/index.tsx`), and it follows the window, with a two-pane landscape layout.** It was built from Solitaire's own (see the shell's `screens/HomeScreen.tsx` and `homeLayout.ts`): the cold-launch resume `<Redirect>` before the menu mounts, `isWide` (window wider than tall) putting the hero and then the player button in a left pane and the game tiles in a right-pane grid of vertical tiles (2-3 columns) with a 56pt gutter for the cog, each side hugging its own safe-area inset, the layout held while Home is covered (`useFrozenWhileHidden`, so turning the phone in a game doesn't rebuild the invisible menu), every preview held back a frame, and the player menu closed on every layout change. Solitaire's part is the pack's: `HeroTitle` (`components/HeroTitle.tsx`: `HeroCards`, the title and the subtitle from `wordmark`, the fan scaled into the wide pane's `fit` by `heroScaleFor`, the same sizes Solitaire's own Home used, and paused on Home's `active`), each game's `Preview` and `Rules` (`games/shared/gameDefinition.tsx`), and `usePickerAlign` (`useFrameAutoAlign`). The tiles' names and blurbs are Paper's `titleLarge`/`bodyMedium` (`titleMedium`/`bodySmall` wide), a size up from Solitaire's own 20/13, and the tiles are buttons, not `<Link>`s. A phone held sideways has only left/right insets (62 on the island side, 34 on the other, on the simulator) - nothing leans on the top/bottom ones for breathing room.
- **History: hud's `RotatedFrame`** (0.14.0+, lifted from this app's `components/RotatedScreen.tsx`) was the turned frame every shell screen and the root toaster drew in. Since core 0.15 it turns nothing, and hud 0.34 renamed it `ScreenFrame` without `rotation` or `rotatedSafeArea`; the shell (0.11+) lays its Stats, Profiles and toaster out in that (`portalHost`, `passThrough`, and `maxContentWidth` 640 in a wide window), and tests it there. Solitaire uses it nowhere (its `rotatedScreen.test.tsx` went 2026-11-06). The root toaster's swipe-to-dismiss works in any frame (`@rific/toaster` 0.7.1+ measures it in the toast's own coordinates).
- **The Home corner buttons (trophy/cog) live in ONE plain zIndex:10 box-none View, never carry a zIndex themselves.** A Paper `IconButton` is a `Surface`, and on iOS `Surface` (`SurfaceIOS`) moves only `position`/`top`/`left`/`width`/`flex`/... onto its OUTERMOST view and leaves `zIndex` on an inner layer - so an `IconButton` with `style={{ zIndex: 10 }}` is still stacked at 0 on native, below any later full-screen sibling (Home's `ScrollView`), and got no touches (it looked right because the scroller is transparent there; web/Android keep the whole style on one view, which is why it only ever failed on iOS). `GameChrome`'s `CornerActionButtons` (Home/Settings) sidestep it differently: they render through a Paper `Portal` into `withGameProviders`' in-frame `Portal.Host`, so they paint above everything a screen draws over its board (the win/loss dialog's backdrop, the win cascade, fireworks, ghosts) and stay crisp and tappable while a win/loss dialog is up. That is why the win dialog has no Home button of its own (2026-10-12, Jay: the loss dialog has no room for a third button). They are rendered before `GameChrome`'s Settings/Rules dialogs, so those portals (mounted later) still cover them.
