The win/loss dialogs (`WinBanner`/`LossBanner`) are `@tastic/hud`'s overlay card (`OverlayBackdrop`/`OverlayCard`/`DialogHeader`/`overlayActionStyles`, ConfirmDialog's look; see Dialogs below) and their backdrop takes every touch. The card is the theme's surface and blurs while the player's Blur setting is on (the shell's `Theme` passes `expoBlur`, added here 2026-10-15; it only takes effect in a new native build). Any absolute Paper button that must out-stack a later sibling needs a wrapper View carrying the zIndex. Pinned by `paperIconButtonZIndex.test.tsx` (documents the Surface behaviour); Home's corner buttons are the shell's since 2026-11-02 (later siblings of its scroller, so they need no zIndex), tested in the package.
- **Home's press-away overlay must be a SIBLING of the popover's owner, not of the scroller.** `PressAwayOverlay` (zIndex 5) used to be a screen-level sibling above the whole `ScrollView`, while the profile popover lives inside the scroll content: on native zIndex only orders siblings of ONE parent, so the overlay covered the popover's rows and tapping "Manage"/a profile only closed it (portrait, pre-existing). In portrait it is now the first child of the scroll content, next to `profilePanelWrap` (zIndex 10); in the wide layout the left pane is itself a screen-level zIndex:10 sibling above it.
- **Popover placement is `useFrameAutoAlign`** (`components/useFrameAutoAlign.ts`), the pack's `usePickerAlign`: Home's player menu, the setup and stats screens' pickers, the Profiles color swatch (the shell's Profiles passes `{ maxContentWidth: 640 }`, its roster column) and the pills for seats 3 and 4 on Four Crowns' and Crazy Eights' setup screens (`RulePickers`). It makes hud's `useAutoAlign` decision (centred if it fits, else flush to the edge with room; below unless there is genuinely more room above), but against the window LESS its safe-area insets (`computeFramePlacement`, so a menu keeps off the island side of a phone held sideways, which `useAutoAlign` doesn't know about) and, in a wide window, the capped column (`columnInsets`, the desktop Profiles roster, where a scroll view clips the popover). The shell's README says a pack may no longer need one since core 0.15; Solitaire keeps it for those two. History: until 2026-11-06 it also converted a trigger measured in the drawn turn's frame (`frameRectFromScreen`, deleted). Unit-tested in `useFrameAutoAlign.test.ts`. Still unverified: the software keyboard while naming a profile in landscape (`ProfilesManager`'s `KeyboardAvoidingView`); the drawn turn's `portraitWhileKeyboard` held the frame upright while typing, and is gone.
- **Settings live in Redux** (2026-10-07), like the fleet's other games: `src/redux/settingsSlice.ts` (`DEFAULT_SETTINGS` plus `@rific/core`'s `createSettingsSlice('settings', { initializeMode: 'merge' })`), read and written only through `useSettings()` (`src/hooks/useSettings.ts`, `{ settings, setSettings(patch) }`), with the `Settings` type in `src/types/index.ts`. `useSettings.ts` and everything in `settingsSlice.ts` except `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` are byte-identical to AirHockey/BoxHockey/LightCycles/Pong/Snake (`settingsSlice.test.ts` was too, until Lock Orientation moved to the shell's `appSettings` on 2026-10-29). No validation, no migration: the old `solitaire.settings.v1` AsyncStorage blob was deliberately abandoned (Jay), and the factory's REHYDRATE fills any newly added field from the defaults. This replaced `stores/settings.tsx` (`SettingsProvider`/`updateSettings`), its pre-mount loader in `_layout.tsx` and the `'appSettings'` splash gate: `PersistGate` already holds every consumer until the store has rehydrated. Tests mock the hook (`jest.mock('@/hooks/useSettings', ...)` spreading `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` from `jest.requireActual('@/redux/settingsSlice')`). Everything else this app persists moved into Redux the same day (Jay: everything in Redux, old saved data deliberately not imported, players start fresh):
- **Theme:** the store's `theme` slice (`@rific/auto-paper`'s `createThemeReducer`, seeded with `DEFAULT_SEED_COLOR`, now in `src/constants/theme.ts`), bridged into auto-paper's Provider by `@tastic/shell`'s `Theme` (Solitaire's own `components/ThemeBridge.tsx` until 2026-11-02), which also opens the 'theme' splash gate. It sits first inside the shell's `PersistGate`, so nothing loads before mount: fonts are the only thing the root waits for (the shell's `Fonts`, with the pack's `fonts`).
- **Last active game:** the shell's `lastActiveGame` key since 2026-11-02 (every game is `resumable`, so `createPackStore` mounts it; the same key, shape and action types Solitaire's own `redux/lastActiveGameSlice.ts` had, so a saved resume carried over). The shell's play route (`PlayRoute`) sets it as a play screen mounts and clears it as it unmounts while the app is active (the web pop unmounts it too, seen 2026-11-02), and its `LastActiveGameProvider` hands the shell's Home a one-shot read. **Swiping the app away lost the resume, in 1.2.8 too** (Jay's phone, 2026-11-03): iOS terminating an app it hasn't suspended yet (swiped away from the app switcher soon after leaving it) makes React Native stop every surface (`RCTSurfacePresenter`'s will-terminate handler), which unmounts the whole tree, and the play screen's cleanup (1.2.8's game screens did the same) took that for the player backing out. A `simctl terminate` or a web reload unmounts nothing, which is why no check before had seen it. The shell now clears only while `AppState.currentState` is `active` (the app switcher sends `inactive` the moment it opens); fixed in `@tastic/shell` 0.2.1 (installed here 2026-11-04, the iOS fingerprint still `65ef1391...`, so it needs no new native build). Seen on the iOS 27 simulator: a 1.2.4 build relaunched on Home after a swipe-away; today's bundle with the fix, swapped into that build, went back to its Klondike deal, and tapping Home in the game still cleared it. `useStuckLossCheck`'s unmount (records the loss, clears the save) would run on that same teardown once `DEAD_BOARD_LOSS` is on again: give it the same `active` check then. The game screens and `GameChrome` no longer set or clear it.
- **Saved games in progress:** `redux/gameInProgressSlice.ts`, one slot per game, each a nested `persistReducer` with its OWN key (`solitaire:gameInProgress.<id>`; `persist:` before 2026-10-17), blacklisted from the root config and skipped by the dev serializability check. A save can be several MB, and Android AsyncStorage can't read back one entry past ~2MB, so never combine them into one key. `stores/gameInProgress.ts` keeps its API and debounce (500ms, max 3s) in front of the dispatch; `flushGameInProgress` also awaits `persistor.flush()`. `preloadGameInProgress` was deleted (saves are already in memory). Screen tests swap `@/redux/store` for `src/testUtils/gameInProgressStore.ts` (a store with just this slice) and seed through `seedGameInProgress`. **Saves are per profile** (2026-10-15, Jay: his daughter's new profile resumed his deals): each game's slot is keyed by owner (seat 1 of the shell's `profileSelection`, or `GUEST_PLAYER_ID` `'guest'`, in `constants/player.ts`), owners as the slot's top-level keys so redux-persist re-serializes only the mover's board. Every read, save and clear in `stores/gameInProgress.ts` is the active player's; a coalesced save keeps the owner it was handed in with. A deleted profile's saves go with it (`onProfileRemoved`, `redux/packReducers.ts`, through each slot's reducer so its own entry is rewritten), and so does a player's reset (`stores/playerData.ts`, see Settings follow the player); one deleted in a sibling app is left behind. Reset Stats on Everyone drops everyone's (`clearAllGamesInProgress`). All of a game's owners still share that game's one storage entry, so two long games of the same game sum toward Android's per-entry limit. Saves from before (a slot's `saved` key) go to whoever is active the first time any save is touched after the update (`adoptLegacySaves`). The test store has no profileSelection slice, so tests run as guest. Pinned by `stores/gameInProgress.test.ts`'s per-profile and legacy cases. Not checked on a device.
- **Stats and achievements:** `redux/achievementsSlice.ts` (`@tastic/achievements`' `createAchievementsSlice`, needs 0.3.0+), read and written by `stores/gameStats.tsx` via `useAchievements` with `select`/`actions`. The old per-game stats import (`stores/statsLegacy.ts`) and the achievements screen's loading branch are gone; `migrateStats` moved to `stores/statsValidation.ts`.
- **History: Lock Orientation** (gone 2026-11-06, see "The OS turns"; the OS's own rotation lock replaces it) was the shell's `appSettings.lockOrientation` (2026-10-29; `Settings.lockOrientation` before), `@tastic/core`'s `OrientationProvider freezeWhileLocked`, with the locked angle round-tripping through `appSettings.lockedRotation` so a relaunch reopened in it.
- **The status bar is set by the root Stack's screen options, never a `StatusBar` component** (2026-10-15): the pack's `useScreenOptions` (`components/packRoot.tsx`) returns `statusBarStyle: 'light'` (the felt is dark in every appearance mode), which `@tastic/shell`'s RootLayout merges into the stack's `screenOptions` after its own; Home stays mounted under a game, and the top screen's options win. Until 2026-11-06 it was `@tastic/core`'s `useRotationAwareStatusBar`, which also hid the bar while the drawn turn was sideways (it's glued to the device's physical top edge and couldn't turn with it). It used to be `RotationAwareStatusBar` (expo-status-bar) in `withGameProviders` and a focus-gated one in Solitaire's old `RotatedScreen`, plus a light `<StatusBar>` beside the Stack: all of that stopped working in an Xcode 27 build (Jay's phone, iOS 27), because an app linked against the iOS 27 SDK can't set the status bar through `UIApplication` (UIKit's header: setting it "does nothing if ... linked against SDK versions 27.0 or later"), which is the only thing React Native's `StatusBar` does on iOS. react-native-screens sets it per view controller instead, which needs `UIViewControllerBasedStatusBarAppearance` on: `app.json`'s `ios.infoPlist` sets it (`@tastic/core`'s config plugin did, 0.14.0 to 0.14.x). With it on, any React Native `StatusBar` logs an error (a red box in a dev build), so nothing here mounts one: the shell's `Theme` passes auto-paper's `Provider` `statusBar={false}` (auto-paper 0.13.2+; it and core 0.14.0 were unpublished and yalc-linked here as of 2026-10-15), and `statusBar.test.tsx` fails if any source file imports `expo-status-bar` or React Native's `StatusBar` again. Permanent, not a stopgap for SDK 57: it doesn't depend on UIScene or `withSceneDelegate`, and cloud builds need it as soon as they link against the iOS 27 SDK. Verified on the iOS 27 simulator with Release builds linked against the iOS 27 SDK (Xcode 27), 2026-10-15, under the drawn turn: no red box with the Info.plist key on. **Not in a dev-client (`development`) build:** expo-dev-launcher puts its own `DevLauncherViewController` between the window's root view controller and react-native-screens' stack (its own comment in `ExpoDevLauncherReactDelegateHandler.swift` says this "blocks react-native-screens' single-level VC traversal for orientation and other window traits"), so even `statusBarHidden: true` forced on every screen leaves the bar showing there. Release builds (preview, production, `--local` included) don't have that controller. Not yet checked: a real device, Android.
- **The felt is ONE `TableBackground`, mounted at the root behind the whole `Stack` (the pack's `Backdrop`, which `@tastic/shell`'s RootLayout draws); no screen paints its own.** Every top-level screen (each game via `GameChrome`, Home, Achievements, Profiles, `GameLoading`) is transparent and draws over it - it used to be a per-screen first child, which left every screen that hadn't rendered its content yet (a game waiting on its saved-board read) showing the Stack's black instead of felt. It only shows if TWO things are transparent, and only one of them is obvious: the screens' `contentStyle` (the shell's stack sets it over a backdrop, overriding the opaque black `useThemedRootBackground` returns) AND the React Navigation theme's `colors.background` (made transparent by the shell, wrapped around the `Stack` with expo-router's `ThemeProvider`). React Navigation paints `colors.background` on the layers AROUND each screen - the `ScreenStack`'s native container on iOS/Android, each screen's wrapper on web - and `contentStyle` only reaches the layer inside them, so `contentStyle: transparent` alone left an opaque light-grey (`rgb(242,242,242)`) slab over the felt on web (and would have hidden it behind the native container). The root window colour `useThemedRootBackground` also syncs stays black underneath, untouched. **The felt follows the profile color** (2026-10-13, Jay: "I didn't want the felt to be strictly green ... I like the blue and red felt"): `derivePlayerTableColors` makes it the card back's opposite hue, darkened (`getTriadicPalette(cardBack, 'complementary').tertiary` at `getTonalColor` 0.24 / 0.16 dark, the vignette `tableFeltDark` at 0.15 / 0.08), so the default Rose gets a green felt, Orange a navy one, Teal a red one. From 2026-09-20 until then it was one fixed green, pinned to the native splash's `backgroundColor`; that was undone, and from 2026-10-13 the splash was the fleet's plain `#FAFAFA` / `#050505`. Since 2026-11-05 it is the DEFAULT player's felt, Rose's `#1E5C47` / `#123F30` (`derivePlayerTableColors` for `DEFAULT_SEED_COLOR`, light/dark), so a fresh install fades into its own Home with no change of color; any other player's launch fades from that green into their own dark felt (see "Icons and splash" below; a splash change needs a new native build). If the felt tones or the default color ever change, recompute these two by hand: nothing pins them. `palettes.ts`' `tableFelt` values are never rendered. **The muting keeps the profile's hue:** `previewCardBack` blends the profile color 55% toward a neutral grey anchor (`palettes.ts`' `cardBack` `#565656` / `#404040`, `cardBackPattern` `#737373` / `#515151`, each the HSL lightness of the navy it replaced); the old navy anchors dragged every hue toward blue (red came out plum), which also skewed the felt and the Paper theme. **The Paper theme uses `triadic` harmony** (`THEME_HARMONY` in `constants/theme.ts`, set in the store's initial state AND by `ProfileThemeSync`, because a saved theme keeps the harmony it was saved with): auto-paper's default split-complementary puts secondary and tertiary 150 and 210 degrees from the seed, only 60 apart at the same saturation and lightness, so they read as one color (the default's were two greens). Verified: web (all four top-level screens) and the iOS 27 simulator (video frames through a real Home -> Klondike push and a Home tap back: the sliding panel covers the previous screen cleanly, no bleed-through, no black flash, one continuous felt). **Android is handled by source-reading only, NOT observed** (no emulator was available): its default push/pop tween holds the outgoing screen at full alpha for ~450ms (`rns_default_enter_out`, API 33+), which would show Home straight through the transparent game screen, so the shell's stack sets `animation: 'fade'` for Android only over a backdrop (the pack's `useScreenOptions` leaves `animation` out unless Reduce Motion is on, or it would undo that) (`rns_fade_*`, a 150ms symmetric crossfade) and leaves iOS on its UIKit push. If Android still ghosts a screen during a push/pop, or the fade feels wrong there, that is where to look. Its `Svg` is keyed on the window size, so turning the phone redraws the vignette for the new shape: `react-native-svg` doesn't re-resolve its percentage-based gradient `Rect` after a resize.
- **Every game screen's loading state is the one shared `GameLoading`** (`components/GameLoading.tsx`; `if (!state) return <GameLoading />`, Spider's is `!ready || !state`) - four suit glyphs pulsing in a wave, dead-centered, all opacity on the UI thread. It replaced eleven hand-copied "Loading..." branches (nine top-aligned, Spider centered, Pyramid on a different text style) and it draws no background of its own (the root felt shows through). The indicator (`LoadingIndicator`, also used by Achievements while stats load) stays invisible for the first 750ms and then fades in (`REVEAL_DELAY_MS`). The read it covers is one AsyncStorage round trip (a saved-board read that `preloadGameInProgress` already starts on finger-down), a few dozen ms, but a game's loading screen stays up until the board has been built too (~400-500ms of JS); at the old 250ms an ordinary entry flashed a few half-faded suits that vanished before one pulse (840ms) played. Not added: a minimum display time once shown - holding `GameLoading` up after `state` is set would unmount the board while each screen's `[state]`-effect runs its deal measurement (see the deal-race paragraph above), so it would have to gate the `setState` in every screen's load path instead. Pinned by `src/__tests__/components/gameLoading.test.tsx`.

**Going Home / entering a game is fast because of four invariants - don't undo them.** It used to take seconds (5s then 7.6s on the 1st and 2nd Home tap in a dev bundle, and growing by ~2.5s every round trip; 2.7s then 4.1s even with a production JS bundle), and this file used to call that "pre-existing, wait it out". It was not: it was algorithmic (the production-JS figures are roughly half the dev ones, so most of it is real work rather than dev-mode overhead; the native side was still a Debug build, so a Release build on a phone remains the true check). Measured with the Simulator (production JS = `expo start --dev-client --no-dev --minify`, which isolates React dev-mode overhead from the real cost) after these fixes: Home tap ~20ms with no JS stall (was 2.7-4.1s), Home's first mount ~140ms (was 1.3-1.5s), game entry 0.2-0.5s of JS.
- **Home is `router.dismissTo('/')`, never `router.replace('/')`** (`GameChrome.tsx`). `replace` mounted a brand-new Home on top of the one already beneath the game, so every round trip leaked one more live Home (each a full menu); `dismissTo` pops back to the existing Home and only falls back to replace semantics when nothing is beneath (a deep link or the cold-launch resume). `back()` errors in that no-history case and `dismissAll()` returns nothing for a single-route stack, which is why `replace` had been picked in the first place.
- **`lastActiveGame` is not React state** (the shell's `hooks/useLastActiveGame.tsx`, ported from Solitaire's `stores/lastActiveGame.tsx`). Only Home ever reads it, as a one-shot cold-launch resume; exposing it as state made every set/clear (each game mount, each Home tap) re-render every consumer and made each Home in the stack swap its whole menu for a `<Redirect>` and rebuild it, with the one-frame preview deferral already defeated (its `previewsRevealed` state survives the swap). The provider value is stable for its lifetime and Home reads `consumeResumeGame()` once at mount. Any set/clear also drops a pending resume, so a deep link straight into a game can't later bounce back into a stale one. Tested in the package.
- **Home's tiles are one pre-rendered face layer plus one small `Svg` of backs each, not real cards** (`components/GamePreview.tsx`). The menu used to mount ~374 full `@tastic/cards` CardFace/CardBack trees drawn ~7x10pt - ~12k native views (every react-native-svg element is a real, never-flattened native view; court cards alone are ~200KB of path text each). Now each tile's face-up cards are ONE transparent `Image` (`previewFaceBitmaps.ts` + `assets/previewFaces/`, 11 games x 4 card styles x 2 inks, 1.3MB, rendered by the real CardFace by the same `scripts/generate-card-face-bitmaps.mjs` as the card faces - regenerate when a `previewLayout.ts` changes too), and the face-down cards stay live SVG under it, a solid back in the profile's `cardBack` colour with a face-coloured frame (a tint can't recolour a baked back without flattening its frame). Column layouts merge every back into one `Path` of *visible strips* (`components/previewGeometry.ts`, pure and unit-tested); Pyramid/TriPeaks draw a `Rect` per back. That only works while no layout paints a back ABOVE a face - the generator throws if one ever does. The layout helpers live in `previewCanvas.ts` (dependency-free so the generator can bundle them; `GamePreview` re-exports them). **Each tile zooms onto its own deal** (2026-10-20, Jay: the previews didn't match the games, lots of space around the cards): a layout places its cards at one card size with the real boards' tight gap (`PREVIEW_GAP`, 0.04 of a card, CARD_GAP_MIN beside a phone card), and `previewFrame` (its cards plus `PREVIEW_MARGIN`, grown to the tile's aspect ratio) is both the Svg's viewBox and the region the generator draws the face layer through. They used to spread every game's columns over the whole 360-unit canvas at one fixed card size, so Klondike's gaps were nearly as wide as its cards. Pinned by `previewGeometry.test.ts`'s `previewFrame` block. The generator needed three bundling fixes for SDK 58 (a stub for React Native's removed asset registry, `expo-modules-core` and react-native-svg's touch-sound module external); card faces regenerated byte-identical. `src/__tests__/components/gamePreview.test.tsx` fails if the per-card art comes back.
- **Nothing ticks state at `GameChrome` level.** The once-a-second clock lives in `ElapsedClock` (`GameChrome.tsx`), so only the moves/score/time readout re-renders per second; the whole chrome used to, at ~140ms of JS per tick (~14% of the thread while dragging cards). Likewise `HeroCards` cancels its forever-running wave while Home is blurred (`useIsFocused`), since Home now stays mounted under every game.
- **The hero fan's idle wave is ONE clock** (`components/heroFan.ts`: `heroBumpAt` maps one shared `withRepeat` clock to each card's bump; `HeroCards` owns the clock and restarts it whenever its `active` prop comes back on: the shell's Home's, focused with the app in the foreground). It used to be five per-card forever-repeating `withSequence`s whose periods only matched on paper: every step ends on the first frame past its duration, so each card's real period was a different whole number of frames and the left-to-right ripple scrambled within a minute. Never give the cards separate looping animations again. The fan is a true arc (every card turns about one pivot below it, `HERO_FAN_STEP_DEG`/`HERO_FAN_PIVOT_PX`), a card's bump moves it along its own axis, and `HeroTitle`'s `heroScaleFor` sizes it into the landscape left pane from the resting fan's own box (`HERO_FAN_SPAN`/`HERO_FAN_HEIGHT`), not copied numbers. Pinned by `heroFan.test.ts`, `heroCards.test.tsx` and `heroTitle.test.ts`. Also: Reanimated's `withTiming` continues the timeline of a preceding `withTiming` to the same value (`timing.ts`'s `onStart`), so a hold step after a same-target step is silently shortened - that is why the old wave's 500ms rest was really 240ms, the value kept.

Also fixed on the same pass: the deal's full-screen skip-tap layer (`DealLayer.tsx`) stops short of the top chrome band (Home/Settings/title), so tapping Home mid-deal leaves at once instead of first just skipping the deal; and `PlayingCard`'s per-card gloss is one `Image` of a pre-baked gradient (`assets/card-sheen.png`) instead of a 7-view SVG on every card.

**Also landed (second pass):**
- **Saves are coalesced** (`stores/gameInProgress.ts`). Every move used to `JSON.stringify` the whole board plus the whole undo stack (each undo entry embeds a full state snapshot - ~1MB after 300 Klondike moves, several MB for a long Spider game) synchronously in the move's own commit, and a foundation cascade does dozens of moves back to back. `saveGameInProgress` now only remembers the latest object and writes it after 500ms of quiet (3s max wait during an unbroken burst); it also flushes when the app leaves the foreground, when any game screen unmounts (`useSaveInProgressBackstop`), and before every read, and `clearGameInProgress` drops a pending write so it can't resurrect a cleared game. A hard crash can lose only the last half second of moves. Covered by `src/__tests__/stores/gameInProgress.test.ts`. `lastActiveGame` also skips rewriting an unchanged value.
- **Deal ghosts are a small pool of reusable slots** (`DealLayer.tsx`: `GhostSlot`, `ghostPoolSize`, `LOOKAHEAD_MS`/`LAUNCH_TICK_MS`; the absolute-deadline departures, face-up launch cap and slot-idle coalescing added on top are described in the face-up bullet below). A deal used to mount a full `PlayingCard` per dealt card (~52 mounts + 52 unmounts of card art; measured at ~half of a deal's remaining JS cost). Now `start()` sizes a fixed pool from the delays (the most flights that can overlap, counting each ghost as occupied from 150ms before its flight to ~80ms after it lands: 17 slots for 28 cards, 31 for 52) and `launchDue()` hands each card to a free slot ~150ms before its flight, re-basing its delay onto the launch. The deal's clock starts when the slots have MOUNTED (the `[generation]` effect), not at `start()`, so a slow mount delays every departure uniformly instead of spending the first cards' delays and sending them off together; cards that are LATE after a JS stall are spaced `LATE_LAUNCH_MIN_GAP_MS` apart (on-time cards keep their scheduled time) so a hitch doesn't become a blob plus a second blob. A slot is launched imperatively (`launch(spec, order)`: shared values reset to the origin/opacity 0 *before* the React commit that shows it, so the previous landing spot never flashes) and is hidden again by React state (`job` -> null), NOT a UI-thread opacity write, so it disappears in the same commit the caller's `onCardLanded` reveals the real card (a UI write ahead of that commit would blink a card-shaped hole). A landed slot is offered for reuse only from an EFFECT after that commit (`onIdle`), never from the landing callback itself - relaunching it in its landing tick would write the reset to the UI thread ahead of the reveal. Face-down deals reuse identical backs (the slot's face element is `useMemo`'d, so a relaunch renders nothing); face-up deals re-render a face instead of mounting one. `order` becomes `zIndex` so a later card still paints above an earlier one whichever slot it lands in. Skip lands both the in-flight slots (`completeNow`) and the not-yet-launched queue; a restart mid-deal keys slots by `${generation}-${slot}` (same key-reuse guard as before). The click and buzz play on the deal's beat, not per card (see Haptics below). Covered by `src/__tests__/components/dealLayer.test.tsx`; verified on device for Yukon (52), Klondike (28) and FreeCell (52).
- **Every flight container must have an explicit, non-zero size - a React Native (Fabric, iOS) view-recycling bug puts a zero-frame view at its PREVIOUS life's position.** `RCTViewComponentView`'s `prepareForRecycle` resets its stored `_layoutMetrics` to a default-constructed ZERO frame (not `EmptyLayoutMetrics`, whose size is -1x-1), and `updateLayoutMetrics` only re-applies `center`/`bounds` when the frame changed or the old metrics are `Empty` - so a recycled UIView reused for a view whose Yoga frame is exactly `(0, 0, 0, 0)` keeps the position it had before it was recycled. DealLayer's slot wrapper (`styles.slot`: absolute, top/left 0, only an absolute child) and DragGhostLayer's ghost container (only absolute children) laid out as exactly that. New Game unmounts all 52 real tableau cards (a card whose flight hasn't landed is only a bare `FlyingCardSlot` - see the dim-after-deal bullet below), whose outer views have frame `(0, index * fanOffset)` inside their column - a slot wrapper that drew one from the (LIFO) recycle pool sat at `y = index * fanOffset`, and every card that slot ever flew rendered exactly that many fan steps too low (x untouched, since a card's x within its column is 0), from its very first flight, for the whole deal, while the shared values, the shadow tree AND `measureInWindow` all said it was on target (they read the shadow tree; only the UIView was stale). Then the ghost's settle check passed (the values WERE right), the real card revealed at the right spot, and the card visibly "snapped up" - the bug this project chased across several sessions as a timing/settle problem. Pinned down with a frame-scanning detector over Simulator recordings (a card top edge below the deepest legal row): offsets of exactly 4 and 5 fan steps, always the same two of 18 slots, in every deal; 0 anomalous frames in 392 over 5 deals once the wrapper and card box carry `slotSize` (`width/height` = card size), and DragGhostLayer's container its group size. Ruled out on the way (each an A/B on the Simulator, not reasoning): the wrapper's zIndex/opacity toggling, the card box's size changing at its first face mount, Reanimated's synchronous transform path (animating `top`/`left` instead had the identical offsets), and card-view recycling alone (keeping the cards mounted only reduced it - the previous deal's pool and other unmounts feed the same pool). Guarded by `dealLayer.test.tsx`'s "ghost slot frames" and `dragGhostLayer.test.tsx`'s "ghost container frame" - nothing else about the layout needs the size, so it is exactly what a cleanup pass would remove. Pyramid's/Spider's/WinCascade's flight views have in-flow card content (non-zero frames) and are not exposed; a NEW overlay whose only children are absolute must set a size.
- **Unplayable cards dim once, for the whole board, after the deal - never card by card while it deals** (`games/shared/useDimAfterDeal.ts`, wired into FreeCell, Golf, Forty Thieves and Pyramid - the games whose deal leaves face-up cards buried; Spider/Canfield deal none, so they don't need it). A screen calls `hold()` with its New Game, `release()` when the deal has landed (its `onAllLanded`, and every bail-out path) and `releaseNow()` on restore; the dimming (`PileView`'s `dimUnplayable`, Pyramid's `dimHold`) is off while `held`, and turns on `DEAL_DIM_SETTLE_MS` (150ms) after the last landing - every buried card's scrim then fades in together (`dimFadeIn`/`HeldDimScrim`), and a `DEAL_DIM_MAX_HOLD_MS` backstop dims a deal that never reports back. Why: dimming each card as it landed mounted ~35 animated scrims one by one under cards still in flight, and **on iOS a fade's final opacity was measurably lost** - a whole deal's first row of buried cards stayed undimmed while JS had rendered them dimmed and every fade reported `finished` (reproduced ~1 deal in 3, pixel-checked; a resumed board's plain scrim never failed). Reanimated then committed animated props through the shadow tree (`IOS_SYNCHRONOUSLY_UPDATE_UI_PROPS` was off until 2026-10-17), where they raced React's own commits. So **`FadingDimScrim` hands over to the plain, React-owned scrim the moment its fade reports done** (`fadeInDimScrim`'s `onDone`, `DIM_FADE_BACKSTOP_MS` if the callback never comes) - a finished dim is never left depending on the last value an animation wrote. The hand-over stays with the flag on (the fade is written straight to the view now): rechecked 2026-10-17 on the Simulator with Release builds, 5 FreeCell deals per build, each settled board pixel-identical to the same board resumed after a relaunch. Verified: 11 FreeCell deals and a Forty Thieves deal pixel-checked after settling (0 wrongly lit cards; the flagged ones were all real two-card runs), and frame-tracked: ~0 dimmed cards during the deal, all ~43 at once after. **The dim waits for the deal to finish in JS, not on screen:** the ghosts reach their spots on the UI thread on schedule, but each landing's reveal commit costs JS time, so the landing reports trail the ghosts (52-card FreeCell: last report ~2.1s after the first against a ~1.72s schedule), and the dim - and the skip layer's end - follow the reports, roughly 0.5s after the board looks complete.
- **A card whose flight has not landed is a `FlyingCardSlot`, not nothing and not the real card** (`PileView.tsx`). Every game's deal (all but FreeCell, which computes destinations analytically) and every tap-to-move/drop onto a tableau column finds where to fly by measuring the destination card through `CardRegistry` (`measureInWindow` on a registered, non-zero-size view). An earlier pass skipped flying cards from the tree entirely to save their mount cost and adapted only FreeCell - **that silently dropped eight games' deal animations (Forty Thieves measured 0/40 destinations; the cards just appeared) and every tableau-bound flight (the ghost was hidden, the card popped in)**, and no test noticed. Mounting the real `DraggableTableauCard` hidden instead was measurably slower (tap to first landing 0.85-1.25s against ~0.6s; landing reports trailing by ~1.4s against ~0.35s), so the slot is one bare, card-sized, registered `View` at the card's spot, and the real card mounts in its place when the flight lands. Pinned by `pileViewMemo.test.tsx` ("keeps a card whose flight has not landed mounted and registered"). **When you change what a shared component mounts, check every game's use of what it used to provide** - here, a registration nine screens measure.
- **Every face-up card is ONE pre-rasterized `Image`** (`PlayingCard`; `src/components/cardFaceBitmaps.ts` + `assets/cardFaces/`, both GENERATED by `node scripts/generate-card-face-bitmaps.mjs` - rerun it whenever `@tastic/cards`' `CardFace` art or `palettes.ts`' card inks change; nothing checks for drift). A live `CardFace` is 14-17 native views (a J/Q/K ~100 with the vector figure, 17 with `@tastic/cards`' court-figure bitmap, which this supersedes - `bitmapCourtFigures` is no longer passed anywhere here); a full face-up deck went ~785 views -> 52. All 4 card styles x 2 inks (`normal`; `invert` = `invertDarkModeColors` in DARK mode - in light mode invert is meant to be a no-op, so it draws `normal`, which differs from the old vector only by the frame border being `#000` instead of `#1A1A1A`) x 52 = 416 faces, 392 files after dedupe (compact/numeric share courts), 3.6MB. The generator renders the REAL `CardFace` (esbuild + react-native-web SSR + headless Chrome over CDP, software-rendered and whole-pixel-aligned so it is byte-for-byte repeatable) at 288px wide (CARD_WIDTH_MAX at 3x) laid out at 1 CSS px per device px - laying out a 96pt card at DPR 3 made Chrome snap the frame's 1.92pt border to 1pt - then quantizes to 256-colour PNGs (`upng-js`; mean error <0.2/255). `palettes.ts` exists so the generator can bundle the inks without Paper/the profile store (`theme.ts` re-exports it). Measured on the iOS Simulator (dev bundle, the lag metric below, 4 FreeCell New Game deals each): 1349-1450ms of JS lag per deal with the vector faces (court bitmaps on) vs 1024-1150ms with face bitmaps (~-24%). A frame scan of a recorded deal found no blank/unloaded face, but the recorder only kept ~87 frames, so a one-frame decode flash is not ruled out - check a Release build on a device. `FoundationSlot`'s muted hint and `SettingsDialog`'s style swatches still draw the vector `CardFace`.
- **`PileView` is memoized with a custom comparator** (`pileViewPropsEqual`): a card landing anywhere replaces the one `hiddenCardIds` Set every column shares, so it compares that Set by membership over the pile's own cards, not identity, and FreeCell hands each column a stable `drag`/`onPressCard` (`dragConfigs`/`onPressCardConfigs`, reading the live handlers through `latestRef`). **Anything `drag.getRunCount` reads besides the pile's own `cards` must be passed as `runCountKey`** - FreeCell's supermove capacity (`maxSupermoveCount(state, -1)`) comes from the freecells and empty columns elsewhere on the board, and without the key a column whose own cards didn't change kept stale run counts: a run that had just become movable stayed dimmed and undraggable. Pinned by `pileViewMemo.test.tsx`. A screen that builds `drag` inline every render (every other game) re-renders its piles anyway and needs neither.
- **Initial deals are compressed to ~1.2s** (`dealStaggerMs(count)` in `dealSpring.ts`). A 52-card deal (FreeCell, Yukon, Spider, Scorpion) used to take ~2.1s with the board locked (only Home stayed live); each game's initial-deal line now uses `step.visitOrder * dealStaggerMs(sequence.length)`, which tightens the stagger just enough to fit `DEAL_TARGET_TOTAL_MS` (floor 14ms; 28-card deals keep ~34ms so they are unchanged). Measured on Yukon: 52-card deals now land in 1.22-1.29s (was ~2.1s) with ~770-1050ms of JS lag over the deal (was ~1250 over 2.1s). Stock draws and undo flights keep `DEAL_STAGGER_MS`. The remaining fixed cost is the ~400-500ms board commit at deal start (the pool size doesn't affect it: capping the pool at 10 left the start stall unchanged).
- **Face-up deals are slower on purpose, and hand off without a visible pop** (FreeCell, Yukon's face-up half, Forty Thieves, Golf). The compressed ~18ms stagger is fine for identical card backs, but a face-up ghost is a distinct readable card that costs a real React commit per launch (a court card is ~100-150 SVG nodes, 63% of a face-up deck's nodes; a face-down relaunch renders nothing), so those deals janked. `paceFaceUpDeal(entries)` (`dealSpring.ts`; applied in each of those four `runDeal`s right after the entries are built, before anything reads them) stretches only the gaps in front of FACE-UP groups to `FACE_UP_STAGGER_MS` (44ms; picked when every card clicked and buzzed, each throttled to one per 40ms; a deal now plays both on its own beat, see Haptics below) capped so the whole deal fits `FACE_UP_TARGET_TOTAL_MS` (2000ms): FreeCell ~33.7ms/2.0s (was 1.2s), Forty Thieves 44ms/2.0s, Golf 44ms/1.8s, Yukon 28 backs at 18ms then 24 faces at 44ms/1.8s. A gap never shrinks and face-down flights keep their exact delays; Klondike/Spider/Scorpion/Canfield/Aces Up/Pyramid/TriPeaks are deliberately not wired (face-down, or already ~33ms so 44 buys nothing). A NEW face-up game must call it too, or it silently keeps the fast pace. Alongside the pace: (1) **nothing is dimmed while a deal is in the air** - superseded the per-card fade-at-landing this bullet used to describe; see the dim-after-deal bullet below. (2) **Departures are absolute deadlines** (`Job.departAt`): a slot's springs/take-off timer/landing timer count down from the LAUNCH, not from when its face commit finished, so a slow court-card commit is absorbed by the 150ms lookahead instead of pushing the departure late; a card that arrives with no runway left leaves at once but no closer than `LATE_LAUNCH_MIN_GAP_MS` to the previous take-off (`lastTakeOffRef`, shared across slots - two cards from one slow commit used to leave in the same frame). (3) **A launch pass hands at most `MAX_FACE_UP_LAUNCHES_PER_PASS` (2) face-up cards to slots** (face-down cards are never capped) and comes back on `CAPPED_TICK_MS` (16ms); the slot-idle handler only RE-ARMS the launch timer instead of calling `launchDue()` itself, so k slots landing in one commit share ONE capped pass rather than launching k faces into the next commit (pinned by `dealLayer.test.tsx`'s burst test, which fails - 10 faces in a commit - with the old direct call). (4) FreeCell/Yukon's deck placeholder hides on the deal's own clock (`onCardDealt` of the last card to leave), not a `setTimeout(lastDelayMs)` armed before the pool had mounted. **Measured only in jest/V8 - not on a phone or the simulator**: the case for court-card render cost dominating is code-level, the dim pop is code-verified but unobserved; check a Release build on a device (lag metric below) and, if it still janks, the next levers are seeding each pooled slot's initial face (the pre-roll already precedes the deal clock), bitmap court art in `@tastic/cards`, or a per-deal spring duration.
- **The corner index is a glyph-outline rank centred on the suit pip, and the fan strip is 0.45W** (`@tastic/cards` 0.3.0; 0.2.1 drew the rank as `Text` with a 0.44W strip). In the compact/numeric/tarot styles the rank is drawn from DejaVu Serif Bold glyph OUTLINES (`react-native-svg` `Path`, 0.37W, a faux-bold stroke of 0.035em, so it no longer depends on the font having loaded), each rank's ink box centred on the pip's centre (digits/A/K used to sit ~0.035W high while J/Q were already centred), and the '10' ink-left pinned 0.062W from the card edge at every width (it used to hug or poke past the edge under an absolute `-2pt` hack). The corner row grew 0.38W -> 0.39W, so `faceUpFanOffset(w)` is 0.45w (was 0.44w). Everything here that derives from it moves with it and nothing hard-codes 0.44: `PileView`'s fan, every screen's multi-card ghost `offsetStep`, `StockWaste`'s waste fan, `gameLayout.ts`'s `FACE_UP_FAN_RATIO` (so every `boardModel.ts` follows). **`CardSizeContext.tsx`'s `FACE_UP_OFFSET_RATIO` (a fraction of card HEIGHT: Pyramid/TriPeaks geometry and the Home thumbnails) went 0.26 -> 0.31 = `faceUpFanOffset(1) * CARD_ASPECT` (0.3097), because a strip shorter than the corner row clips the index: 0.26 is only 0.378W, which already left the Jack ~0.045W too tall for its strip.** `cardSizeContext.test.ts` pins the relationship to within 0.001, so a drift like the old 0.26 fails. Effect on the 874x402 fitted card widths (the real `boardModel`/`geometry` functions, run against the package tarball in a scratch clone; no test flipped): Klondike 61.4 -> 61.0, Yukon 46.2 -> 45.8, FreeCell 47.9 -> 47.3, Scorpion 58.9 -> 58.0, Canfield 59.3 -> 58.7, Forty Thieves 51.2 -> 50.6, Golf 70.5 -> 69.9, Aces Up 64.4 -> 63.8, and **Pyramid 62.8 -> 57.9 (the big one: its strip grew 19%)**; Spider (62.9, width-bound) and TriPeaks (62.9) are unchanged, and so is every portrait size. Spider is the tight one: its height fit allows 63.26pt against the 62.9 width-derived size, so a strip beyond ~0.46W would make it height-bound and flip `spider/boardModel.test.ts`'s no-op case (Canfield's "similar size to Klondike" test has a similar limit at ~0.4615W). Measured in jest only - not observed on a device. **The draw-3 waste fan is the one place the strip is not enough:** it is horizontal, so a covered card shows only its left step, and the new '10' ink reaches ~0.507W (`RANK_INK_RIGHT_FRACTION`, exported by 0.3.0; the old '10' ended near 0.42W) - `wasteFanStep` (`components/wasteFan.ts`) widens the step to `max(faceUpFanOffset(w), w * (RANK_INK_RIGHT_FRACTION + 0.02))` = ~0.527W; `StockWaste` and Klondike's/Canfield's draw-3 flight ghosts all take that one `wasteFanStepFor` (a ghost offset by the bare strip would land ~0.08W per step off the fanned card). Klondike's and Canfield's fans (the same 7-slot top row) have the room: the far card stays clear of the foundations by `W + 2*gap - 2*step`, which was only ~1pt on a phone in portrait until 2026-10-22, when `wasteFanStepFor`'s `gap` argument started capping the step for `WASTE_FAN_MIN_CLEARANCE` (4pt, see that section) (0.7-1.6pt over 320-440pt; measured 1.0pt at 402 on the web build, 2026-10-11 - this note used to say 9-10pt, the plain strip's figure), ~25pt at 768, ~27-45pt landscape (44.7pt at 874x402, 26.6pt at 568x320). Canfield's fan used to have none (the reserve sat in its slot) and kept the plain strip via StockWaste's `widenFan={false}`; no caller passes that any more - see Top rows.
- **Cards don't re-render when the screen does** (`PileView.tsx`). `DraggableTableauCard` is `React.memo`'d and every prop is a primitive or a stable identity: the fan position is `top`/`zIndex` numbers (not a `cardStyle` object built per render), and the press/drag callbacks handed to every card are stable wrappers that read the latest `cards`/`drag`/`onPressCard` through a ref refreshed after each render. A screen re-renders on every card landing, flight, hint and selection, and each one used to re-render every card's whole wrapper chain in every column. Don't pass an inline object/lambda into a card, and don't give `PileView` a callback that changes identity without going through `latest`. Pinned by `src/__tests__/components/pileViewMemo.test.tsx` (which needs stable shared-value/ref mocks - the repo-wide Reanimated mock returns fresh ones every render).
- **Per-card animation state is lazy or shared.** The hint pulse (a shared value, an animated-style mapper and a UI-thread animation) lives in `HintRing`, mounted only while a card is hinted - every card used to allocate it and run a cancel+timing animation at mount; the drag-start position values are per column (`startAbsX/Y`), not per card; and `PlayingCard`'s `StyleSheet`s are built once per theme/invert/radius and shared (`stylesFor`) instead of once per card instance.
- **The saved-game read starts on finger-down** (`preloadGameInProgress`, called from each Home tile's `onPressIn`), so the AsyncStorage round trip overlaps the tap and the screen's mount; any save or clear invalidates it, and it is skipped while a save is waiting out its debounce.
- **Tried and rejected: the React Compiler** (`experiments.reactCompiler` in `app.json`; the repo already lints for it). Measured with the deal metric below on Yukon's 52-card deal: 1354/1361/1744ms of JS lag per deal with it vs 1365/1409/1571ms without - no gain, so it isn't worth the risk to the gesture/animation code. The lag metric: a 25ms `setInterval` accumulating `now - last - 25` (idle floor is ~250ms per second of wall time from timer coalescing, so compare deals to each other, not to zero). With the card memoization above the same deal measured ~1320/1366/1085ms (about -13%); stubbing the ghosts' card content with a plain box measured ~880ms - so the ghosts' real card art (52 full `PlayingCard` mounts+unmounts) is roughly half of the deal's remaining excess JS, and the board commit at deal start most of the rest.
- **Considered and NOT shipped: corner-only rendering of covered cards.** A fanned card shows only its top strip once the next card overlaps it, yet renders its whole face (a covered court card ~98 SVG elements, a covered back ~23 views). A pixel-exact corner-only face exists (design verified against `CardFace`'s source: the strip is at most `faceUpFanOffset(w)` = 0.44w (0.45w with the glyph-outline index, see above), the center art is disjoint from it), but it is a net LOSS on a fresh deal: a covered card whose coverer hasn't landed yet has to be drawn in full (the body shows), so each covered card mounts light, remounts full at landing, then goes light again - total deal work up ~20% (the entry commit peak drops 38-57% in FreeCell/Forty Thieves/Golf/Yukon/Scorpion), and swapping `CardFace`<->a clone remounts the frame too. Only resume/mid-game boards are net wins (-18% to -54% views). Revisit once `@tastic/cards` grows a `cornerOnly` prop on `CardFace` (a ~4-line upstream change: the swap then preserves the frame/corner row and only mounts/unmounts the center) - do NOT ship an in-app clone of its private constants.

**Known remaining costs, measured/estimated but not addressed** (each needs care because of the deal/gesture machinery documented above, and ideally a Release build on a phone to profile first): (1) the board commit at deal start / game entry is ~850-1770 native views (each card ~23-41 views); (2) every tableau card still allocates a shake shared value + animated-style mapper and a Gesture (one `Pan` per column instead of per card would be a real refactor); (3) the persisted undo stack is unbounded (still one write per pause, but the blob keeps growing and is parsed on resume); (4) every game mounts twice (a chrome-less `GameLoading` commit, then the board); (5) `@tastic/cards` evaluates ~5.4MB of art data at startup and there is no `metro.config.js` (`inlineRequires` off). Don't tap while a dialog is still closing - the tap is swallowed.

**Measuring navigation cost without editing app code paths:** serve the app twice to a Debug dev client (`expo start --dev-client` vs `expo start --dev-client --no-dev --minify`, then `xcrun simctl openurl booted "exp+solitaire-app://expo-development-client/?url=http%3A%2F%2Flocalhost%3A<port>"`), and add temporary marks plus a 50ms `setInterval` lag detector that log a "JS stall Nms" when the thread was blocked >120ms. Production bundles don't forward `console.log` to Metro, so have the marks also `fetch('http://localhost:<port>/<msg>')` to a tiny local HTTP sink that appends arrivals to a file (arrival time is only reliable at stall boundaries: sends made inside one long JS task are delivered when it ends). Terminate the app between runs so each starts with zero stale screens.

**Testing rotation in the simulator:** since 2026-11-06 the OS turns the screen, so rotate the simulator itself (Device > Rotate, or Cmd-Left/Right). To SEE a ghost mid-flight, temporarily multiply `DEAL_SPRING`/`SNAP_BACK_SPRING`/`springForDistance` durations by 10. `ios/` (gitignored) targets the iOS 27 SDK; SDK 58's native template adopts UIScene, so it runs on the iOS 27 simulator as is (on SDK 57 a `plugins/withSceneDelegate.js` did that, and before it the runtime aborted with `NoSceneLifecycleAdoption`).

**Auto-play only ever follows the player's own play of a card to a foundation - in every game, a tap or a drag** (Jay, 2026-10-11: "Auto play should ONLY be instigated by the user playing a card to the foundation ... then if there are other available ones to be played THATS when auto play takes over"; and 2026-10-09: anything else "is robbing the player of their discovery"). The cascade takes ONE call for every non-silent player move, `afterPlayerMove(to)` (each screen's `applyMove`): a foundation destination arms it, ANY other destination stops a cascade still running from the player's last play (so a card that move uncovers is theirs), and a player's draw/recycle, an undo, New Game and a restore call `disarm()`. The solved-board finish is the one exception: any move of the player's that leaves the board solved starts it (next paragraph). **An arm never outlives its cascade:** `useAutoFoundationCascade` disarms on every way out of its loop (ran dry, Auto-Move off, an undo, a 3s landing wait that ran out, misses) and drops an arm its effect can't start at once; `useAutoFinish` drops a move's permission when its loop ends or it can't follow (Auto-Move off, an undo), and keeps it only while the board isn't solved yet (a cascade a foundation play set off may still make it so). Before this, a left-over arm (Auto-Move toggled off and on, a held Undo released early, a timed-out wait) restarted auto-play on the player's next unrelated move. **A card already being measured when the cascade ends doesn't commit either:** every fire function re-checks `cascade.isArmed()` after its measurement await (FreeCell's `firePlay` takes the calling automation's check - the finish passes `finish.isAllowed()`), and the cascade's own stock draws (`handleDrawStock` with `opts.sweep`) bail after their awaits if it was disarmed or the board moved on. Without it, an auto-played card mid-measurement when the player tapped the stock went up after the draw, and the draw's stale result then undid it on the board (pinned by Klondike's `an auto-played card still being measured when the player taps the stock...`). **The PLAYER's own draws never commit over a newer board either (2026-10-12):** Klondike's draw-1/recycle, Canfield's single-card draw and Forty Thieves' draw used to work out `drawStock(current)` before their measurement await and commit it after, so a second stock tap inside one measurement drew the SAME card again (two moves, two undo entries for one card) and a tap-to-move that committed meanwhile was undone in state while its ghost flew on. Each `handleDrawStock` now measures first (Klondike both the stock and the waste, since the re-read draw may turn out to be a recycle; Canfield/Forty Thieves the stock), then reads `stateRef.current`, recomputes `drawStock` and commits it - with its undo entry holding that board and `session.registerMove()` - in one synchronous stretch, branching on what the re-read draw really is (a draw, a recycle, a fan when a recycle ahead of it refilled the stock, or nothing - Forty Thieves' stock ran out). Pyramid's `handleDrawStock` is the model. What stays at the tap, before the await: `cascade.disarm()` and the `startFlights(1)` claim. A New Game or restore mid-measurement (`dealTokenRef` moved on) drops the draw without settling, since `resetFlightConcurrency` already dropped its claim. Draw-3 fans (and Canfield's recycle, which doesn't fly) have nothing to measure first and still commit at the tap. Pinned by the `the player's draw is worked out from the board as it is once its measurement is back` describes in `klondikeScreenWin`/`canfieldScreenWin`/`fortyThievesScreenWin` (their `mockMeasureViewMs` delays every `measureView`; all ten cases fail on the old code). **What went (2026-10-11):** `armFromAceEvent` - an ace a column move turned over (Klondike, Yukon) or a Canfield foundation-starter the reserve turned over flew straight up; FreeCell's tap-only cascade (`viaTap`/`armsSafeCascade`: a drag to a foundation now arms it too) and its finish after ANY move (`allowsFinish`; brought back for every auto-play game on 2026-10-12, dropped again on 2026-10-14 and brought back for good on 2026-10-17, see the next paragraph); and Klondike's/Forty Thieves' New Game and restore never disarmed, so a cascade still running carried on into the new deal (a face-up Ace there flew up). `detectRevealedCard` and Canfield's `isFoundationStarterCard` existed only for the reveal trigger and are deleted. **Still auto-played, deliberately:** whatever a foundation play (or its cascade's own moves) uncovers, if safe - an ace turned over UNDER the card the player sent up goes next; and the end-game finish through the stock (Klondike/Canfield/Forty Thieves' `isFullyAutoCompletableWithDraws`, which reads face-down and stock cards to decide) - Jay chose to keep it (2026-10-11). Spider's completed-run removal and Pyramid's win sweep are rules, not auto-play, and were left alone. **Known edge, not fixed:** a cascade can take a card the player has just picked up to drag (nothing tells it which card is in hand); the drop is then refused by the head-card check below. Pinned by `useAutoFoundationCascade.test.tsx`/`useAutoFinish.test.tsx` (rule + no-lingering-arm cases), `klondikeScreenWin.test.tsx`'s `auto-play only ever follows the player's own play of a card to a foundation` (drawn ace, column-move ace, waste reveal, a column move stopping a running cascade, New Game mid-cascade - each fails on the old code), the same-named describes in `yukonScreenWin`/`canfieldScreenWin`, and FreeCell's screen tests. A new game with auto-play must route every player move through `afterPlayerMove` and disarm on New Game/restore.

**A SOLVED board finishes by itself after any move of the player's, and its pace ramps up** (Jay, 2026-10-12, asked for it to start after ANY move; 2026-10-14 changed his mind: "I dont want it to start automatically, I want it to also wait for user interaction to play a card to the foundation, and I want the speed to ramp up, instead of just immediately flying in to the foundation"; 2026-10-17 changed it back: "I want auto play to start automatically after cards are all revealed and sorted highest to lowest in games like klondike and yukon and freecell", "and whatever else", so every game that has the finish; the ramp stays). `useAutoFinish` (FreeCell, Klondike, Yukon, Canfield, Forty Thieves; Spider and Scorpion build no foundations card by card, so a sorted board there is not one foundation moves alone can clear):
- **Solved** (each game's `canAutoFinish`): not already won (`isFullyAutoCompletable` is TRUE on a won board), nothing hidden (no face-down card anywhere; the stock, waste and Canfield's reserve empty), and every remaining card clears by foundation moves alone (the NON-draw `isFullyAutoCompletable`; Forty Thieves' own, since its 8 foundations are not one per suit). FreeCell hides nothing, so its `canAutoFinish` is just every column sorted. The hidden-card checks are the point: the draws-aware finish through the stock reads face-down and stock cards, and a plain column move setting it off would give the stock order away. That one still needs a foundation play, unchanged.
- **Trigger:** ANY move of the player's, tap or drag, to any pile, that leaves the board solved (`finish.afterPlayerMove(from)` next to `cascade.afterPlayerMove(to)`: a column move that turns the last face-down card over, one that empties the waste or the reserve, a foundation play), or the cascade a foundation play set off, the moment it makes the board solved (the move's permission is kept while the board isn't solved yet). Two things never start it. A move that takes a card back OFF a foundation (`from` a foundation): it stops one still running, and the card isn't sent straight back up. And an undo, a resume or New Game (`finish.suppress()` next to every `cascade.disarm()` there; in `startNewGame` itself, since the win banner calls it directly): undoing a card off a solved board leaves it solved, and it would fly straight back up. The player's next move starts it again. A player's draw never sets it off (a draw always leaves the stock or the waste with a card). Honors Auto-Move to Foundation.
- **Hand-off:** every game's cascade `computeNextStep` (FreeCell's `nextSafeCascadeStep`) returns null once the board is solved, so the cascade ends and the finish takes over at its own pace; otherwise both loops fight over the same cards (misses, and three misses end a loop). A draws-aware sweep through the stock switches to the finish pace the moment it empties the stock and waste.
- **Pace ramps up:** `finishGapMs(i)` (dragSpring.ts) is the gap in front of the finish's i-th card: `FINISH_RAMP_START_MS` (= `AUTO_PLAY_STAGGER_MS`, 96ms, so a cascade handing over to the finish doesn't slow down) for the first, then each gap keeps `FINISH_RAMP_FACTOR` (0.85) of what is left above `FINISH_STAGGER_MS` (`scaledMs(30)` = 48ms, the top speed): 96, 89, 83, 77, 73 ... ~59 by the 10th card, ~50 by the 20th. A miss doesn't advance the ramp. Those three constants are the knobs. Flights unchanged (`FLIGHT_SPRING`, so cards to one foundation still land in order). The gap runs on a FIXED clock: each card leaves its gap after the card before it LEFT, not after that card's measurement and commit (the old loop slept a full gap after each commit, so the real gap was 96ms plus that overhead). A card slower to measure than the gap is followed at once. 48 is just above `useCascadeFeedback`'s 45ms floor, so a step whose measurement comes back quicker than the one before can occasionally lose its tick. The hook owns the wait for a card whose own pile is still landing (`isLanding`, bounded by `FINISH_LANDING_WAIT_MS`, now in `useAutoFinish.ts`; a wait that runs out is a miss). Every screen's `firePlay` takes a third `stillWanted` check (default `cascade.isArmed()`; the finish passes `isAllowed`).
- **The clock is NOT stopped when the board is solved** (Jay, 2026-10-12): it runs until the last card commits, as before. A 52-card FreeCell finish is roughly 2.8s of take-offs (a flat 48ms gap was 2.5s; ordinary auto-play's 96ms would be 5s).
- Pinned by `useAutoFinish.test.tsx` (any-move trigger, a move off a foundation never, the ramp, fixed clock, landing wait) and each game's screen tests (a tap or a drag to a column, or off the waste, starts it; the ramped gaps, no double fire, hidden-card, off-a-foundation and undo/resume/New Game guards). Not played on a device.

**FreeCell's auto-play is two separate things** (`src/games/freecell/autoPlay.ts` is the pure policy, unit-tested in `src/__tests__/games/freecell/autoPlay.test.ts`): the cascade, started only by the player's play of a card to a foundation, and the finish, started by any move of theirs that leaves the board solved (above).
- **The safe-card cascade** (`useAutoFoundationCascade`, engine mechanism A) starts from the player's tap or drag of a card to a foundation. Like every game's cascade (see below), its cards overlap.
- **The finish** (`useAutoFinish`, engine mechanism B) starts as soon as the whole board can go up on foundation moves alone (`canAutoFinish` - every column sorted highest-to-lowest, freecell cards included) after any move of the player's, or once the cascade a foundation play set off makes it so - without waiting for that move to land. It is a plain async loop like the cascade's (below): every card, the first included, leaves its ramped gap (`finishGapMs`, 96ms down toward 48) after the one before LEFT (the player's own, for the first), so several flights overlap. The hook holds back a card still flying in to its own pile (`isLanding`, e.g. a card the player's column move put there just before their foundation play - pinned by the `CLUBS_LEFT_TWO_ON_ACE` test, which fails without it), bounded by `FINISH_LANDING_WAIT_MS` (two flights). The board's LAST card does NOT wait for the earlier flights: it leaves one stagger after the card before it like every other. It used to hold until every flight had landed so the win cascade wouldn't start over cards in the air, which read as the stream, a pause, then one straggler (Jay, 2026-10-03). That wait was redundant anyway: the win cascade starts on `winRevealed`, which waits for `settled`. Pinned by `the last card leaves one stagger after the card before it...`. `nextSafeCascadeStep` returns null the moment the board is finishable, so the two never both fire at one settle.
- **Auto-played cards follow the player's card at once and overlap, in every game with auto-play** (`useAutoFoundationCascade`, FreeCell/Klondike/Yukon/Canfield/Forty Thieves). The cascade used to send one card per settle - each card waited for the one before it to LAND, the first one for the player's own (a full flight plus two measurements and a couple of renders each), which read as "way too slow" (Jay, 2026-10-02). Now it is a loop that starts the moment it is armed (its `[state]` effect runs right after the arming move's commit) and sends every card `AUTO_PLAY_STAGGER_MS` (96ms) after the one before LEFT (a fixed clock since 2026-10-17, like the finish's; it used to count from each card's commit, after its measurement) - the first one 96ms after the player's own card, while that is still in the air - so ~5 cards are in the air at once. Because every flight takes the same time (see the flight-pace paragraph below), they land in exactly the order they left, and the gap is purely how quick the stream reads. The ONE thing a card waits for is its own pile to stop landing (`isLanding`, from `useFlightConcurrency`, kept live in `updateFlying` rather than read off a render): picking up a card whose own ghost is still airborne shows it twice (a card the sweep just drew onto the waste, a card the player just dropped there). A draw/draw-and-play waits the same way for the stock and the waste (the deal layer flies one draw-3 batch at a time; a second `deal.start` would cancel the first). After a wait the card goes at once, with no extra gap. The hook has no `settled` input any more; a wait that runs out (3s) ends the cascade (the next foundation play starts a new one). Every screen's `firePlay` resolves `true`/`false` (committed or not), for the miss count. One gap for every auto-played card: the old separate sweep gap and the `sweepingRef` latch are gone. Pinned by `useAutoFoundationCascade.test.tsx` and FreeCell's screen tests (`the follow-up cards leave one stagger apart...`). Not observed on a device.
- **A stock draw never sets it off** (Jay, 2026-10-09). Klondike, Canfield and Forty Thieves used to auto-play a drawn ace (Canfield: a drawn foundation-starter) straight up (`armFromAceEvent` in each `handleDrawStock`); that call is gone, and a player's draw or recycle (not a sweep's own draw, `opts.sweep`) calls `cascade.disarm()`, so a cascade still running from the player's last play can't take the card just drawn either. See the rule paragraph above for the rest (2026-10-11).
- **Every auto-played step is its own undo entry, in every game with auto-play** (FreeCell, Klondike, Yukon, Canfield, Forty Thieves), so one Undo takes back exactly one card and the player can stop where they like. Each silent foundation move, sweep draw and sweep recycle pushes an entry holding the board just before it; a draw-and-play (stock straight to a foundation) pushes TWO (the draw, then the waste->foundation play), so each undo is a move kind `handleUndo` already flies. Since 2026-10-14 each auto-play also counts as a move (see Scoring), and every `handleUndo` calls `cascade.disarm()` so an undone card doesn't fly straight back up. The old shapes (Klondike/Yukon/FreeCell folded auto-plays into the previous entry's `cascaded` list; Canfield/Forty Thieves left them off the stack, so they snapped back with the player's move) survive only as legacy-save handling: the `cascaded` undo paths and the buried-foundation-card lookups stay for saves written before this change. Spider's completed-run removal is a rule, not an auto-play, and still undoes with the move that completed it.
- **What suppresses it:** an undo, a resumed save and a new deal (`finish.suppress()`) - nothing finishes again until the player's next move (any move), so undoing back onto a finishable board can't immediately re-fire the cards just taken back. The player's move to a column or a freecell stops the CASCADE, not the finish (a move that keeps every column sorted lets the finish carry on). Both halves honor Settings' Auto-Move to Foundation. A resumed, already-finishable board sits until the player's next move.

**Undo is a queue, every press is one undo, and undos overlap each other's flights - a held Undo streams the cards back like the auto-play streams them up** (2026-10-07; Jay: holding Undo "only undoes one move every other click"). Every game's `handleUndo` used to refuse to start while ANY flight was in the air, and the hold repeated on a fixed 400ms `setInterval` (`@rific/feedback-press`'s `useHoldToRepeat`) against a ~540ms real undo flight: every other tick was dropped, and still clicked. Now:
- **`useFlightConcurrency`'s `runUndo(gate, run, opts)`** (every screen's `handleUndo` is just `runUndo(undoGate, revertTopUndo, opts)`): requests queue and run one at a time, in order; `run` (the screen's revert of the TOP entry) starts synchronously when it can, so a lone undo behaves exactly as before. `undoBusyRef` is now held from the REQUEST until every undo is done and its flights have landed (every other action still refuses/bails on it; the cascade and finish hold back on it), and a request that cannot start waits instead of being dropped - refused only when there is nothing to undo, when `opts.cancelled()` turns true, or after `UNDO_MAX_WAIT_MS` (3s, then `undoBusyRef` is let go). `reset()` (New Game/restore) and unmount refuse everything queued (`undoEpochRef` guards a revert still awaiting its measurements). A press event handed in as `opts` is ignored (only a real `cancelled` function counts).
- **The first undo waits for every flight in the air, as undo always did; from then on nothing but an undo can start one** (`undoStreakRef`), so each later undo checks only its own entry's **`UndoGate`**: `{ takesFrom }` starts as soon as nothing is LANDING on those piles (`isLanding` - picking up a card whose own ghost is still airborne shows it twice; the same rule the auto-play cascade follows), `'exclusive'` waits for everything to land and holds every later undo until its own flights have landed (what undo used to be for every entry), `'blocked'` waits for a reason of the screen's own (Spider's pending sweep), `null` = nothing to undo. Landing order stays FIFO because every undo flight is `FLIGHT_SPRING`, so the count-based destination hiding (`flying` entries) still reveals the right card when two undos land on one pile. **Per game:** a plain one-move revert through the ghost layer is `{ takesFrom: [move.to] }` everywhere (FreeCell, Yukon, Scorpion, Spider, Aces Up incl. the discard, draw-1 undos in Klondike/Canfield/Forty Thieves/Golf/TriPeaks); one that LANDS on the waste adds the waste (`[move.to, WASTE]` - its frozen-top bookkeeping expects one arrival at a time; Golf/TriPeaks never land on it); Pyramid's removals are `{ takesFrom: [] }` (+ `[WASTE]` for a waste-bound card - nothing lands on the discard during an undo) and its draws `[WASTE]` (overlapping draws share the one `stockHidden` flag: the first landing unfreezes the stock while a later back is still flying in, which shows a back that is already there). **`'exclusive'`:** every deal-layer revert (draw-3 fans, Spider/Scorpion stock deals, Aces Up rounds - a second `deal.start` cancels the first), recycles, Spider's run completions, legacy entries with a `cascaded` list (their single-slot `pendingCascadeUndoRef`), and every `move: null` entry. A NEW kind of undo entry needs a deliberate gate - `'exclusive'` if in doubt.
- **Cards still flying in turn back in mid-air instead of landing first (2026-10-16; Yukon first, then every game with tableau undos).** Jay found a held Undo in Yukon "a little chunky"; his phone recording showed the stream stopping dead whenever the next undo needed cards the undo before it was still flying back (a run put on a column, then cards taken off it): it waited for the landing, plus ~0.2s of nothing (the spring's invisible tail before its settle timer, then the new ghost's paint gate), so that gap was ~0.5s instead of ~0.1s. In both stops the next undo took only the TOP part of the run in the air, never exactly the same cards. Now `DragGhostController.peel(content)` takes the top cards of a `show()` flight that has shown its ghost and not landed (`canPeel`), giving them a flight of their own: the new ghost rides along with them (it follows the old flight's values, `followIndex` cards down its fan: `liveLift`/`liveStep`) until its `flyTo` starts it, and then one `scheduleOnUI` worklet (`takeOver`) hides them in the old ghost (`visibleCount`, per-card opacity), copies where they are drawn and springs on from there (`lift`, the offset down the old fan, springs to 0). The rest of the old flight lands as before. Yukon's `undoGate` returns `{ takesFrom: [] }` when `ghost.canPeel(undoGhost(...))`, and `revertTopUndo` peels in the same task, with no await before it, and calls `useFlightConcurrency`'s `shrinkLanding(primary.to, count)` so the old flight's landing reveals only what it still carries. A peeled flight is never adopted (`adoptable` wants every card drawn), its landed hold waits only for the cards it still draws, and a peel hidden before it takes over still hides its cards in the old ghost. Still waits as before: an undo that needs MORE than what is in the air (some cards on the pile, some flying). Known edge, not fixed: the rest of a peeled run keeps the landing spacing it measured before the peel, so in a compressed column it can land a few points off the real cards' spacing. Unfanned ghost cards are now animated views too (`StackedGhostCard`, opacity only), for a whole-stack peel. Verified on the web build in headless Chrome over CDP (a seeded 6S-5H-4C chain, a held Undo, every face image logged per frame at 60fps): the 4C turned back 133ms after the run left and the 5H 84ms after that, against ~1.1s and ~1.6s before, with no frame missing a card, no two copies of a card apart, and at most 16px of motion per frame. Not checked on a simulator or device (the simulator's dev client was SDK 57). **Rolled out (Jay, same day)** to Klondike, FreeCell, Spider, Scorpion, Canfield, Forty Thieves and Aces Up, each the same shape as Yukon: an `undoGhost(move, current)` helper shared by `undoGate` and the revert, the peel before any await, `shrinkLanding` on the pile it took them from. Per game: Klondike, Canfield and Forty Thieves never peel off a flight landing on the WASTE (its frozen top and fan expect one arrival at a time, so a draw's undo still waits for its card to land there; a waste-bound undo keeps `[WASTE]` in its gate either way); FreeCell peels off freecells too, and its freecell's `isFlyingHere` now ignores an entry a peel emptied (`f.count > 0`); Aces Up never peels off the discard (nothing lands there during an undo); Spider's is in `finishMoveUndo` (its `undoGhost` is declared above it: the React Compiler lint flags a use before the declaration). Golf, TriPeaks and Pyramid need nothing: their undos take from the waste or the discard, which no undo lands on. Pinned by `dragGhostLayer.test.tsx`'s peel block and each game's `an undo that needs ... still flying back` describe (every one fails with its gate's `canPeel` switched off; Klondike's also checks the waste still waits). The screen tests' stand-in ghosts get `canPeel`/`peel` from `src/testUtils/peelableFlights.ts` (tracks which cards each stand-in flight still carries); a suite that is not about undo chains stubs them to never peel (`winCascadeHandoff`, `spiderSequenceHold`). Only Yukon was checked on the web build; none of it on a device.
- **A tapped run that reaches into cards still flying in to its column sends them on from mid-air too (2026-10-16, Jay's last pre-release bug).** His Yukon recording: the 8D's 13-card run tapped onto the 9C, then the QH above that 9C tapped ~350ms later, before the run had landed. The QH's run (17 cards) was lifted whole from the column, so the 13 in the air were drawn twice (the first ghost still landing them, the new one carrying them off), and the column's landing count (`flying`, still 13) hid EVERY card left in it (the face-down cards and the King just turned up) until the first flight landed. `games/shared/inAirRun.ts`: `liftTappedRun` (each column game's `handleCardTap`, between its `startFlights(1)` and `completeMove`) shows a ghost of only the run's cards on the board, and `completeMove`'s `inAir` pieces are `peelInAir`ed AFTER `applyMove` commits (a peeled card can't go back), each with its own `beginFlying` and pending flight to its bottom card, after the board ghost so they start and land in stacking order; `shrinkLanding(from, n)` clears the old column's count. Spider hangs a completed run's sweep on the last (top) piece. When the cards can't be peeled yet (their flight's ghost not shown, a drag's drop still settling, or two flights' cards not each the top of one flight), the tap polls (`whenReady`, up to two flights) until they can be or have landed. `useFlightConcurrency` gained `landingCount(pile)`. Two more pieces: `DragGhostLayer`'s peel now marks its cards ghost-pending under `peel:<id>` (no pile reads those ids), and a landed flight waits on them, so a flight landing before its peeled cards' new ghost has painted keeps drawing them (it vanished them for a frame or two before; an Undo peel could hit it too). A DRAG never picks up such a run: `PileView` passes `liftable={false}` (the pan is off, the tap still works) to every card under the topmost card still flying in, except cards of a drag already under way. Verified on the web build in headless Chrome over CDP (the recording's exact board seeded, the two taps 91-631ms apart, every face image and back logged per frame): 0 frames with a card drawn twice and 0 with the column blank, against 9 and 11 with the fix disabled, and the QH run leaving 88-116ms after its tap at every gap (93ms before the fix), so no tap waited. Not checked on a device. Pinned by `yukonScreenWin.test.tsx`'s `a tapped run that reaches into cards still flying in` and `dragGhostLayer.test.tsx`'s landed-before-the-peel case and `PileView - a run still landing on its column` (each fails with its change removed). Only Yukon has a screen test; the other six games run the same two helpers.
- **Reverts read the stack and board through refs, never the render closure** (several undos can run between two renders): `useUndoStack` (`games/shared/useUndoStack.ts`, a `useState` whose stable setter also updates `undoStackRef` synchronously - every write must go through it) and `stateRef`; `resolvePile` takes the live board (`resolvePile(ref, current)`), Spider's `finishMoveUndo(current, ...)` too.
- **The hold is `components/undoHold.ts`'s `useUndoHold`, not `useHoldToRepeat`:** undo at the long press, then the next one `UNDO_HOLD_REPEAT_MS` (= `AUTO_PLAY_STAGGER_MS`, 96ms) after each undo has STARTED (the request resolves at its commit); it passes `cancelled` = "the finger lifted", so releasing never lets one more through; it stops at the first refused request (nothing left), at press-out and on unmount. It has no click of its own: each undo's card sound + haptic is the feedback, so there is exactly one per card that comes back (and since 2026-10-16 its long press plays nothing either, see Hints). `GameChromeProps.onUndo` is an `UndoHandler` (`(opts?) => Promise<boolean> | void`); its tap is `onPress={() => void onUndo()}`.
- **Verified** on the iOS Simulator (Metro-served Debug build, frame recordings): FreeCell, 13 undos in ~4s held and 7 fresh ones in ~1.3s, several cards in the air at once, nothing shown twice or blank; Klondike draw-1 undos overlap back into the stock. Two things seen there are pre-existing, not this change: a legacy `cascaded` entry's auto-plays fly back on the DEAL layer, which paints above every DragGhostLayer ghost and has no take-off hold - the cascaded card was visible nowhere for ~2 frames and drawn over the primary card landing on it (see "Not covered" in the take-off/landing paragraph below); and a draw-undo ghost shows its face on the stock for ~100-200ms before the back. Pinned by `useFlightConcurrency.test.tsx` (the runner, against a stand-in screen), `undoHold.test.tsx`, FreeCell's `undos pressed back to back ...` / `an undo pressed while the tapped card is still flying ...`, and Pyramid's/Spider's "undo waits" cases (which used to assert a refusal).

**Every card flight takes ONE fixed time, there is ONE pace knob, and a ghost is only ever retired at its spring's REAL end.** `dragdrop/dragSpring.ts`'s `FLIGHT_SPRING` (scale-1 225ms = 360ms at the default) is every flight: tap-to-move, undo, draw-1, a recycle, every auto-played and finish card (`DragGhostLayer`'s `flyTo` on a `show()` flight), Pyramid's pair flights (`FlyingPairCard`), Spider's completed-run flights (`FlyingSequenceCard`) and Klondike's/Canfield's draw-3 fans and their undo (through the deal layer: `DealAnimator.start`'s optional 6th `spring` argument, which also sizes the ghost pool and times the landing check) - however far it goes (Jay, 2026-10-01). Distance-scaled flights let a short hop overtake a long one to the same pile (two quick taps, or an auto-played card after a tap), and `FoundationCell`'s `settledCard` counts in-air cards, so the overtaken card showed on the pile while still airborne; and once flights overlap, a stream's pace is its gap, not its flight.
