Two exceptions: **a drag's drop** (`flyTo` on a `showLive()` flight - `flyTo` takes no pace options, it decides by `source`) settles over `dropSpringFor(the distance the finger left)` (scale-1 130-320ms = 208-512), because the card is usually nearly there and a full flight would leave the ghost covering the real card after every drag; and **everything else through the deal layer** (`DEAL_SPRING`, 280ms, unscaled - `start`'s default: deals, Spider/Scorpion stock deals, Aces Up rounds, Pyramid's win sweep, Klondike's legacy cascade-return undo). `SNAP_BACK_SPRING` (240ms) is unchanged too. `FLIGHT_TIME_SCALE` (1.6) multiplies the flight and drop durations. `scaledMs(ms)` is how any constant that must follow a flight derives from it (never hard-code a second copy): `AUTO_PLAY_STAGGER_MS` (`scaledMs(60)` = 96) and `FINISH_STAGGER_MS` (`scaledMs(30)` = 48, the solved-board finish's top speed), so the auto-play streams overlap their flights the same way at any pace, `useAutoFinish`'s `FINISH_LANDING_WAIT_MS` (2 x `springSettleMs(FLIGHT_SPRING)` = 966), `GameChrome`'s `UNDO_HOLD_REPEAT_MS` (= `AUTO_PLAY_STAGGER_MS`, see the undo paragraph above), Pyramid's `DEFER_MAX_WAIT_MS` (3 x a flight's settle = ~1.45s, so a deferral can never expire before its own draw ghost can land) and `SWEEP_WIN_REVEAL_MAX_WAIT_MS` (twice the nominal last-removal + sweep + hold timeline). Deliberately NOT scaled: `DEAL_SPRING`/`dealStaggerMs` (the deal), `SNAP_BACK_SPRING`, `useWinReveal`'s 400ms hold (a beat after the LAST landing, whatever the pace) and its 2.5s max wait (still ~3.7x the slowest single flight), `useCascadeFeedback`'s 45ms floor (a sound-pool limit, not a pace). **Reanimated's duration-based `withSpring` `duration` is the PERCEPTUAL duration: the spring really runs 1.5x that** (springUtils.ts's `perceptualCoefficient`), **and its solver caps stiffness at 8000, which with the default mass of 4 is reached at ~184ms: every shorter duration silently ran as the SAME ~283ms spring.** So the old 40-170ms (scale 1) sweep/auto-play flights were ~283ms springs whose ghost was retired at 40-170ms, with up to ~180px still to travel - the "cards vanish partway through" report. `FLIGHT_SPRING_MASS` (0.1) lifts the cap (critically damped motion depends only on sqrt(stiffness/mass)); the springs are exact down to ~30ms. Every timer that covers a spring uses `springSettleMs(config)` = `ceil(1.25 x duration + 33)`, never the bare `duration` (`DragGhostLayer`'s `settleAfter` - snapBack included - Pyramid's `FlyingPairCard`, Spider's `FlyingSequenceCard`; the deal's own timer is left at the bare `DEAL_SPRING` duration - measured 2.5px short of a 300px flight (7px of 900px) with the spring two frames late, judged not visible; a deliberate follow-up if it ever is): the 1.25 puts the tail under 0.4px for any distance, and the 33ms (two 60Hz frames) is the UI thread starting the spring after the JS timer that covers it - absolute, so it is added rather than folded into the factor (a flat 1.3 leaves 2.6px of a 100px sweep flight). `src/__tests__/components/dragSpring.test.ts` drives Reanimated's OWN `withSpring` frame by frame and requires < 1px residual at the retire time for every config at 60-900px (worst 0.68px, with the spring starting two frames late; the old timer left 50-310px on the sweep tier), and that every flight really ends at 1.5 x its duration. **Same-source hand-off rule (`DragGhostLayer.tsx`):** `showLive`'s persistent gesture values are shared by every drag from one slot/column, so a flight only sits on them WHILE ITS DRAG IS IN PROGRESS - `DragFlightHandle.release()` (called by `PileView`/`DraggableCard` as the first thing at drag end, before any await: a drop can sit in a hit test, a measurement or Pyramid's deferral for hundreds of ms) hands the ghost values of its own holding the same position. The ghost reads BOTH value sets from its first render and a `handedOff` shared value, written last, switches it (no re-render, no remount, no seam - cloning values under a mounted ghost, tried before, needs a re-render and jumps). After release a next drag from the same source can never touch the flight, applied or not; only a SNAP-BACK in progress (the very card being picked up again) and a drag that never released are still ended by a new claim (`liveOwnersRef`). `PileView` also captures its drag's own flight BEFORE the hit-test await (it used to read `flightRef` after it, so an overlapping drag's drop was applied with - and flew - the wrong ghost), and both `PileView` and `DraggableCard` stamp each drag with a token so a late drop/snap-back outcome never un-hides the cards under a NEWER drag. A flight's motion and settle timer begin once its ghost view has MOUNTED (`MountGate`, a layout effect): `flyTo` right after `show()` (the undo recycle flights) used to start the spring a whole render-and-commit before the ghost existed, so the ghost first appeared partway along its path. Pinned by `dragGhostLayer.test.tsx` (the real provider + `PileView`/`DraggableCard` with captured pan callbacks and a Reanimated stand-in whose values really animate over fake time).

**A landed card is never handed over to a face that has not painted, and a shake plays once per card - the "stutters at the end of a move" fix (2026-10-03, both reproduced and verified on the Simulator with a frame detector).** Two separate glitches read as that report. (1) **Shake replay:** most screens keep their last `shake` for good (only Pyramid and TriPeaks clear it, on New Game/restore), and both card components played the shake whenever `shakeToken` was truthy, mount included - so the last card ever tapped while stuck wiggled again right after it LANDED anywhere (PileView remounts a card at landing), after an undo, in the next deal (ids are reused). Pixel-measured: 36/36 landings of a shaken card wiggled; 0/37 after. `useShakeStyle(shakeToken, cardId)` (`shakeAnimation.ts`) plays a token only once per CARD: seeded with the token it mounts with, re-seeded (not played) when a reused slot (the waste top, Canfield's reserve - one unkeyed `DraggableCard` showing whichever card is on top) starts showing a different card. A real shake always targets a card already on screen, so that is safe. (2) **Blank face at the hand-off:** a face is ONE RN `<Image>` (`cardFaceBitmaps.ts`), and RN's iOS `RCTImageManager.requestImage` always `dispatch_async`es the load (only `RCTRunningInTestEnvironment` gets the sync manager), so a face mounted in a commit is filled in a frame or two later unless the load beat the mount - a plain face-colour card with no ink in between. Every landing reveals a freshly mounted card (PileView swaps a `FlyingCardSlot` for the real card), so a landed card flashed blank (~1 in 25 tableau landings, a Metro-served dev build on the Simulator). `cardFacePaint.ts` is a registry of board faces still loading (`PlayingCard`'s `CardFaceImage` reports from its layout effect until its `onLoad`, only when the native image will really reload - a cardId change on the same bitmap, e.g. twin cards in two-deck games, or a Fast Refresh, would open a tracker nothing closes); **ghosts never report** (`FaceLoadTrackingContext` false in DragGhostLayer, DealLayer, WinCascadeLayer, Pyramid's and Spider's flight overlays - a new overlay must wrap its cards the same way). `DragGhostLayer` marks a settled flight `landed` (same commit as the screen's reveal - both set from the settle timer) and its provider's layout effect - which runs after the board's in that commit - removes the ghost once none of its card ids is loading AND none is taking off again with a ghost not on screen yet (`whenCardsSettled` - it is often the only painted copy of its card), else on that, capped at `LANDED_GHOST_MAX_MS` (250); a landed ghost draws no shadow (the real card's own shows), and is dropped at once when a newer card lands over its rest rect (`land()`; a quarter turn swaps the rect's axes and spans the sideways fan). `DealLayer`'s slots do the same (`holding`, `DEAL_LANDED_MAX_MS` 150): the slot is idle for the pool's bookkeeping all along (a relaunch ends the hold), the provider keeps the pool mounted past the deal's end while any slot holds (`anyHold`, only updated after the deal so landings don't re-render the provider), and the same overlap rule applies (`handleHold`). Related: a card turning face-up IN PLACE (the card a move exposes) keeps its back drawn over the new face until it paints (`PlayingCard`'s `backUntilPainted`, board cards only, `FLIP_BACK_MAX_MS`); a hidden (dragged) face-up card keeps its face Image mounted; and the single-card piles' "card before the flight" overlays (foundation, discard, waste, Canfield reserve) stay mounted and invisible (`PileOverlay`: `aria-hidden`, its card `disabled` - on web a Pressable inside still takes clicks and Tab focus) so starting a flight only flips their opacity instead of mounting a blank Image. The stock's overlay is not one of them: a back is SVG, drawn in the frame it mounts, and always mounting it swallowed stock clicks on web. **Take-off (2026-10-04/05, Jay's call):** a NEW ghost is a fresh Image too, so it was blank at its source in the commit the move removed the real card (most tap moves). Every ghost overlay now waits for its faces (`useGhostPaintGate`: opacity 0 until each face's `PlayingCard onFacePainted` (on Android, a bitmap already decoded reports in the commit that mounts it: see "Android: a flight leaves in the commit that mounts its ghost" at the end), or a cap - `GHOST_PAINT_MAX_MS` 80 for flights, the win cascade passes its own), and its motion and settle timer start only when it shows (DragGhostLayer's MountGate opens in `onVisible`; Pyramid's `PairFlightGroup`, Spider's `SequenceFlightGroup`). The cap alone does not keep a stream in order (it counts from each ghost's own commit, and adopted or face-down ghosts skip it), so DragGhostLayer lets its `show()` flights start in `show()` order (`startQueueRef`: a flight's MountGate opens once its ghost is visible AND every earlier queued flight's has opened; any removal drains the queue; `showLive` drags are left out). It orders gate openings, not `flyTo` calls: landing order also relies on callers calling `flyTo` in start order, which every screen's streams do (FIFO measurements). Meanwhile the SOURCE keeps drawing the departing card - the same, already-painted instance: a flight marks its visible face-up cards with `markGhostPending` synchronously as it starts (in `show`/`showLive`/`startPairFlight`/`startSequenceFlight`, before the move's commit renders; only the top card of an offsetStep-0 stack; Spider's restore flights and the undo-deal flights take theirs at REVERT time instead), released in the commit its ghost shows, in `hide()`, on unmount, or by a 1s backstop (`GHOST_PENDING_MAX_MS`).
- **PileView** keeps a card that left `cards` and was DRAWN last render (not a FlyingCardSlot) at its old top, same key, inert (`held`: pointerEvents none, unregistered from CardRegistry), and lays the column out in the fan each held card was drawn in (`DepartingCard.slots`) until all of them have gone, so a compressed column doesn't re-spread under a held card and a second departure during a hold keeps its drawn top. A dragged card is hidden only once its drag ghost isn't pending (`activeDrag` is keyed by card ids).
- **Single-card piles** use `useTakeOffHold` (StockWaste's waste top and stock, FoundationCell, DiscardCell, FreecellCell, ReserveCell, Spider's completed-run slots, Pyramid/TriPeaks cells): a held card is `disabled` with no press/drop - deliberately NOT forwarded to the pile's real top, since a forwarded tap would send off a card that was never drawn at its source (the stock does forward: identical backs) - gets no hint/shake, registers the pile's real top (`DraggableCard`'s `registryId`), and isn't hidden by a card arriving on the same pile (FreecellCell/ReserveCell gate their flight hide and overlay on `!holding`). When a hold ends on a pile that still has a card, the hook REVEALS: the slot's one Image was just re-sourced from the departed card and keeps that bitmap on iOS, so `revealing` hides the real card and shows the always-mounted `PileOverlay` (painted with the new top since the move's commit) until the new face paints (`REVEAL_MAX_MS` 150) - wired in the waste top, FoundationCell, DiscardCell and ReserveCell (not while `dealHidden`). Known residual (in `useTakeOffHold.ts`): a card taking off again mid-reveal is held by the real card, possibly with a stale bitmap. `DraggableCard`'s post-drop reveal (`revealToken`) likewise waits while its own drag ghost is pending, then until the next card's face has painted.
- **A lifted card shows the card under it** (2026-10-11, Jay): `DraggableCard`'s `underCard` is drawn in the slot while its own drag hides the card, instead of `emptyFallback`'s empty outline. It stays mounted and invisible the rest of the time, so its face has painted before a drag shows it. It is frozen while the card is lifted (`useFrozenWhileHidden`), because an applied drop changes the pile under a slot that stays hidden until its next face paints, and the card two down would otherwise flash there. It is never shown while the caller's `hidden` is on (its overlay is in charge then). Wired for the waste (`StockWaste`'s `wasteUnder`, passed by all six callers) and Canfield's reserve (a back). While a slot still holds a card that has left, the card under it is the pile's TOP (`holding ? top : under`), not the card two down. A draw-3 fan that already shows the card under (`wasteBehind[0]`) gets neither the card nor the outline. Pinned by `dropReveal.test.tsx` and `stockWaste.test.tsx`.
- **A flight's `beginFlying` pile is where it LANDS, never where it left.** Klondike's recycle counted itself as landing on the waste, which froze the waste on its last card (StockWaste's under-the-ghost overlay) for the whole flight: the last dealt card showed twice, once on the waste and once flying to the stock (fixed 2026-10-11; Pyramid's recycle was already right). Pinned by `klondikeScreenWin.test.tsx`'s recycle case. The same goes for `stockHidden`, which is only for a flight landing ON the stock (a draw's undo, a recycle). Undoing a recycle (Klondike's and Pyramid's) used to set it too, freezing the stock the cards were LEAVING, so a back sat on it for the whole flight while a face-up card flew to the waste. Now the stock empties as they take off, and the ghost is ONE face-down card for the pile (a stack shows only its top, and 24 SVG backs would be costly to mount). It carries the id of the card that ends on top of the waste, so the landed ghost waits for that face. A face-down ghost has nothing to paint, so it shows in the commit that empties the stock: no gap, no overlap. It turns face up on landing, the mirror of the recycle, which lands face up and becomes a back (2026-10-11, Jay). Pinned by the recycle-undo cases in `klondikeScreenWin.test.tsx` and `pyramidScreenQuickPlay.test.tsx`.
- **Adoption:** a flight ADOPTS a ghost of exactly its cards that has been on screen (same React key, `prePainted`, no hold): a landed one (found in the synchronous `modelRef`, so a card landing and leaving in one task still adopts) or, in `showLive`, the snap-back the re-grab ends.
- **Pyramid/TriPeaks cells** are memoized components (`PyramidCell`/`TriPeaksCell`, exported), keyed by index and always `useTakeOffHold > DropZone > DraggableCard` (covered = disabled, `DropZone enabled={false}`, `registryId={null}`; Pyramid dimmed, TriPeaks `faceUp: false`), because a covered card used to be a plain PlayingCard that REMOUNTED blank the moment a move exposed it. Their props are primitives plus stable callbacks reading the screen's latest handlers through `cellHandlersRef` (refreshed in a layout effect): each cell has a GestureDetector, which re-sends its config to native on every render. A held cell is `pointerEvents: 'none'`, so a tap on the card it uncovered goes through. Covered cells get the shake token too (a covered card can't get a new one, so it must record the stale one as played, or it wiggles when uncovered). A drag-completed Pyramid pair keeps the drag ghost until the pair shows.
- **More landed holds:** Pyramid's pair ghosts (`PairFlightState.landed`, `retirePairCard`) and Spider's sequence ghosts (`SequenceFlightInstance.landed`, `retireSequenceFlight`; `settled` ignores landed groups) stay, shadowless, until `whenCardsSettled(ids, LANDED_GHOST_MAX_MS)` from a layout effect (their overlays render after `GameChrome`, so the board's faces have registered); all accounting still happens at the landing. Spider's New Game/restore retires every sequence flight and every drag-ghost flight (`retireSequenceFlights`/`retireMoveFlights`, like Pyramid's `hideAirborneFlights`).
- **Deals:** a face-up deal ghost with no lookahead runway (a deal's first cards, or a late launch) waits for its face before it takes off, never past the later of its departure and `GHOST_PAINT_MAX_MS` (`GhostSlot`'s `paintedFaceRef`, keyed by suit-rank since two decks' identical cards share one bitmap) - Pyramid A/B: 15 of 16 deals started with blank cards without it, 0 of 17 with it. Undoing a stock/round deal (Spider, Scorpion, Aces Up) marks each card in `handleUndo` right before the revert and hands each release to its `DealCardSpec.releasePending`; DealLayer gives it back exactly once, `TAKE_OFF_RELEASE_LAG_MS` (20) after that card's take-off (a JS timer racing the UI thread's opacity flip, the same race as Pyramid's `SWEEP_PILE_BLANK_LAG_MS`) or at once on a skip, landing, new `start()` or unmount; each screen releases a mark it never handed over on every bail-out, and New Game/restore drops them. An idle deal slot zeroes its card box's opacity as its hold ends (`releaseHold`): a relaunch's UI-thread resets could reach the screen after the commit that re-showed the slot, which drew it for one frame at its PREVIOUS landing spot - a card back flashing over a just-dealt card, 3 in 32 Klondike deals in recordings from before any of this work, 0 in 66 after.
- **Pixel results** (Metro-served dev build, Simulator, frame detectors): take-off blank frames 36 -> 0 in a 95s Klondike run; landing wiggles 36/36 -> 0; win hand-offs with blank Kings 8/8 -> 0 (see the win paragraph); Spider stock-deal undo: every card drawn in its column until its own ghost lifts off.
- **Tests:** `takeOffHold`, `slotTakeOffHold`, `dropReveal`, `pileViewHold`, `ghostStartOrder`, `dragGhostLayer` (its PlayingCard mock reports paint; `mockPaint.defer` holds it), `dealLayer`, `pyramidCells`/`tripeaksCells`, `spiderSequenceHold`, the three `*UndoDealHold` suites, `winCascadeHandoff`.
- **Not covered:** Klondike/Canfield draw-3 undo (StockWaste's draw-3 fan has no per-card hold: holding only the top card over the reverted fan would show a wrong mix), the legacy-save cascade returns (`runCascadeReturn`), and a face-up undo ghost landing on the face-down stock flips to a back ~80-190ms after it looks arrived (the deal layer reports a landing at the spring's nominal duration, plus dev-build JS latency) - pre-existing and cosmetic. **Fixed since:** in-flight ghosts used to hold a frame and then jump whenever a React commit landed mid-flight (a deal commits at every landing), because Reanimated paused its shadow-tree commits from React's commit until its mount; `IOS_SYNCHRONOUSLY_UPDATE_UI_PROPS` is on now, see "Animated props skip the shadow tree on iOS" below. **Metro-served builds load images slowly in bulk:** each new Image re-fetches its bitmap (no-cache) and iOS loads a few at a time, in mount order - mount the faces needed first first (see the win cascade).

**The win celebration is delayed until the winning card has flown in and landed (`useWinReveal`, `games/shared/useWinReveal.ts`) - a card must never teleport.** `won` flips in the SAME commit as the winning move, and the old code hung the celebration (foundations hiding their real cards so the bouncing win cascade can take over, fireworks, banner) straight on it. Hiding the foundations unregisters the very card the last flight is measuring to land on, so `CardRegistry.measure` returned null, the drain effect took its `!landed` branch (`flight.hide()`), and the winning card simply APPEARED while the fireworks started. Now `won` stays game state only (clock, recorded stat, cleared save, undo/hint gating) and a separate `winRevealed = useWinReveal(won, settled)` - true once `won` AND nothing is flying have held for 400ms (2.5s max wait so a win is never withheld; the optional third `maxWaitMs` param is for a screen whose winning move starts a longer sequence the reveal must wait for - Pyramid's sweep deal passes 4s) - gates only the visuals and the start of the win cascade (its `pendingWinCascadeRef` effect returns early unless `winRevealed`, deps `[state, winRevealed]`). Don't move a save/stat/clock concern onto `winRevealed`: the win must be recorded the instant it happens. **The reveal hands the real piles over to the bouncing win-cascade ghosts only once those ghosts are on screen with their faces painted, in every game** (Scorpion's pattern, `cascadeHandoffFor`/`cascadeHandoff` in each screen): `runWinCascade` measures the always-mounted, card-sized origin containers while the real cards are still drawn (Pyramid's is one chain from the discard, after its sweep deal - see the Pyramid paragraph), then calls `winCascade.startChains(chains, cardWidth, onShown)` with its hand-off setter as `onShown`. The cascade mounts invisible (React-owned opacity) with its clock stopped (`startMs` null, so nothing moves while invisible) until every face drawn while parked (each head; every `cardOrigins` card) has painted, capped at `WIN_CASCADE_PAINT_MAX_MS` (500 - nothing waits on it, the real cards stand in meanwhile), then shows, starts its clock and calls `onShown` exactly once (never after `clear()` or a newer `startChains`: `CascadeChains` is keyed by generation), and the piles blank in the next synchronous commit. The ghosts are mounted heads first (every head, then every second card, and so on; the old tail-to-head paint order is kept with `zIndex`): iOS requests each fresh Image's bitmap in mount order and a Metro-served build loads them a few at a time, so mounted tail-to-head the heads queued behind ~48 hidden followers and the cap handed over blank Kings (8 of 8 wins on the Simulator; 0 after). The real cards blank only when `winRevealed && handoff matches the current state object` (identity, so an undo or New Game un-blanks with nothing to reset; a pile whose chain didn't start, e.g. an unmeasurable origin, is never blanked). The older orders - blank on `winRevealed`, THEN await `measureView`, THEN `startChains` (every pile empty for ~140ms before the ghosts mounted), and blanking in the same commit the ghosts mounted (a blank head on every pile for a frame or two) - are both what this replaced. `WinCascadeLayer` also parks each chain's head ghost visible at its origin until its staggered entrance (followers peel off one at a time), and `ChainSpec.cardOrigins` (opt-in; Scorpion only) lets a fanned run keep every card's own spot into the cascade instead of collapsing onto the King's slot (`winCascadeMotion.ts`'s pure `cardXAtElapsed`/`cardYAtElapsed`/`fanOffsetScale`). Aces Up's origin is the drawn Ace's own `CardRegistry` rect (its pile container is `maxHeight` tall, not card-sized, and `ChainSpec.origin` must be card-sized for the rotation conversion) - worth a look under fake landscape. Spider refuses a second stock deal until the first has fully landed (`stockDealTokenRef`) and, while a completed run is still assembled awaiting its sweep (`sweepPendingRef`/`claimSweep`), ignores deals, undo, and taps/drags touching THAT column (`isColumnSweeping`) - the sweep cuts 13 cards off the end of it; the other columns play on (2026-10-06, Jay). A move made meanwhile records its undo snapshot through `sweptView` (the board with every waiting run already gone), or undoing it would bring back a run nothing sweeps again; a sweep takes the next free slot when it LEAVES (all 8 slots are measured), since runs completed close together can sweep in either order. FreeCell also falls back to flying to the foundation slot's own card-sized container (`foundationSlotRefs`) when a landing card can't be found in `CardRegistry`, instead of hiding the ghost, and refuses a move whose grabbed card is no longer at the head of the pile (`moveHeadCard` in `freecell/rules.ts`, checked in `applyMove`): `tryMove` only knows positions, so a drop arriving after an auto-play took that very card would otherwise move whatever now sits there while the ghost shows a different card. Klondike, Yukon, Canfield and Forty Thieves do the same since 2026-10-11 (`applyMove` checks `resolvePile(from, current)[length - count]` against the anchor card; a tap measures before it commits, so a tap racing an auto-play had the same hole). Pinned by Klondike's `a drop for a card that has since left is refused...`.

**Edge-of-game behavior (2026-10-06 audit).** Nothing is ever written on the felt: every notice is a toast (Spider's old inline "Can't deal" text, which also had the rule backwards, is gone). No board element may vanish and let a row collapse: an exhausted pile keeps a dashed `EmptySlot` in its place (Scorpion's dealt assisted stock, Spider's dealt stock - right-aligned where the last packet sat, and inert). `StockWaste`'s `canRecycle={false}` (Golf, TriPeaks, Forty Thieves) drops the recycle arrow and the tap on a stock that never turns over. A single card landing on a fanned waste (Klondike draw-3's last card, an undo back to the waste) freezes the whole fan until it lands (`hiddenPeak`/`singleArrival`), or the old top showed twice; Canfield's draw-1 waste no longer fans. Spider deals onto empty columns when the tableau holds fewer cards than columns (`canDealStock`; otherwise the stock could lock for good). **A dead board is a loss** (OFF since 2026-10-23, see "Records, and the loss dialog off" below; `useStuckLossCheck` + `LossBanner`, revealed after the last landing via `useWinReveal(stuck, settled)`, clock display stopped, loss recorded only when the player leaves the dead board: see Scoring) in every game: Aces Up, Golf, TriPeaks, Forty Thieves and Scorpion through their exact `isStuck` (no recycle), and since 2026-10-22 every game with a `progress` spec through the background look-ahead (see that section), which gave Klondike, Canfield, Pyramid, FreeCell, Yukon and Spider a loss at last. Not done: Canfield's recycle has no flight (Klondike's does), and none of this was checked on a device.

## Scoring (audited 2026-10-14)

- **Score = moves + seconds, lower is better** (`engine/score.ts`'s `computeScore`, the one formula: GameChrome's readout, WinBanner, the recorded stat).
- **A move** is one `session.registerMove()`: every board change a player could make, tap or drag, a stock draw or recycle, a Spider/Scorpion stock deal, an Aces Up round, a Pyramid pair or King, AND every auto-play (the cascade, the solved-board finish, the draws-aware sweep's draws, recycles and draw-and-plays, which count as two) - Jay, 2026-10-14: with auto-plays free, turning Auto-Move off raised the score; now it only saves taps. NOT counted: Undo presses (and an undo never takes a move back off the count), hints, Spider's run removal and Pyramid's win sweep (rules, not moves a player could make).
- **Variants** (`engine/variants.ts`): one game under one rule set, the unit stats are kept in (`StatsState.variants` is keyed by `VariantId`) and par is set for. A game's DEFAULT variant keeps the bare game id (`'klondike'` = Draw 1, `'spider'` = 1 Suit, `'canfield'` = Draw 3, `'scorpion'` = Assisted...), so stats from before variants carry over into it with no migration; the rest are `<game>-<rule>` (`'klondike-draw3'`, `'spider-4suit'`...). Each screen names its deal's variant from the rule it was DEALT with (its own state, e.g. `klondikeVariant(klondikeDraw)`), not the live setting, and passes it to the win/loss hooks and every `record*` call; the hooks derive the game (`gameOfVariant`) for the save. Per-game figures ("Klondike Solved", Games Solved, totals) sum over a game's variants (`getGameWins`/`getGamesWon`); streaks are per variant. The Stats screen shows each game's default variant always and the others once played.
- **Expert par** (`engine/par.ts`, `parFor(variant)` = `{ moves, seconds, score }`; plain "par" until 2026-10-17, see Personal par below): moves = the median winning move count of a simulated Hint-following player (a little randomness, up to 10 tries per deal, 300 deals per variant, on the real `rules.ts`; 2026-10-14), seconds = the moves a player decides (all but foundation plays in the five auto-play games) x `SECONDS_PER_DECISION` (3, the one time knob), score = moves + seconds. ESTIMATED, not measured: Spider 2/4 suit and both Forty Thieves rules (the simulated player never won), and Golf No Wrap / Aces Up rest on 9 and 2 wins. **Re-measure with `npm run par`** (`scripts/simulate-par.mjs`, ~40s, deterministic; `--only <prefix>`, `--deals`, `--tries`) after any change to a game's rules or to Hint: it bundles the real rules with esbuild (with `@tastic/cards` aliased to its plain `src/types.ts`) and prints a PAR_INPUTS block to paste, commenting out variants with under 20 wins. A new variant needs a controller in its `CONTROLLERS` map or it stops. WinBanner shows the player's par (below) under each number, plus "Score N% under your par" ("expert par" before a first win): the percentage is the score's alone, with no "Lower is better" since par already says it, and no period (Jay 2026-10-17). **The trophy and "You Won!" take the medal the score earned, in metal** (`playerParMedal` in `engine/par.ts`, `games/shared/MedalHeader.tsx`, Jay 2026-10-17), against the player's par; against expert par (`parMedal`, before a first win) bronze at par or over, silver for any amount under, gold from 35% under (`GOLD_MEDAL_PERCENT_UNDER_PAR`, the Low Score achievement's gold line), rounded like the "Score N% under par" line. Each is a gentle top-to-bottom metal gradient (`MEDAL_METALS`: a touch lighter at the top, a touch deeper at the bottom, no hard step) over a 2pt stamped drop shadow, the same in both modes: flat colors (the tier colors mixed with black) read as brown, dull silver and mustard gold in light mode, and he asked for a sheen. A polished-chrome version with a pale highlight and a hard horizon just below the middle was tried first and dropped: it read as old-school WordArt and cut the trophy into two bands. `MedalHeader` redraws hud's `DialogHeader` layout (its 64/32pt icon, gaps and title variants) because `DialogHeader` takes one flat color. Native masks a gradient View (`experimental_backgroundImage`) with each glyph through `@react-native-masked-view/masked-view`, the pair auto-paper's `FadingScrollView` uses; the web clips a CSS gradient to the letters (`backgroundClip: 'text'`), since the web MaskedView only draws the mask. Seen in headless Chrome on the web build, all three medals in light and dark: readable, silver the palest on the light frosted card. **Not seen on a phone**: the native masked path is unverified. Not the Low Score achievement's own tiers (bronze at par, silver 20% under), which are lifetime milestones. Expert par is also in the Rules sheet's Scoring paragraph (GameChrome works it out from its `variant` prop; Home's sheet has none) and on the Stats screen as each rule set's Expert Par row, with each best compared to it in a word ("320 · 7% under", see Look-ahead hints, stats). The speed/fewest_moves/low_score achievements (same ids, so unlocked tiers stay) are now best/par ratios across every variant (`getBestParRatio`): at par / 20% / 40% under on time, at par / 5% / 10% on moves, at par / 20% / 35% on score; the Overall section shows one "Best vs Par" (expert par) instead of the raw cross-game bests, which only ever named the quickest game.
- **Personal par** (Jay, 2026-10-17: expert par was out of reach, silver rare and gold never, and he wanted it to follow each player and "still feel rewarding without being easy"): a win's medal is measured against the player's OWN last `RECENT_WINS_KEPT` (20) wins in that variant. `playerParFor(variant, recent)` gives `{ kind, moves, seconds, score, goldScore }`: each number its own median (so they needn't add up), `goldScore` the best fifth's last score (`floor(n/5)`-th best: the 4th of 20, so about 1 win in 5; null under five wins, so gold needs five wins on file), `kind: 'expert'` (= `parFor`) with no wins yet. `playerParMedal`: bronze at or over (rounded, so "at your par" is bronze), silver under, gold at or under `goldScore`, never without silver. The bar follows the player both ways, so a steady player gets silver or better about half the time and gold about 1 in 5 (pinned by a simulation in `winBanner.test.tsx`). **Stored** in `redux/recentWinsSlice.ts` (persisted in root): by player (profile id or `GUEST_PLAYER_ID`), then variant, oldest first, `{ score, moves, seconds }`. `stores/gameStats.tsx`'s `recordDealWon` takes the par from the store BEFORE adding the win (`lastWin: { variant, par }` in the context) and WinBanner (now given `variant`, not `par`) judges the shown score against `lastWin.par` (expert par if it names another variant), so a win reached again after an Undo, which is recorded once, still gets its own score's medal. A player's reset and a deleted profile drop theirs (`stores/playerData.ts`), Reset Stats on Everyone everyone's. Stats keep only a best per variant, so nothing from before fills it: every player started on expert par. The Stats screen shows "Your Par" per variant for one player (the one picked, Guest included, or guest on a phone with no profiles; not Everyone). The achievements stay on expert par. Pinned by `winBanner.test.tsx` (the card, `playerParFor`, the medal rates) and `stores/playerData.test.tsx` (measured before joining, per player, the cap, resets). Seen on the web build in headless Chrome (402x874): with no wins a seeded Klondike win showed expert par and silver; with ten wins of 330-420 on file a 322 showed gold, "Par 375" and "Score 14% under your par", and joined them (Stats: Your Par 370). Not seen on a device.
- **The clock** (`useGameSession`) starts with the first move, stops at the winning commit (Spider: the winning move, before its last run sweeps), and PAUSES while anything holds it (`setPaused(reason, paused)`, a set of reasons): `'app'` whenever the app is not `active` (a call, the app switcher, the home screen) and, on the web, while the window is out of focus; `'idle'` from the app's return (and from a resumed save with moves) until the next move (2026-10-22, see that section); and `'dialog'` while GameChrome's Rules or Settings is open (Jay, 2026-10-14; the New Game confirm doesn't pause). A first move or a resume made while held starts held. It used to keep running in the background, so time away counted only when the OS kept the app alive. Elapsed is banked in ms, so pauses don't drop part-seconds. Time on Home doesn't count either: the game screen unmounts and its save holds the elapsed time it was left at.
- **A save holds the elapsed time from when it was MADE**, so `useSaveInProgressBackstop` saves again (through the screen's own guarded `save`) on unmount and when the app leaves the foreground, as well as every 10s. Before, leaving a game handed back up to 10s of clock every trip.
- **Win recording reads `session.getMoves()`/`getElapsed()`**, never a render's `session.moves`: an auto-played winning card commits after a measurement await, and a move the player made during it is only in the live count (`checkWin(next)` takes no move count any more).
- **Losses:** `record.losses` (labelled "Lost" on the Stats screen, "Losses" in its Overall well) is both a New Game over a deal in progress and a dead board (`useStuckLossCheck`, off since 2026-10-23: only the first counts for now). Both use `recordDealAbandoned`. **A dead board's loss is recorded when the player LEAVES it** (Jay, 2026-10-14): New Game (each screen's `handleNewGame`, which the LossBanner's New Game also calls) or leaving the screen (`useStuckLossCheck`'s unmount, which also clears the save). Undo out of it cancels it. It used to be recorded on arrival, which counted one deal twice after an Undo (a loss plus a win, or two losses). The dead board is never saved (the saves skip `stuck`), so a killed app resumes one move before it, the same as an Undo.
- Pinned by `components/gameSession.test.tsx`, `components/stuckLossCheck.test.tsx`, `gameChromeHowToPlay.test.tsx` (the dialog hold), `statsEngine.test.ts` (variants, par ratios), `games/shared/winBanner.test.tsx`, and the five auto-play games' screen tests (move counts).

## Hints (2026-10-14)

- **No more ping-pong.** Every game's `scoreHintMove` measures a move's runs the same way before and after it (`a` = the run the moving group's base card continued at its source, `b` = the run it lands on, net = max(count + b, a) - max(count + a, b)), so a move and its exact reverse score as opposites and a relocation that gains nothing scores 0; the size tiebreak only applies to a score already above 0. Aces Up gained a scorer (it had none, so Hint showed a lone card to the empty pile and back). Scorpion's already-complete K-A column moved whole into an empty column no longer earns the completion bonus. Klondike and Forty Thieves also score a waste play onto a column (it scored 0, so Hint drew instead); Forty Thieves' waste card into an empty column still scores 0. On top of that, `findHint` never suggests a SHUFFLE (`isShuffle`: scores 0 or less and its exact reverse is legal right after), which is what kept the ping-pong going on boards with nothing to draw (Yukon, FreeCell, Spider with an empty column, Scorpion's dealt stock); if that leaves nothing, Hint draws or reports none ("No helpful moves right now."). A followed-Hint simulation over 200 deals per game: Canfield, Aces Up and FreeCell 0 reversals; Yukon 20, Spider 1-suit 39, Spider 4-suit 8, Scorpion 8-9 (from thousands each), all after a move that turned a card over, which can't really be undone. Each game's `rules.test.ts` pins its case, `engine.test.ts` the shuffle filter. **Known, not fixed:** Spider's Hint never suggests filling an empty column so the stock can be dealt again; Aces Up's `isStuck` never declares a loss while a lone card can still move to an empty pile, though that move can never lead to a discard.
- **A run gets ONE outline, not a ring per card** (Jay, 2026-10-14), for a hint and a selection alike (since 2026-11-07 a hint is a glow under the run instead, see "The hint glow" at the end; a selected run keeps this ring): `PileView` draws `RunOutline` (PlayingCard.tsx, the same `hintRing`/`selectedRing` style, so it looks like one card's ring stretched over the run) over the run's own box from `columnTops` (head's top to the bottom card's bottom, zIndex above every card, `layout={FAN_GLIDE}` so it glides with the column), and the run's cards draw no ring of their own. Per-card rings drew a ladder of lines across a run, and each hint glow spilled over the face of the card above. A single card keeps its own ring; a selection wins over a hint (as on one card); no outline while any card of the run is hidden. No screen passes a `selectedCount` above 1 today, so only hints show it. Pinned by `pileViewRunOutline.test.tsx`; the outline was NOT looked at on the simulator (the simulator tool's taps arrive late and kept landing on the wrong screen).
- **The hint marks only what to move** (`hintCountFor` marks the source run, never the destination; Pyramid marks both cards of a pair, `pairHinted`, since both leave). It was a ring with a soft glow that breathed (`startHintPulse`, 1100ms to 0.4 and back); since 2026-11-07 it is the glow alone, under the cards (see "The hint glow" at the end). **Its color is the app theme's tertiary, lifted** (Jay, 2026-10-14: a theme color, muted like everything else, and not the card back's own hue, or a ringed card in a stack blends into the backs beside it): `theme.ts` fills TableColors' `hintGreen` slot (the name is `@tastic/cards`' type; nothing in that package draws it) with `getTonalColor(getTriadicPalette(cardBack, THEME_HARMONY).tertiary, HINT_RING_TONE)`. `ProfileThemeSync` seeds the Paper theme with that same muted card back, so this IS the theme's tertiary, only lighter (`HINT_RING_TONE` 0.62 in `constants/theme.ts`, so it reads on the dark felt by lightness, not hue alone). The card back is the theme's primary; the felt stays the card back's 180-degree opposite (Jay kept it over a triad felt), so the ring sits 120 degrees from the back and 60 from the felt. The guide's `GuideHint` uses the same color. Pinned by `hintRingColor.test.tsx` (8 profile colors, light and dark).
- **The glide** (Jay: "a ghost animation of one card going to another"): each press of the Hint button shows a see-through copy (0.7 opacity) of the hinted card or run lifting off, gliding onto where it would land (`HINT_GLIDE_MS` = `scaledMs(300)`) and fading there; the real cards never move. `components/hinting/HintGlideLayer.tsx` (`HintGlideProvider`, innermost in `withGameProviders`, so it paints over the board and under every real flight) draws it; `games/shared/useHintGlide.ts` measures the anchor card (CardRegistry) and the landing spot: the destination's top card (one fan step below it in a column, at the step the column uses now, `fanTops.ts`), an empty pile's drop zone, or a card-sized container for a spot that isn't one (Aces Up's and Pyramid's discard, `measureSlot`). It claims nothing, hides nothing and registers nothing, so no flight or undo waits on it; a board change drops it (on screen or still measuring). A drawStock hint has no glide (the stock glows), and neither has an Auto Hint (2026-10-15, Jay: the button shows the ghost and the outline, Auto Hint just the outline, a glow since 2026-11-07; each screen's `handleHint` skips `playHintGlide` on `request.auto`). Pinned by `hintGlide.test.tsx` and `klondikeScreenWin.test.tsx`'s `hint` describe. Seen working on the web build (mounted on the ringed card, retired on time); the motion itself was NOT watched (the browser pane was hidden, so no frames were drawn), and nothing was checked on a device.
- **Holding Hint steps through the hints** (2026-10-16, Jay): a hint at the long press (400ms, `HOLD_LONG_PRESS_MS`, Undo's too), then the next every `HINT_HOLD_REPEAT_MS` (= `HINT_GLIDE_LANDED_MS`, 830ms: the last glide has landed and had its beat, and the next replaces it as it would fade), the same cycle pressing Hint again steps through (findHint's `skip`, wrapping round). `components/hintHold.ts`'s `useHintHold`, wired in `GameChrome`. It stops by itself when there is nothing new to show: findHint's move result carries `choices` (how many moves it cycles through), and every screen's `handleHint` returns true only for a move with `choices > 1`, so a stock draw, a lone move or the no-moves toast shows once. Each hint of a hold clicks (`useCardSoundEffects`' card sound + selection haptic), as each tap's press does, and counts toward Hints Used, as a tap does. **A footer button's long press plays nothing of its own** (`FooterPressable`: feedback-press with only the touch-down click wired): its default plays the 'notification' feedback on a long press, which this app maps to the invalid-move sound, so holding Undo used to play that at 400ms along with the first undo's card sound. Pinned by `hintHold.test.tsx` and `gameChromeHintHold.test.tsx`. Not seen on a device.
- **Auto Hint** (`Settings.autoHintSeconds`: 0 = Off, 15, 30; default Off; a Settings row that opens its three choices in place, `RuleRows`, since 2026-11-08; it stepped through them on each tap before): `components/autoHint.ts`'s `useAutoHint`, run by `GameChrome`, asks for a hint once the board (`autoHintKey`, each screen's state object) has sat unchanged that long. It counts exactly like a pressed hint (Hints Used, Wins Without Hint: Jay's call). Held back, with the wait starting over, while a hint is already showing (`hintShowing`), the deal is won or dead (`hintDisabled`), a dialog is open, the screen isn't focused or the app is in the background. At most once per board; it only rings the cards (no glide, see above), and with nothing to suggest it shows no toast (`HintRequest.auto`). Pinned by `autoHint.test.tsx`; seen firing at 15s on the web build.

## Look-ahead hints, a loss dialog everywhere, stats, touch and crowding (2026-10-22)

A coworker of Jay's sent six notes. Each is below. None of it was checked on a device or simulator.

- **Hint looks ahead** ("Hint is sometimes not helpful ... just moving a card from one stack to the other and not unearthing anything, then telling you that there are no moves"). `engine/progressSearch.ts`, driven by a `progress` spec on each controller (Klondike, Spider, FreeCell, Yukon, Canfield, Forty Thieves, Scorpion, Pyramid, Aces Up; Golf and TriPeaks have none, since every legal move there is progress). A spec says what progress is (counts that only go up as the deal is won: cards turned over, cards on foundations, runs completed, cards removed), every move to try (a direct generator per game: legalMoves narrows each run to the one column tap-to-move picks), a position key (columns in any order, two cards of a kind alike), whether a draw is a step of the search (`drawIsStep`: a stock that comes round again, Klondike, Canfield, Pyramid), and `rescueMoves` (a card back down off a foundation: the dead check tries them, Hint doesn't, because from the board after one the card going straight back up is "progress" and the two hints alternate). `ProgressSearch` is breadth-first, expands each position once and never looks past progress, so a hint leans on no face-down card beyond "a card turns over". `findHint` blends: the scored hint stands while it offers a productive move (a foundation play, or a positive `scoreHintMove`); where it would offer a 0-score move, a draw or nothing, the look-ahead's first step of the shortest way to progress, and the boards that step leads to are remembered (`planBoards`, 32) so later hints finish the plan instead of the scored hint undoing it (2-suit Spider went round in circles without that). A board the look-ahead finds no progress from at all gets a deal if a stock dealt for good still has cards, a rescue move if that is the only way, else "none". Tried and dropped: pure shortest-path hints (Klondike Draw 3 fell from 20 to 7 wins of 60: they drew past waste cards the scored hint plays), and a "headway" fallback (longer runs, empty columns: not monotone, so hints circled). A simulated Hint-following player, 40 deals each, old then new wins: Klondike Draw 1 11/19, Draw 3 11/13, FreeCell 6/13, Yukon 17/24, Canfield 13/17, Canfield Draw 1 22/28, Scorpion 11/14, Spider 1 suit 8/10; Pyramid 18/18, and 2/4-suit Spider, Forty Thieves and Aces Up 0 either way. In every game most lost deals now end on a board recognised as dead instead of looping. The harness was a scratchpad copy of `scripts/simulate-par.mjs`'s bundling.
- **Par was measured with the old Hint** (`engine/par.ts`) and not re-measured: `npm run par` now runs a look-ahead on every unproductive hint and would take hours at 300 deals x 10 tries. Re-measuring moves the expert pars (more wins, other move counts), and with them every medal threshold measured against expert par.
- **Costs.** ~2-15us a position in Node (Klondike ~12, Spider with empty columns ~70), several times that in Hermes (not measured). A Hint press searches for itself only when no finished look is cached: `HINT_SEARCH_BUDGET` 1500 positions, `HINT_SEARCH_MAX_MS` 80. `games/shared/useBoardLookahead.ts` looks ahead after every board change in the background: 150ms after the change, 4ms slices a frame apart, up to `LOOKAHEAD_BUDGET` 40000 positions or `LOOKAHEAD_MAX_MS` 1000 of its own time, and never while a stock dealt for good still has cards (such a board can't be dead, and 2/4-suit Spider's look is the costliest, ~85ms a board in Node). Outcomes are cached by board object and rules (`ProgressSpec.id`) in engine/hint.ts, so Hint, Auto Hint and the background share one.
- **Every game has a loss dialog** ("There is no game over dialog shown on a loss condition"). Switched off the next day, see "Records, and the loss dialog off" below. `useStuckLossCheck` is the game's own `isStuck` OR the look-ahead's dead verdict: every reachable position expanded with no progress, confirmed by a second pass with the rescue moves, and no stock left that isn't part of the search. Klondike, Spider, FreeCell, Yukon, Canfield and Pyramid are wired like the other five (the save skips `stuck`, the clock stops, Hint is disabled, `LossBanner` shows after the last landing). A deal dead from the start and left without a move records nothing (the `session` argument). Seen on the web build: a dead Klondike board (the simulator's deal 15 after 84 hinted moves: no empty column, every Ace buried, no black Jack reachable) seeded as a save shows No Moves Left within 1.5s. Screen tests whose boards leave out most of the deck stub `useBoardLookahead` (they'd rightly be dead).
- **The clock waits for the next move** ("The timer needs to stop when the app is backgrounded or closed or otherwise made inactive"). It already paused while the app was in the background (2026-10-14). What was left: it ran again the moment the app came back, a resumed save's clock ran from the instant the screen opened, and on the web a visible tab whose window had lost focus kept it running. Now an `'idle'` hold goes on when the app comes back and when `restore` resumes a deal with moves, and `registerMove` lifts it; the web also listens to window blur and focus. Pinned by gameSession.test.tsx.
- **More stats.** VariantStats gained `totalSeconds` and `totalMoves` (every deal that ended; a lost one's come from `recordDealAbandoned`'s new `played` argument, given by every screen's New Game and the dead-board unmount), `winsCounted`/`winSeconds`/`winMoves`/`winScore` (the average win) and `undosUsed` (GameChrome's `onUndoUsed`, once an undo resolves true: a tap, or each repeat of a hold; the loss dialog's Undo isn't counted). All start at 0 on older stats (`VARIANT_COUNTERS`, backfilled in statsValidation.ts). The Stats screen adds Win Rate, Average Score/Time/Moves, Time Played, Hints Used, Undos Used and Wins Without Hints to each variant, and Most Played, Time Played, Moves Made, Undos Used and Wins Without Hints overall. Par was already there: each best shows "· expert par N", and Your Par has its own row. Also (the same day, Jay): Cards Dealt overall (deals that ended x 52, or 104 for Spider and Forty Thieves); `ProfileStats.winStreak`, a win streak across every game ("Win Streak, All Games"; OVERALL's old "Best Streak", the best single-variant streak, is now "Best Game Streak"); and `ProfileStats.days`, every day a deal ended (local YYYY-MM-DD: deals, wins, seconds, deals per game), read by Most Deals in a Day and kept so a calendar of play has history when there is one. ACTIVITY moved up under OVERALL. **@rific/heatmap** (Jay wants it on the Stats screen eventually) is plain JS on react-native-svg, which the native build already has, so it should ship in an OTA: check the fingerprint is unchanged when adding it. Its `DataPoint` takes the same date string as `days`' keys. Added the same day (Jay installed 0.3.4 and checked the fingerprint was unchanged): `components/PlayCalendar.tsx`, the Stats screen's first well (CALENDAR, above OVERALL: a year at a glance, and its tap tooltip lands over the label instead of covering a stat). A day is shaded by deals that ended on it, in even steps from a pale tint of the theme's primary to full; a day with no deals is the felt softened over the well (`EMPTY_DAY_FELT` 0.7; Jay asked for the felt or a disabled variant of it, and 0.45 all but vanished in dark mode); a tap on a day shows its deals and wins on the well's title line, right of the CALENDAR label ("Sep 3 · 5 deals, 1 won"; the year only for last year's days; `dayLabel` names the past week Today, Yesterday, then the weekday, since a weekday a week back would read as this week's), and with no day tapped the line shows today ("Today · No deals"). It replaced the package's popover (Jay, the same day: it ignored the theme and Blur, poked out above the well and would have covered days near the one tapped). `PlayCalendar` owns the picked day (the calendar's `selectedDate`/`onSelectionChange`, 0.6.0+) and passes a `renderTooltip` that draws nothing (until 2026-11-08 a `SelectedDayReporter` stood in as the tooltip, from before the package let a host own the selection). The line reads the days as they are now, so the pickers update a day that stays picked, and it sits absolutely over StatSection's label row (`bottom: '100%'` plus StatSection's 8pt gap, `left: DAY_LINE_LEFT` 105 to clear the label, ellipsized past that). Seen on the web build at desktop size and 375 wide; no load ripple under Reduce Motion. **It needs @rific/heatmap 0.3.5 or later** (released the same day): 0.3.4 called `InteractionManager`, which React Native 0.88 removed (an invariant in dev, undefined, so a crash, in a release build; react-native-web still exports it, so the web build hid it), and its grid height and labels ignored each cell's padding (Saturday's row cut off). Metro reads the package's `src` on iOS/Android (its `react-native` export condition) but its `dist` on the web, so check a heatmap change on a phone as well. Not yet seen on a device.
- **Stats screen pickers and par** (the same day, Jay). Two pills at the top (the shell's stats screen since 2026-11-02, built from this app's `components/StatsFilters.tsx`): whose stats (Everyone, Guest or one player, only once a profile exists, as on every Tastic game's stats screen; see the Everyone and Guest bullet below) and which game (All Games or one). Both are @tastic/profile's `OptionPicker` on the theme's surface, the pill Home's player button is; the profile picker used to be `ProfilePicker`'s plain name trigger, which a game pill beside it wouldn't have matched (ProfilePicker draws a pill only with a color section). Like Home's button they sit in a raised wrapper (zIndex 10) over a press-away overlay (zIndex 5): without it each later card painted over the open menu. A game narrows the calendar, the totals (under the game's name, without Games Solved, Most Played and Least Played), Activity (days and day streaks from the day log, which now keeps each game's own played/won/seconds: `DayRecord.games` is a `DayTally` per game; no cross-game win streak) and the rule-set sections (all of that game's rules) through `statsEngine.getGameView`. Achievements stay unfiltered (milestones across games). Each rule set shows **Expert Par** in full (score, time, moves), **Your Par** with how it compares ("370 · 7% over"), and each best as "320 · 7% under". Since the same day the comparisons don't name the par (Jay: "7% under expert par" on every row was wordy): `engine/par.ts`' `vsParShort` gives "7% under", "even" (golf's word for at par) or "7% over", and the Expert Par row right above says which par; Overall's row, with no par row near it, is labelled "Best vs Par". The win card keeps `describeVsPar`'s sentence ("Score 14% under your par"). Seen in the browser pane (web, desktop size): the menu over the cards, Spider narrowing everything. `GAME_TITLES` now lives once, in statsTypes.ts.
- **Stats and Achievements tabs, commas and Least Played** (the same day, Jay; a Records tab joined them on 2026-10-23, see below). The screen opens on a Stats | Achievements switch under its header (Paper `SegmentedButtons` on the theme's surface, the shell's stats screen; Records is the pack's `statsTabs`); the header's title follows the tab (`BaseStatsScreen`'s `title`). The player picker is on both tabs and the game picker only on Stats (the row is left out when it would be empty: the Achievements tab with no profile). Reset sits under both. Every count the app shows goes through `utils/commaString.ts` (copied from Hangman; 10,088 not 10088): the Stats screen, the scoreboard (GameChrome), WinBanner, the Rules sheet's par, the achievement descriptions and the calendar's tooltip. Times, dates and percentages don't. **Least Played** (`statsEngine.getLeastPlayedGame`) sits under Most Played on All Games only while one game has strictly fewer deals than every other (an unplayed game counts as 0, so it stays hidden until every game but one has been played), never a tie. Pinned by `solitaireStats.test.tsx` (the commas), the shell's own tests (the tabs), `statsEngine.test.ts` and `utils/commaString.test.ts`. Seen in the browser pane (web, desktop size): both tabs, the title switching, the game pill hidden on Achievements, the commas.
- **Everyone and Guest** (the same day, Jay asked whether guest showed up in All Profiles). The player picker reads Everyone, Guest, then each profile. Everyone was "All Profiles": it is the phone's totals, which always counted guest's play, so the old name undersold it, and it is 30pt shorter. Guest's play used to go only into those totals; since this change it also goes into a bucket of its own, `StatsState.profiles[GUEST_PLAYER_ID]` (`gameStats.tsx`'s `player`, the profile playing or guest, for every deal, hint and undo), with its own achievements (`guest:` unlock keys) like a new profile's, so guest's history starts that day: the totals can't be split back out (bests, streaks and days aren't sums). Saves, settings, color picks and Your Par were already guest's own. Guest is offered only once a profile exists (with none, Everyone is just guest), and the screen drops back to Everyone if the last profile goes. Reset on Guest is `resetPlayer(GUEST_PLAYER_ID)` ("Reset Guest's Stats"). The other Tastic games still say All Profiles. **The pills still wrap on most phones:** each has 84pt of fixed parts (chip, chevron, padding, all in @tastic/profile's `PickerMenu`), so Everyone | All Games needs a 363.5pt row, 404pt-wide phones and up with the stats screen's 20pt margins (the 402pt iPhone 17 and Pro miss by 1.5pt), and a long game name or profile name wraps on any phone. Letting a pill shrink and cut its name with an ellipsis is a @tastic/profile change, which is native, so it needs a new build. Pinned by the shell's own tests, `solitaireStats.test.tsx` and `stores/playerData.test.tsx`. Seen in the browser pane: the picker's four rows and Guest's empty stats.
- **A press anywhere closes the pickers' menu** (the same day, Jay: they "don't press away in some areas"). The pickers' `PressAwayOverlay` fills only the scrolling content, so a press on the header (the title, the blank beside it) or, in the wide layout, on the felt either side of the 640pt column left a menu open (mapped with `elementFromPoint` on the web build; Home's popover had no such gap). `StatsPressAway` (the shell's since 2026-11-02, every pack's) wraps the whole screen (outside the turned frame, so the pickers' `usePopoverHost` sits above it) and, only while a menu is open, takes a press that nothing under the finger claimed (`onStartShouldSetResponder`) and closes it on release; the back button and the menu's rows claim their own presses first. The picker row claims its own blank parts while open, so the menu's padding doesn't close it. Seen on the web build at 1280x800 (the margin, the header and the title close it; the menu's padding doesn't; a row still picks). Not checked on a device. Pinned by the shell's own tests.
- **The Stats screen opens on whoever is playing, on the Stats tab** (the same day, Jay). The player picker starts on the active profile, or Guest when guest is playing (`selectedProfileId`'s initial value); with no profile there is no picker and it is Everyone. Someone opening it from Home wants to see how they are doing, and Solitaire has one player at a time; the other Tastic games open on the combined view because two seats leave no one player to pick. Stats stays the first tab and the trophy stays the button: stats change every game, achievements every few weeks. Seen on the web build with guest playing (opens on Guest); a profile playing is pinned by the shell's own tests (`guestStats`).
- **Touch** (Spider: "sometimes I can't grab the right card from the stack, and sometimes it autodrops to the commit pile when I want to reserve and hold"). A press held 400ms (`HOLD_CANCELS_TAP_MS`, components/dragdrop/holdGuard.ts) and let go without starting a drag no longer plays the card (a Pressable fires onPress on release however long the finger was down): PileView's cards and DraggableCard, through PlayingCard's new `onPressIn`. `PileDragConfig.grabRunBelow` (Spider only): a touch on a card that can start neither a drag nor a tap (face-down, or above a break in the run) acts on the longest run at the top of the column, its ghost starting on the run's head (`grabShiftX/Y`: the head's offset turned with the frame, written with the shared values' `.set()`, which the React Compiler lint accepts where `.value +=` isn't). A release within `DROP_SLOP` (12pt) of a pile but on none drops on the nearest (DropZoneRegistry.hitTest), for the gaps between narrow columns. Pinned by pileViewGrab.test.tsx. "Reserve and hold" was read as holding a card in place; if it meant select-then-place (tap to pick up, tap where it goes), that isn't built.
- **Crowding** ("Klondike 3 card draw is crowded"; every game audited at 375x667, 390x844, 430x932, 844x390 and 1024x768 on the web build). The draw-3 fan keeps 4pt clear of the last foundation (`WASTE_FAN_MIN_CLEARANCE` through `wasteFanStepFor`'s `gap`: a 0.500W step on phones, a '10' losing under half a point), measured 4.0pt at all three phone sizes. Covered face-up ranks stay whole: `MIN_FACE_UP_FAN_SCALE` 0.95 (one 0.8 floor cut a covered Q to an O and a J to an I in FreeCell and Yukon held sideways, and in Aces Up after a few rounds); face-down steps give way first, to `MIN_FACE_DOWN_FAN_SCALE` 0.55. Columns scroll a little sooner. Aces Up's piles keep 12pt apart (`PILE_MIN_GAP`; 2pt on a 390pt phone). Scorpion's wide-layout packet sits `WIDE_PACKET_SEPARATION` (10pt) more than a gap from column 7 (2pt, it read as an eighth column). Not done: a covered draw-3 card shows its rank but not its suit pip (the pip starts at 0.65W; only fanning two cards, or a rank-over-suit corner index in @tastic/cards, would show it), and the board-wide 2pt gap (`CARD_GAP_MIN`).

## Records, and the loss dialog off (2026-10-23)

- **The loss dialog is off, in every game** (Jay: it "renders too soon, doesn't let me puzzle at it at all"; he'll revisit it). One switch, `DEAD_BOARD_LOSS` in `constants/deadBoardLoss.ts`, makes `useStuckLossCheck`'s `stuck` always false, so a dead board plays on like any other: no No Moves Left, the clock runs, Hint stays on (it says it has nothing), the board is saved, nothing is recorded on leaving it, and New Game records the loss as a deal walked away from. The exact no-moves games (Aces Up, Golf, TriPeaks, Forty Thieves, Scorpion) lost theirs too, which they had since 2026-10-06. The background look-ahead still runs, since Hint reads what it finds. The tests of the loss path (`stuckLossCheck`, `boardLookahead`, Golf's dead-board describe) mock the switch on; `stuckLossCheck.test.tsx` and Golf's first dead-board case pin that it is off.
- **Records** (a stats-loving friend of Jay's: "wins losses high score fastest time. avg is good but high scores are peak", "maybe game rank, 3rd fastest ever"). `engine/topWins.ts` keeps each player's 10 best wins per variant on two boards, lowest score and fastest (`TopWin`: score, moves, seconds, `at`), plus `seen`, how many of their wins the boards were offered; `redux/topWinsSlice.ts` holds them by player (profile id or `GUEST_PLAYER_ID`), then variant, persisted in root, written by `gameStats.tsx`'s `recordDealWon`, dropped with a player's reset or deletion (`playerData.ts`) and by Reset Stats on Everyone. **A store from before has no `topWins`, so its first rehydrate builds each player's boards from their recent wins** (`recentWinsSlice`, the latest 20 per variant since 2026-10-17, the only older wins with all three numbers; undated). It is a REHYDRATE matcher in the slice: redux-persist's default merge keeps a reducer's changed value over a missing stored key, and a store that has boards rehydrates them untouched (pinned through real redux-persist in `topWins.test.ts`).
- **Ranks are only claimed when known** (`rankWin`). A player's boards hold every win of theirs in a variant when `seen` covers their stats' wins there; then a win's place is exact. Otherwise (wins before 2026-10-17, or more than 20 since) nothing past the boards is known, so only a win better than every one, the stats' best included, is ranked: the best ever. Nothing for a first win, past 10th, or for the worst win so far (a 3rd fastest of three is just the slowest). So Jay's family, who played before the boards existed, mostly see "Fastest ever" and no lower places; a new player sees them all. Guest's stats bucket only started 2026-10-22, so a guest who won before 2026-10-17 can be told a place an unkept older win beat (not fixable: those wins are only in the Everyone totals).
- **The win card** says it on a line per board under the numbers ("2nd best score ever", "3rd fastest ever", "Tied for fastest ever"), judged from the numbers it shows against the player's wins as they stood before the win (`lastWin.before`, taken with the par), so a win reached again after an Undo is ranked by its own numbers. Joined on one line the two wrapped on a 375pt phone, leaving "ever" alone.
- **The Records tab** (Stats | Records | Achievements; `components/RecordsList.tsx`): a Best Score | Fastest switch, then a well per rule set with a win (narrowed by the game picker) holding a table: place, score, time, moves, date; the board's own column in full contrast, the rest muted (the app font is bold throughout, so weight can't mark it). Equal wins share a place. Under Everyone every player's boards merge into one, the player's name taking the moves column (six columns don't fit a phone), ranked only when the boards together saw every win on the phone; with no profile there are no names. A board that can't be ranked shows its wins unnumbered with "Not ranked: some earlier wins weren't kept". **The three tabs fit a phone only as label-wide segments with no icons** (Paper pads each label 16pt a side, "Achievements" is ~109pt in the app font): 309pt of a 375pt phone's 335pt row, and fits a 360dp Android. With icons they needed ~387pt. On iOS and Android they were really equal thirds (cut off) until the shell's `flex: 0` fix (see "Segmented switches on iOS").
- **Stats tab:** Overall gained a Losses row under Wins (`getTotalLosses`), and each rule set's "Lowest Score" is now "Best Score", the word the Records switch and the win card use.
- Pinned by `topWins.test.ts` (boards, `rankWin`, the rank line, the slice and its first rehydrate), `winBanner.test.tsx`'s rank-line describe, `playerData.test.tsx`'s records describe and `solitaireStats.test.tsx`'s Records tab describe. Seen on the web build at 375 and 360 wide: a store from before building its boards on reload, Everyone with names and an unranked Spider board, a seeded Klondike win showing "2nd best score ever" and "2nd fastest ever" and landing at #2 with its date. Not seen on a device.

## Dialogs (2026-10-15)

Every dialog is one family (Jay: "I value consistency above all else"; it started with the win/loss/New Game cards reading as a different shade from the Settings sheet): the same untinted, blur-aware card, the same header and the same buttons. The pieces are `@tastic/hud`'s (hud's CLAUDE.md, "One family of dialogs"); NEEDS hud 0.18.0, `@tastic/profile` 0.13.0 and `@rific/auto-paper` 0.13.0, unpublished as of this note (yalc-linked here to build this: `.yalc/` and `yalc.lock` are temporary, never commit them).
- **Card:** hud's `OverlayCard`, now the Settings sheet's surface: no elevation (the old MD3 elevation-3 wash of the primary was the different shade), no border, the Dialog's 28pt corners, 24pt padding. The Settings sheet is exactly as wide as the cards (24pt from the frame's edges, capped at 360).
- **Header:** hud's `DialogHeader`, a 64pt icon over a large bold title in the theme's primary (the danger role on a destructive confirm). Settings `cog`, Rules `information-outline` (the button that opens it) with the game's name, How to Play `help-circle-outline` (the guide now says what it is above its first card, since it opens unprompted on a first launch), New Game `alert-circle-outline`, Win `trophy`, Loss `cards-outline` (in the primary now; it used to be the muted ink), Delete Profile `alert-outline`. Titles are Title Case ("Start a New Game?").
- **Compact:** when the space a dialog lays out in is under 528pt tall (a phone held sideways), every header switches to a 32pt icon beside a smaller title (`useCompactDialog`, which measures the `OverlayBackdrop`; the Settings sheet and the guide compute it from the frame). On a 402pt-tall window the guide's pages fit exactly (measured on web); a 375 or 360pt-wide phone held sideways scrolls a page a little.
- **Buttons:** the shared action row (`overlayActionStyles`); a one-button dialog ends in a single contained primary button. Settings and Rules both end in **Done** (Rules' was a 160pt "Got it"); a tap outside still closes either, and Android back closes Rules.
- **RulesDialog** is rebuilt on `OverlayBackdrop`/`OverlayCard` (it was a Paper `Modal`), with no `compact` prop of its own: its max height is the frame less its rotated safe-area edges and the 24pt gutters.
- **On the two sheets that scroll (Settings and Rules) the header scrolls away with the content** (Jay: so a long list gets the whole card once scrolled), and Done stays pinned below. The scroll area runs to the card's top edge (`RulesDialog`'s card has no top padding or gap; its `scrollContent` carries the 24pt inset, the 16pt header gap and 16pt below the last line). The guide's header stays fixed (its pages swipe sideways), and the cards that never scroll are unchanged. Seen on the web build in portrait and sideways. Profiles' Delete Profile confirm is hud's `ConfirmDialog` (`cardBg` is gone from `@tastic/profile`).
- **Settings rows:** every group under a section label in the same style (hud's APPEARANCE and VERSION, Solitaire's GAMEPLAY, CARDS and the per-game KLONDIKE etc., all `labelMedium`, letter-spaced), so a 24pt section gap always comes before a label (an unlabeled group left a bare gap after Blur). GAMEPLAY: Auto-Move to Foundation, Dim Unplayable Cards, Auto Hint, Reduce Motion; CARDS: Invert Dark Mode Colors, the face and back previews, the art credit. Toggle rows sit 4pt apart (hud's own toggle group; Solitaire's used to be 12). A row's state or meaning goes in a one-line `bodySmall` caption in `onSurfaceVariant` under its label: Auto Hint's choice is its caption ("Off", "After 15 seconds"; it used to sit on the right, the only row that did), and hud's Blur row says "Frost behind dialogs". Every color in the sheet is an auto-paper theme role (the art credit used Paper's default text color at 70% opacity; it is `onSurfaceVariant` now), except the card previews, which draw the real cards in the table colors, and their selection ring, the theme's `secondary` like the selected Appearance segment. Pinned by `settingsSections.test.tsx`.
- **Scroll cue:** on Settings and Rules the rows fade out over 24pt at each edge while there is more past it (above Done until the end, at the card's top edge once scrolled) (`@rific/auto-paper`'s `FadingScrollView`, 0.13.0+: a mask, so the card stays one surface; it lived in hud first and moved upstream so every app can use it). A frosted Done footer was tried first and rejected for its seam (hud's CLAUDE.md). On iOS/Android it needs `@react-native-masked-view/masked-view` handed to auto-paper's Provider: the shell's `Theme` passes `maskedView={MaskedView}`, and the package is a direct dependency now (it came through expo-router before, so it is already in the native build; no rebuild).
- **Still different:** the Settings sheet fades in and out (auto-paper's Dialog) while the cards appear at once.
- **Cards outside dialogs are the same surface** (2026-10-17, Jay: the Achievements wells looked different from Home's game tiles): Home's tiles (both layouts) and player button, and the stats screen's wells (every `StatSection` and `AchievementRow`), are the theme's `surface` with `onSurface`/`onSurfaceVariant` text, in both modes. The tiles and wells also sit on MD3's level-1 shadow (`@tastic/hud`'s `WELL_SHADOW`, no elevation tint; the player button is a control, not a card, and has none). The wells take this from hud's own defaults (`useWellColors`, the fleet-wide change it came with), so Solitaire's achievement list passes no colors to them. NEEDS `@tastic/hud` 0.22.0. The tiles used to be a fixed near-white `#ffffffee` in light mode, and the wells a 12% white tint over the felt with white text (`CHROME_SECTION_BG`, now deleted). What sits straight on the felt (titles, the Achievements picker and list label, every header) stays `CHROME_FG`. The check mark and progress bar of an unlocked achievement are its tier color mixed half with black in light mode, as hud's `AchievementCatalogSection` does. Seen on the web build (402x874, light and dark: a tile and a well read back the identical color); an unlocked achievement's colors were not seen (none unlocked).
- Pinned by `dialogConsistency.test.tsx` (Rules, Win, Loss) and hud's own tests (ConfirmDialog, the guide, the Settings sheet). Seen on the web build (402x874 and 874x402, dark): Settings, Rules, the New Game confirm and the guide; the win and loss cards and Delete Profile were not opened live.

## Toasts (2026-10-15)

Jay: the toast was hard to swipe away, sat on the footer (so "No helpful moves right now." covered Undo, the button the player wanted next), and looked generic (the felt's dark vignette color, white system text and a thin blue bar, since no icon was wired).
- **Swiping is the toaster's own (upstream, `@rific/toaster`, see its CLAUDE.md):** a flick dismisses however short it is (about 500pt/s, at least 12pt), the sideways distance needed dropped from 40% of the width to 30%, and a toast can also be pushed back off the edge its stack sits on (UP here), past half its height; a drag the other way only gives a little. The axis locks when the pan activates. Speed is sampled from the toast's own local positions, so it still works inside the turned frame.
- **Placement:** `components/ThemedToaster.tsx` (the pack's `Toaster`, rendered in the turned, pass-through frame `@tastic/shell`'s RootLayout lays it in) stacks from the top, in the band between the two corner buttons (Home/Settings on a game, trophy/cog on Home): `toastStackFrame` puts it 60pt plus each side's inset from the frame's edges, on the buttons' own line (`TOAST_TOP` 14 = their nominal 8 plus Paper IconButton's 6 margin), capped at the dialogs' 360 and centred in a wide frame. It covers the game's title (a Rules shortcut) and never the corner buttons. Measured on the web build: 402x874 pill x 60-342, y 14-54, Home 14-54 and Settings 348-388 beside it; 874x402 pill 360 wide at x 257-617. A second line or a caption reaches 2-30pt into the board's top row. **One toast at a time** (`limit={1}`, Jay, 2026-11-20; 3 before): a newer toast takes the older one's place, and the older comes back if the newer is swiped away before the older's time is up.
- **Look:** a 40pt pill (radius 20, as tall as the corner buttons) drawn on hud's `OverlayCard` (the toaster's `Card` prop), the same untinted surface as every dialog, blurring while the Blur setting is on. Text is the app font (`fonts.bodyMedium` at 13/18, so "No helpful moves right now." stays one line on a 390pt-or-wider phone; it wraps on a 375pt one) in `onSurface`, the caption `bodySmall` in `onSurfaceVariant`. **Each icon is a filled badge** (2026-10-17, Jay: the toasts were "still missing custom colors or icons"): a 28pt circle (`TOAST_BADGE_SIZE`, inset 6 from the pill's edges, so concentric with its rounded end) in the level's color, with Paper's glyph knocked out of it at 18pt in `toastBadgeInk` (auto-paper's `getTintTextColor`, its own on-color rule, so a toast's own color gets a readable glyph too). The level colors are the theme's roles: info the primary (the player's color), success/warning auto-paper's `success`/`warning`, error `danger`. The bare 20pt glyph it replaced was the dark theme's primary, which is the muted card back itself (`#9d2d53` for Rose), about 2:1 on the dark frosted pill, so on a phone the icon all but vanished and the pill read as plain grey. The level glyphs are bare marks (`TOAST_LEVEL_ICONS`: `information-variant`, `check-bold`, `exclamation-thick`, `close-thick`), never a ringed icon in the circle. The pill pads 6 round the badge and 12 on the right, and the title/caption carry 3pt margins so a taller pill's text sits 9pt from its edges; "No helpful moves right now." still fits one line at 390 (211.6 of 214pt). Per-message icons: the no-hint toast is the Hint button's light bulb switched off (`games/shared/gameToasts.ts`'s `showNoHintToast`, used by all 11 games), FreeCell's supermove limit `card-multiple-outline`, Spider's refused deal `view-column-outline`.
- **FreeCell's supermove toast is a title and a caption** ("Only 4 cards can move there" / "With 1 empty freecell and 1 other empty column."): as one ~95-character sentence it ran past the toaster's two title lines and was cut off.
- **Motion:** a toast comes down into place from above and goes back up (`@rific/toaster` 0.8.2's `motion.ts`, published 2026-10-17). The toaster had Reanimated's presets the wrong way round: `FadeInDown` STARTS below its spot and rises (`FadeInUp` starts above), so a top stack's toast rose into place from 25pt below, and a lone one left by dropping down into the board. Measured in headless Chrome on the web build: the pill now starts at y -11 and settles at 14 over 220ms. The exit didn't animate at all on the web build (the clone is gone within ~80ms, before and after; the exit is on a child of the item that unmounts), so it is unverified there and on a device.
- **Packages:** needs `@rific/toaster` 0.8.3 (0.8.0's `Card`, `labelStyle`/`captionStyle` and the new swipe, 0.8.2's motion, 0.8.3's crash fix), installed from npm. JS only, so it can ship in an OTA. **0.8.0-0.8.2 crashed every release build on a sideways swipe** (found 2026-10-17): the swipe rule `swipeShouldDismiss` is a worklet whose `fraction` parameter defaulted to a module constant, and a worklet only unpacks its constants inside its body, after the defaults run, so the default threw on the UI thread (a JS error there aborts a release build). Jest calls the plain functions, so nothing caught it; the toaster's `worklets.test.ts` now runs its swipe rules the way the UI thread does. Never give a worklet a default parameter that names a module constant (`isGhostVisible` here had one, never hit, fixed the same day).
- **Swiping verified on the iOS 27 simulator** (a Release build running a test bundle that showed a toast every few seconds): with 0.8.3 a toast swipes away to the right, to the left and upward with no crash; with 0.8.2 the first sideways swipe aborted the app. Not checked: a flick, a short drag that should snap back, Android. **Not verified live:** the look on a device (the blur, the badges, the island-side top inset). The badges were seen in headless Chrome at 390x844, dark and light.
