Pinned by `components/themedToaster.test.tsx` (placement, the card, the badge and level colors, the no-hint toast) and the toaster's own tests (swipe, flick, Card, text styles).

## Foundation order (2026-10-20)

The suit-locked foundations (Klondike, FreeCell, Yukon, Canfield) run spades, diamonds, clubs, hearts left to right (Jay: new deck order and the app icon's, and the colors alternate). It is `engine/foundation.ts`'s `FOUNDATION_SUITS`, which `suitIndex` and every slot label read; Forty Thieves' 8 slots (any Ace in any slot) label with it too. They used to follow the deck's `SUITS` (clubs, diamonds, hearts, spades: alphabetical, bridge order), which is still what builds every deck: changing that would change every seeded deal and the par simulation. Saves carry `foundationLayout` (`FOUNDATION_LAYOUT`, stamped on every save); one without it is from before, and `loadGameInProgress` moves its piles (the live board's and each undo entry's) and every foundation slot an undo entry names to the new slots (`remapLegacyFoundations`). `seedGameInProgress` stamps test saves; pass `foundationLayout: undefined` for an old one. Pinned by `stores/foundationLayout.test.ts`. Seen on the web build; not on a device.

## Top rows (2026-10-11)

- **FreeCell and Forty Thieves split their top row into two groups** (foundations | freecells; foundations | waste + stock). The row is as wide as the tableau, each group's cards sit at HALF the tableau's gap (`cardLayout.ts`'s `splitTopRowGroupGap`), and what that saves pools between the groups (space-between): `columns * gap / 2`, 16pt for FreeCell and 20pt for Forty Thieves on a phone. So there is something to squeeze, both size their tableau with `SPLIT_TOP_ROW_MIN_GAP` (4pt) as `cardLayoutForRow`'s and `useFittedGap`'s `minGap`: ~1.75pt of card width on a 402pt phone (FreeCell 48 -> 46.25, Forty Thieves 38 -> 36.2), nothing in the wide layout (its gap is already spread). Smaller top-row cards (Jay's first idea) were not built: every ghost layer (DragGhostLayer, DealLayer, WinCascadeLayer) flies a card at one size, so each card crossing between the rows would pop at its landing or need a mid-flight scale in all three. Forty Thieves' top row used to carry a fixed 18pt gap in portrait, 16pt wider than the tableau: it ran ~6pt off both edges of a 402pt phone. Pinned by `cardLayout.test.ts`.
- **Canfield's reserve is in the tableau row, under the waste** (slot 5 of the 7-slot grid: the 4 columns sit under the foundations, slot 4 stays open, slot 6 is empty). Its top row is now Klondike's (foundations, an open slot, waste, stock), so the draw-3 fan spreads into the open slot instead of over the reserve, and widens to clear a 10 like Klondike's. Both rows are `rowWidth` (7 slots) wide. Verified on the web build only (402x874 and 874x402): the measured grid, a reserve tap, undo back onto it, and a fresh deal landing the reserve's 13 cards there.

## @tastic/profile 0.12 and the font-load fix (2026-10-15)

- **@tastic/profile 0.12.0:** a failed App Group read no longer empties the roster (`resolveInitialProfiles`/`useSharedProfilesSync` in `src/stores/profiles.tsx`; nothing changed here). Before, a failed read on a foreground resume emptied the roster, and the next profile create saved over every Tastic game's shared profiles. The selection swap in the same release doesn't apply: Solitaire has one seat.
- **Font-load failure (audit F010):** the `fonts` gate opens on `loaded || !!error` (the shell's `Fonts` since 2026-11-02, `useFontsPreload` before). A failed load used to leave the splash up and `_layout.tsx` on its title forever.
- Not checked live; no new tests.

## Settings follow the player (2026-10-15)

Jay: his kids want sounds and hints, he doesn't; and EVERY setting follows the player, because any line between "the player's" and "the phone's" would be invisible to the people using it. `redux/playerSettingsSlice.ts`:
- **What:** the whole Settings sheet. The `settings` slice (rules, play aids, card looks, Reduce Motion), Lock Orientation with its `lockedRotation` (the shell's `appSettings` since 2026-10-29, filed inside the player's `settings` as before, so an entry 1.2.8 filed and one filed since read the same), `sound`, `haptic`, and the theme's `appearance` and `blur`. The theme's color is already the player's (ProfileThemeSync). Sound can follow the player because the ring/silent switch still mutes every game sound (`@rific/feedback-press` plays them as ambient audio). Within Solitaire only: these settings don't exist in the other games.
- **How:** the live slices always hold the SELECTED player's values, so `useSettings()` and every reader are unchanged (and `settingsSlice.ts` stays byte-identical to the fleet's). The providers bridged from them (FeedbackPressProvider, auto-paper's, the orientation lock) adopt a changed value, see `@rific/core`'s `createSettingsContext`. `playerSettings` (`@tastic/profile`'s `createProfileExtensionSlice`, keyed by profile id or `GUEST_PLAYER_ID`) holds everyone else's. `playerSettingsMiddleware` (the pack's `middleware`, `pack.ts`) compares seat 1 of the shell's `profileSelection` before and after every action, since three actions move it (a pick, the shell's `clearProfile` after a delete, `pruneToRoster` after a sibling app's delete; a rehydrate is skipped, it puts the last player back): files the live values under the player leaving (unless they're off the roster, i.e. just deleted), then loads the arriving player's, merged over the live values, the lock into `appSettings`. It used to match `activeProfile/select` alone. `lockedRotation` is named explicitly: an unlocked player's entry read back from storage has no such key, and a merge would leave the last player's angle behind (pinned).
- **A player with nothing filed** (new, created in a sibling app, or anyone from before this) starts with the settings on screen (Jay, 2026-10-15: picking a new profile should change nothing), so the update needed no migration. A locked player gets their own angle back, so picking them can turn the screen.
- **Per player, in `stores/playerData.ts`'s `usePlayerData()`** (2026-10-15, Jay): `resetPlayer(id)` drops one player's stats bucket (`StatsState.profiles[id]`), their unlocked achievements (`id:`-prefixed keys) and their saved games, and keeps their settings. A deleted profile needs no call: every reducer that keeps something per player answers `@tastic/profile`'s remove, which the shell's `deleteProfile` dispatches (`onProfileRemoved` in `redux/packReducers.ts`: stats bucket and achievements, saves, recent and top wins, settings; the shell's `seatColors` drops the color pick itself). `forgetPlayer` did that by hand until 2026-10-29. The stats screen's reset follows its player picker: a picked player resets just them (button, title and body name the player), Everyone resets everyone (`resetAll`: every stat, achievement and saved game). A player's reset leaves the All Profiles totals alone, since those count every deal the phone has seen and can't be un-summed (best scores, streaks). So does deleting a profile: its deals stay in Everyone (Jay, 2026-11-03, after seeing the total not drop on a dev build; subtracting them was offered and declined). A profile deleted in a sibling app leaves its data behind. A test that renders the stats pieces mocks `usePlayerData` (the real one loads the app store). Pinned by `stores/playerData.test.tsx` and `solitaireStats.test.tsx` (the reset's labels; the reset follows the shell's player picker). Seen on the web build: picking Kid on the stats screen turns the button into "Reset Kid's Stats" with the Kid-only confirm.
- Pinned by `playerSettingsSlice.test.ts`. Seen on the web build: from a pre-update store (two profiles, Dad selected in dark mode, nothing filed), picking Kid changed nothing; Kid set Light; picking Dad turned the screen dark again and Kid's Light stayed filed. Not checked on a device (the lock swap in particular).

## Card back tag: the Monogram back (2026-10-16)

The selected player's tag is its own back style, **Monogram** (`Settings.cardBackStyle` `'monogram'`, last in the Settings row; NOT the default, Geometric still is; Jay, 2026-10-16), instead of a medallion on the geometric back. `components/MonogramCardBack.tsx` draws it on the **Plain** back (Jay liked its double-line border): `@tastic/cards`' `DecoratedCardBack style='plain'` as is, with a second Svg laid over it for the monogram (the package takes no children and doesn't export the art), the wrapper View sized so the overlay never lays out as a zero frame. The monogram is as big as fits inside the inner line (which encloses 0.647W x 1.104W; `monogramFontSize`: `MAX_INK_WIDTH` 0.52W, `MAX_CAP_HEIGHT` 0.6W, from DejaVu Serif Bold advance widths measured off the bundled TTF with fontTools, so a lone S is set at ~0.72W and MMM still fits; an emoji at 0.52W). Letters are the card font in the back's ink (capitals); an emoji keeps its own colors at 60% opacity (Jay, 2026-10-15: not a sticker). With no tag (a guest, or a profile whose tag is empty) it shows **S** (`CardBackArt.tsx`'s `monogramFor`/`DEFAULT_MONOGRAM`). `components/CardBackArt.tsx` is the one place a style picks its art (board cards, Home's fan, the Settings previews; the Monogram preview shows the player's own tag). The geometric back no longer gets the tag, so `@tastic/cards`' `CardBack` `tag` prop is unused here. Nobody was switched to it (Jay's call): a player who had a tag on the geometric back loses it until they pick Monogram. Recoloring an emoji into the ink does not work on iOS (react-native-svg masks and filters on text, and `mixBlendMode`, all fail there while working in a browser): see `@tastic/cards`' CLAUDE.md before trying again. Seen on the web build (phone size, dark): the fan with a unicorn tag (on the Plain back) and with S (on the earlier frame), the Settings row, a Klondike stock. Not seen on a device. Pinned by `cardBackArt.test.tsx`.

**The back's detail keeps its accent with Invert Dark Mode Colors on** (Jay, 2026-10-16): `theme.ts`'s `cardBackPatternInverted` used to be `cardBackPattern` pushed 30% toward black in dark mode, which left the weave and frame barely showing; it is now the same color.

## The player button (2026-10-16)

Jay: it wasn't clear that the color swatch beside the profile dropdown changed the color in Solitaire only, or that it was a different thing from the profile. Home's player control (the shell's Home since 2026-11-02, built from this app's `components/ProfilePanel.tsx`) is now ONE button opening ONE dropdown: `@tastic/profile`'s `ProfilePicker` with its `colorSection` (the button leads with a chip in the color in effect with the player's tag, then the name and a chevron; the dropdown's top row is "Color", caption "Solitaire only" (Jay: three words was too much for a popover; left out for Guest, who has no color in the other games), above Guest, the profiles and Manage). The Color row swaps the dropdown's contents for a back row and the swatches (Jay expected them nested behind the row; the first build laid the grid out above the profiles). A swatch press stays on that page so the felt and the fan change behind it, for trying a few colors (Jay agreed); a press away closes the whole dropdown, and it opens on the list again. The dropdown scrolls when the frame is too short for it (a phone held sideways), with auto-paper's `FadingScrollView` fade, the same cue as Settings and Rules. NEEDS `@tastic/profile` 0.14.1 (`colorSection`, `triggerStyle`, `IdentityChip`, a 3-letter tag sized to fit its chip, plain-fg labels, the edit-row stacking fix; 0.14.1 pins Manage below the scrolling rows, so it stays reachable and the rows fade above it). `@tastic/profile` ships native code (the App Group store), so its version is part of the runtime fingerprint: every bump needs a new build, an OTA can't carry it. Bumped @tastic/profile to 0.16.2 (2026-10-18, no code changes needed); needs a new native build.
- **Each player's pick is kept across relaunches** (Jay: "an override SHOULD live on relaunch"). The shell's `seatColors` holds them since 2026-10-29 (`@tastic/profile`'s `createSeatColorsSlice`, which began as this app's `redux/tableColorSlice.ts`): seat 1's guest color and each profile's pick by id, kept beside the profile's own color when picked; the color in effect is worked out, never stored (`stores/profiles.tsx`'s `usePlayer`: the selected profile's pick while it still has the color the pick was made against, else its own color; guest's for no selection). Before, a profile's pick was reset to its own color at every launch and every switch, and only guest's was kept. Picking the profile's own color drops its pick (so it follows that color again); a new color for the profile itself drops it too, on the Profiles screen or (since the shell) in a sibling app (a rename keeps it); a deleted profile's goes with it, a player reset keeps it, like their settings. A store from before has an `override` key nothing reads and no `profileColors`, so every profile starts on its own color.
- **A dot marks the selected profile's own color among the swatches** while a pick is in effect (the check takes its place when they are the same swatch), so the way back to it doesn't depend on matching the row's color to one of two similar swatches by eye (Jay asked for it). It is the package's (`ColorSection`'s `profileColor`); a profile color outside the palette gets no dot.
- **The selected row in the list still fills with the profile's own color**, so a profile with a pick shows a different color there than on the button. Left as is (Jay: it's clear which one is selected, which is all that matters).
- Home's landscape left-pane budget (the shell's `homeLayout.ts`, `HOME_LEFT_PANE_PICKER_HEIGHT`) counts the 44pt button, not the old 60pt row.
- Pinned by `stores/profiles.test.tsx` ("color picks"), `stores/playerData.test.tsx`, and the packages' own tests (the button is the shell's Home's since 2026-11-02; Solitaire's `profilePanel.test.tsx` went with it). Seen on the web build (402x874 and 874x402, dark): the button, the dropdown's layout, a pick changing the felt and fan live and surviving a reload, a switch to another profile showing their own color, the dot on Kid's own color under a pick, Guest's dropdown without a caption, the dropdown opening above the button sideways, scrolling, and the fade at either end; after the nested rework, the Color row to the swatches and back, a press away closing it and the next open starting on the list (driven through DOM events, the pane was hidden, so no screenshot of the nested page). Then light mode (Kid set to Light and back): the white pill with a black name, both pages readable; the selected swatch's white ring vanishes on the white menu, the check still marks it (hud's picker does the same). The button's name and Manage are plain white/black, the color only on the chip, icon, border and caret (Manage in the muted accent read almost disabled on black). Not checked: a device.
- **Also fixed: the Profiles screen's color popover was drawn under the rows after the one being edited** (Jay found it on the web build; iOS stacks it the same way). A `@tastic/profile` fix (`ProfilesManager`'s edit row gets a zIndex), in 0.14.0.
- **On `@tastic/profile` 0.15.0 and `@tastic/hud` 0.21.0 (2026-10-17).** This button became the fleet's seat control: hud's `PlayerSetupPanel` now draws every loadout seat in AirHockey, BoxHockey, LightCycles, Pong and Snake the same way, with a CPU seat's difficulty levels under the same Color row (profile's new `OptionPicker`). Nothing changes here: `ProfilePicker`'s props are the same, it now draws through an internal `PickerMenu` it shares with `OptionPicker`, and `colorSection` gained `takenValue`/`allowSwapTaken`, which Solitaire doesn't need (one player). The other games' per-profile color picks use the same rule as `tableColorSlice` but through profile's `createSeatColorsSlice`; Solitaire keeps its own slice (it has no seats). Checked on a copy of the repo against the new builds: typecheck, the full suite, and the web Home's button, color page and a live pick.

## OS portrait lock (2026-10-08, gone 2026-11-06)

Gone: see "The OS turns" at the end. What it was: the OS is locked to portrait on every device and the app draws all rotation itself (`@tastic/core`'s `OrientationProvider` reads the accelerometer). `@tastic/core`'s Expo config plugin (`"@tastic/core"` in `app.json`'s `plugins`) owns every native setting that depends on: `orientation: "portrait"`; `ios.requireFullScreen` plus a Portrait-only iPad orientation list (audit F009: without it Expo forces all four iPad orientations, so iOS turned the screen on top of the app's own rotation, and Split View, Slide Over and Stage Manager are the price); and `android:appCategory="game"` (Android 16 and later ignore the portrait lock on tablets and unfolded foldables, except for games). Don't set any of those by hand here, and don't add `expo-screen-orientation` (it re-enables upside down on home-button iPhones). The why behind each is in @tastic/core's README. Check the result with `npx expo config --type introspect`; it only takes effect in a new native build.

## CI

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` now uses the shared reusable workflow (`infinitetoken/Workflows/.github/workflows/npm-ci.yml@v1`, defaults to `npm run verify`) - previously a hand-rolled workflow running `npm ci && npm run ci`. The `ci` npm script was renamed to `verify` to match.

## Web on games.infinitetoken.com (2026-10-17)

The web build is served at https://games.infinitetoken.com/<game>/ with every other Tastic game on that one origin, so they share browser storage and, through it, `@tastic/profile`'s player roster (the web side of the App Group). Games-Web, the hub at games.infinitetoken.com, proxies `/<game>/*` to this project's own production deployment (see its CLAUDE.md). Before this, each game had its own subdomain. Same section in Hangman, LightCycles, Snake and Solitaire.

- **The build:** `build:web` runs `verify`, then `WEB_BASE_URL=/<game> expo export -p web --output-dir web-build/<game>`. `app.config.ts` (identical in all four) turns `WEB_BASE_URL` into `experiments.baseUrl` only when it's set: a base URL also reaches native bundles, asset paths and EAS updates, so it never goes in `app.json`. Expo writes the export flat and prefixes the URLs inside it, so the output folder is what puts the files under `/<game>/`, and this project's own deployment serves the same paths the hub forwards. Building more than one game locally: run them one at a time with `--clear`, or Metro's shared cache leaks one app's config into the next.
- **`vercel.json`** (identical in all four): `buildCommand` is `npm run build:web`, the output is `web-build`, `cleanUrls` is on, and a `/<game>/...` path that isn't a file rewrites to `/<game>/index`, the path `cleanUrls` gives `index.html` (the form Vercel's instant static serving answers; see the 2026-10-16 note). The old `<game>.infinitetoken.com` subdomains were retired 2026-10-22 (Jay deleted their DNS records in DNSimple), and the host-matched redirects that sent them to `games.infinitetoken.com/<game>/` were dropped from this file. A link to one of them no longer resolves.
- **Privacy and store links:** the per-game `public/privacy.html` is gone. The one privacy policy is the hub's `/privacy`, and `store.config.json` points there, with support at `/support` and marketing at `/<game>`.
- **Storage:** redux-persist's `keyPrefix` is `'<game>:'` (still `solitaire:` here after the rename, Jay's call 2026-11-11: players never see it, and changing it would mean importing everyone's data), so this game's state is `<game>:root` in localStorage instead of the `persist:root` every game used to write, which on one origin would have been each other's state. REHYDRATE still reports key `root`, so `guideSlice`'s `persistKey` is unchanged. Any other key this game writes must be namespaced by the game too. The prefix applies on native as well; saves under the old key were abandoned (nothing has shipped).
- **Skia** (Snake, LightCycles): `canvaskit.wasm` is served from `/<game>/canvaskit.wasm`. Until `@tastic/hud` hands canvaskit a `locateFile`, canvaskit resolves it relative to the page, which only works from `/<game>/` and the pages directly under it; the hub redirects the bare `/<game>` to `/<game>/` for that reason.


## Icons and splash (2026-10-13)

Seven games (AirHockey, BoxHockey, Hangman, LightCycles, Pong, Snake, Solitaire) and Expo-Starter use the same four files, `assets/images/icon-light.png`, `icon-dark.png`, `splash-light.png` and `splash-dark.png`, and the same `app.json` config. The icons are opaque 1024px squares on `#FAFAFA` (light) and `#050505` (dark). Each splash is its icon's art on a transparent background. The `expo-splash-screen` plugin paints the same two colors behind it, at `imageWidth` 200. `expo.icon` is `icon-dark.png`, `web.favicon` is `splash-dark.png`, `ios.icon` is the dark/light pair, and there is no Android `adaptiveIcon` (Android uses `expo.icon`). The favicon is the transparent splash art, not the opaque icon, because it looks better in a browser tab (2026-10-16), so `splash-dark.png` has to read on both light and dark tabs. Snake is the only game whose light and dark art differ (its body and head fill). The other games keep two identical splash files so the config is the same everywhere. Icon and splash changes only take effect in a new native build. The splash fades out over 400ms in every game: each calls `SplashScreen.setOptions({ fade: true, duration: 400 })` next to `preventAutoHideAsync()`, because iOS doesn't fade unless told to (Android always does). Solitaire's call was missing until 2026-10-17 (`_layout.tsx`; the shell's `Theme` makes it since 2026-11-02). It's a JS call to a native module every build already has, so it ships in an OTA.

**Solitaire's icon and splash are on felt again (2026-11-05, Jay).** Both `icon-light.png` and `icon-dark.png` are one file, Jay's own export: the four aces from before 2026-10-13 (`git show cabc645:assets/icon.png`, on a brighter green, `#0B6E4F`) redrawn on Rose's felt, `#1D5945` in the middle to `#124030` at the corners. One file in both slots because Jay wants the same icon in both modes, so the icon config stays the fleet's. The splash keeps the fleet's art and `imageWidth` but its background is Rose's felt, `#1E5C47` light and `#123F30` dark, the exact colors the app draws for the default player (see "The felt is ONE `TableBackground`"). Solitaire is the one game whose icon and splash aren't on the plain background. Rose is the default player's color, so a fresh install's icon, splash and Home are one table; the in-app felt follows the player's color (navy, red, brown for others), which neither the icon nor the splash can. Needs a new native build, which the CardTastic rename needs anyway.

**CardTastic has an Android adaptive icon (2026-11-11, for its first Play build), unlike the fleet's other games.** `app.json`'s `android.adaptiveIcon`: `assets/images/android-icon-foreground.png` (the splash art, the same pixels as the iOS icon's aces, at two-thirds), `android-icon-background.png` (the iOS icon's felt gradient, measured off `icon-dark.png`: radial, `#1E5C47`-ish center to `#124030` corners, falling with the distance to the power 2.5, at the same two-thirds) and `android-icon-monochrome.png` (the four cards white, their outlines and ink knocked out, for Android 13+ themed icons). Android shows the middle 72dp of a 108dp layer, so the visible icon is the iOS icon's composition; the aces' farthest corner sits 282px from the center of the 1024px layer, inside the 313px safe circle, so no launcher mask clips them. Without it the launcher drew the square felt icon shrunk inside a white shape. `scripts/generate-android-icon.py` makes all three (`uv run --with pillow python scripts/generate-android-icon.py [preview.png]`): rerun it if the icon or splash art changes. The Android 12+ splash needs nothing: the splash plugin draws the art 200dp wide on a 288dp canvas, so its farthest corner is ~83dp from the center, inside the 96dp circle Android shows. Previewed in a circle, a squircle and a rounded square and themed dark and light; not seen on a launcher.

## Expo SDK 58 (2026-10-16)

Moved from SDK 57 the day SDK 58 went stable. SDK 58's native template adopts the UIScene life cycle that apps linked against the iOS 27 SDK need, so the SDK 57 workarounds are gone: `plugins/withSceneDelegate.js` (and its `app.json` entry) and the `macos-tahoe-26.5-xcode-26.6` image pins in `eas.json`. Builds use EAS's default image for SDK 58 and, locally, this Mac's Xcode 27. Tests need `@react-native/jest-preset` installed (jest-expo 58's peer; the fleet's `legacy-peer-deps` means npm never adds it). React Native 0.88's strict TypeScript types are deferred fleet-wide through `@infinitetoken/tsconfig` 0.6; the web tab title from `expo-router/head` is set after hydration only; see TODO.md.

## Safari shadow lines (2026-10-16)

Jay saw two faint vertical lines under the last card of a FreeCell column in desktop Safari, running down to where the column's old bottom card had been. Every card that leaves a column did it in WebKit: the shadowed Pressable sits inside the card's absolute wrapper, and when the card is removed WebKit does not repaint the part of the shadow outside the card, so its two side edges stay until something else repaints there (a forced repaint clears them; the DOM has nothing at that spot). Not the wrapper's idle `translateX(0)` from the shake style: dropping it changed nothing. The fix is `willChange: 'transform'` on web on PlayingCard's shadow style (`WEB_OWN_LAYER`), so each card is its own compositing layer and its pixels go with it. Don't remove it as noise. Chrome never showed it (its DOM is identical), native is untouched. Checked in a WKWebView window (the same WebKit as Safari 26.6) at 1300x720: without the fix 4 of 4 departures left the lines (three cards to freecells, one undo off a column), with it 0 of 8 (to freecells, to a column, and two undos taking cards off freecells). Not checked: Safari on an iPhone or iPad, and the GPU memory of ~52-104 card layers on a low-end phone's browser.

## Speeding through the stock (2026-10-16)

Jay watched his kids speed through Klondike's stock: quick taps sometimes did nothing, and the flip-over lagged. Every stock tap is one draw now, however fast, and a held stock deals by itself.
- **One press target for the stock** (`StockWaste`): a feedback-press `Pressable` (`testID='stock-pile'`, label Draw / Turn over) that stays mounted and fully opaque whatever it draws; the card back, the empty slot and the frozen look are art inside it, never pressable themselves. Three ways taps used to be lost: on iOS, NO tap reached the stock while a turn-over flew into it (about half a second), because the hidden stock is drawn at `opacity: 0` and React Native on iOS hit-tests no view under 1% opacity (`RCTViewComponentView`'s `betterHitTest`; the web does, which is why it never showed there); a press that began on the card and ended after the swap to the empty slot (or back) went with the unmounted one; and the last card's take-off hold ignored the tap. A tap now always goes to the screen, which decides. Never put a touch target inside a view hidden with opacity 0.
- **A tap waits instead of being dropped** (`games/shared/stockWait.ts`: a 16ms poll, 3s budget, the loop inline right before the commit, like Pyramid's `whenLanded`). Klondike's `drawCanGo`: anything waits while a turn-over's cards are still flying into the stock (the stock is drawn as it was, empty, until they land); in Draw 3 every draw waits while anything is landing on the waste; Draw 1 draws overlap freely. Canfield's: in Draw 3, or for a turn-over (it has no flight), while anything is landing on the waste. Pyramid already waited this way.
- **Draw 3 fans go one at a time.** Two quick Draw 3 taps (Klondike's option, Canfield's default) used to start a second `deal.start()` while the first fan flew, which cancels the first: its cards vanished mid-air and never reported landing, so the waste stayed frozen and Undo waited for good until a New Game. Seen fixed on the web build (fans one after another, Undo working after).
- **The flip-over turns cards back in mid-air** (Klondike Draw 1, `recycleWaste`): the last cards drawn, still flying to the waste, are peeled out of their flights (`peelInAir`, as a held Undo's and a tapped run's are) and go to the stock from where they are; the clump of landed cards takes off from the waste. Before, Klondike turned the waste over at once and the card in the air was drawn twice (flying in, and on top of the clump flying out). The waste keeps drawing the clump's top (`holdWasteTop`, the `wasteTakeOff` state: its own take-off hold is for the card in the air) until the clump's ghost shows. Draw 3 (deal-layer flights can't be peeled) and Pyramid wait for the cards to land instead.
- **Taps that waited are paced** (`createStockQueue`): they go `STOCK_QUEUE_GAP_MS` (`stockWait.ts`, the auto-play's 96ms) apart once the way is clear, not all in the landing's frame (three cards flying as one). Klondike and Canfield only: in Pyramid a tap on a card can be parked beside them and they must resume in tap order (a paced draw let a parked pair go first; `pyramidScreenQuickPlay.test.tsx`'s parked-pair cases).
- **Overlapping Draw 1 draws show the card that landed** (Klondike and Canfield pass `settledWasteTop`, as Pyramid does): the frozen copy of the top from before the whole stream was never refreshed while taps kept coming, so each landing card vanished onto an older one until the next landed.
- **Holding the stock** (`components/stockHold.ts`'s `useStockHold`, the `useUndoHold` loop): a draw (or turn-over) at the 400ms long press, then a draw `STOCK_HOLD_REPEAT_MS` (250ms) after each has started, until the finger lifts. 250 is so each card can be read and let go on (Jay, 2026-10-18: at the auto-play's 96ms it "flies through the cards way too fast"), yet a 24-card stock still goes through in about six seconds; it is the one knob, and queued taps don't use it. It never turns the waste over (`repeat: true` at an empty stock is refused), so it stops where the stock runs out; a hold started on the empty stock turns it over, waits for the cards to land, and deals on. Only where the stock turns over (`canRecycle`: Klondike, Canfield, Pyramid); in Golf, TriPeaks and Forty Thieves one hold would deal the whole stock, which never comes back. A screen's `handleDrawStock` takes `StockRequestOptions` (`repeat`, `cancelled` = the finger lifted) and resolves true once it has committed.
- Seen on the web build (402x874, synthetic pointer events 120ms apart): 30 taps through a whole stock and over the flip-over gave 30 moves, the turn-over went at once (turnaround), the waiting taps went as the cards landed and then ~110ms apart; a hold drew a card every ~105ms and stopped at the empty stock; Draw 3 fans one after another. Not checked on a device: the iOS tap fix itself (pinned structurally by `stockWaste.test.tsx`), and the feel. Pinned by `stockWaste.test.tsx`, `stockHold.test.tsx`, `slotTakeOffHold.test.tsx`, and the `speeding through the stock` describes in `klondikeScreenWin`/`canfieldScreenWin` (each fails on the old code).

## Web scrollbars (2026-10-16)

Jay saw a desktop browser's scrollbar far in from the window's edge, left of the Settings button. A scrollbar is drawn at its scroller's edge, and both scrollers that showed one stopped short of the window. Home's wide tile grid ended at the cog's gutter, 110pt in at 1280 wide. A classic (always-on, 15pt) scrollbar also took width from a grid sized to fill its pane exactly, so its third column wrapped and left an empty column beside the scrollbar. It now runs to the frame's right edge with the gutter as its content's padding, and the tiles sit in a View of their own width (`rightPaneWidth`), which can run into that padding. The game board's scroller (`GameContentGutter`'s content column) ends at the control column, and a classic scrollbar there also covered the last card column, so on web the board shows none at all (`showsVerticalScrollIndicator={Platform.OS !== 'web'}`, Jay's call; the wheel and trackpad still scroll it, native keeps its indicator). Achievements and Profiles hide theirs already (hud's `BaseStatsScreen`, profile's `ProfilesManager`). A scrollbar at the window's edge was tried for the board: the RULES/HINT/NEW GAME captions drew over its track. Home's scrollers are also auto-paper's `FadingScrollView` now (the same day, Jay: every scrolling screen uniform), so the menu fades out at an edge while there is more past it, in both layouts, like the Settings and Rules sheets and the player dropdown. Achievements (hud's `BaseStatsScreen`) and Profiles (profile's `ProfilesManager`) got the same fade in their packages, unreleased as of this note: Solitaire shows it there once it installs the new versions (any new `@tastic/profile` version changes the native fingerprint, so it needs a new build, not an OTA). On web the mask covers a classic scrollbar too, so its ends fade with the content. Seen on the web build in headless Chrome (all four screens, at rest and scrolled, with the packages' changed sources copied into node_modules for the check and put back); not on a device. Was pinned by Solitaire's `homeScreen.test.tsx`; Home's scrollers are the shell's since 2026-11-02 (the same `FadingScrollView`s). The scrollbar fix was checked in headless Chrome over CDP with classic scrollbars forced by injected `::-webkit-scrollbar` CSS (Chrome on a Mac uses overlay scrollbars, headless too): Home at 1280x720 (three columns at the same x as without a scrollbar, the scrollbar at the edge) and Yukon at 874x402 (no scrollbar, the last column whole, a wheel scrolls). Not checked: Windows, Firefox.

## Haptics (2026-10-17)

Jay felt the deal stutter on his phone and asked whether the buzz fired on landing. It didn't (every card buzzed at take-off), but the rule now is: **a card buzzes when it is played, never when it lands**, and the deal and the auto-play buzz on a steady beat. `useCardSounds.ts` has the full list.
- **A press:** every card (and the stock) is a feedback-press `Pressable`, which buzzes at touch-down. That is a tap's only buzz: each screen's tap handler (`handleCardTap`, Spider's `handlePressCard`), the player's draws and turn-overs, and Pyramid's `commitRemoval` no longer buzz a second time as the card takes off (they still click). A held stock's repeat draws (`opts.repeat`) buzz, since nothing pressed them.
- **A drag's release** (`PileView`'s `handleDragEnd`, `DraggableCard`'s `handleRelease`): `hapticCard` once the drop stands. A refused drop's invalid sound and buzz play as the snap-back STARTS (`DragGhostLayer`'s `snapBack`), not when the card is back.
- **A deal** (`DealLayer.tsx`): click and buzz together on a beat picked from the deal's own schedule (`dealBeatTicks`: the first card, then the next card at least `DEAL_BEAT_MIN_GAP_MS` (60) later, so every Nth card of the regular stagger: 68ms for a 28-card deal, 72ms for a compressed 52-card one, 67-88ms face up). `runBeat` plays them on the deal's clock (`startedAtRef`), so a late timer never pushes the rest back; beats a JS stall made overdue are one beat, and a beat within half a gap of the last is dropped. A skip is one beat, Reduce Motion one for the whole deal. It used to be one click and buzz per card from each card's own take-off timer, throttled to one per 40ms: every second or third card at uneven gaps, ~25 iOS haptic calls a second on the main thread, where the flights are drawn. Every deal-layer flight gets the beat (initial deals, Spider/Scorpion stock deals, Aces Up rounds, draw-3 fans, the win sweeps, undo deals).
- **Auto-play** (`useAutoFoundationCascade`, `useAutoFinish`): each screen passes `beat: cascadeFeedback`, played the moment a card leaves on the hook's clock, before its measurement (`firePlay`/`fireDrawAndPlay` play none any more). The cascade's gap is now a fixed clock too. A plain sweep draw plays its own (a draw-3 fan's is the deal's beat).
- **The click keeps the beat too** (`@rific/feedback-press` 0.13.1+): its `useAudioPool` used to rewind a player (`seekTo(0)`) and only play once the seek resolved, on every trigger, and a seek resolves at uneven times, so the deal's clicks drifted off the buzz's beat. Each player now rewinds as its clip finishes (the clip is 0.1s and each of the 8 players comes round every ~0.5s), so a beat only calls `play()`. Not heard on a device.
- **Unchanged:** Undo (each undo clicks and buzzes, so a tapped Undo buzzes at its press and again as the card leaves), Spider's completed-run flight (a buzz per card at take-off), a Hint hold's hints.
- **On Android the buzz is a real haptic since @rific/feedback-press 0.14.2** (2026-11-16): every `selection` is Android's own clock tick (`performAndroidHapticsAsync`, the effect its keyboard uses) and a long press a Confirm, where it used to run the motor for 30ms (120ms for a long press) through React Native's `Vibration`, a dull buzz that blurred into one rumble during a deal (Jay found play on Android not smooth). JavaScript only: it uses expo-haptics' native function, which every build already has, so it ships in an OTA update. It follows the phone's touch feedback setting, so a phone with that off feels nothing. Not felt on a device.
- Pinned by `dealLayer.test.tsx` (`dealBeatTicks`, the steady beat, the stall, skip, Reduce Motion), `useAutoFoundationCascade.test.tsx` and `useAutoFinish.test.tsx` (the fixed clock and the beat before the measurement), `dragGhostLayer.test.tsx` (the release buzz, the invalid buzz at the snap-back's start). **Not felt on a device:** whether the deal still stutters on Jay's phone, and how the beat feels.

## Keep Awake (2026-10-17)

Every game screen keeps the screen from dimming and locking while it's up (`@rific/core`'s `useKeepAwake('game')` in `GameChrome`, which every one of the eleven game routes renders, released on unmount), unless the player turns off the Keep Awake row in Settings (default on, read live so the toggle applies mid-game). There is no `/game` route here, so `GameChrome` is the one place that covers them all; Home, Stats and Profiles don't hold it. One tag is safe because only one game screen is ever mounted: they're reached only from Home (its links and the resume `Redirect`), never from each other. Expo only keeps the screen awake by itself in development builds.

- **The setting is core's, not this app's:** `keepAwakeReducer` is mounted as `keepAwake` in `store.ts` (persisted like every key but `gameInProgress`), and `@tastic/shell`'s `KeepAwakeBridge` (Solitaire's own until 2026-11-02) (FeedbackBridge's shape: `initialValue` from the slice, `onChange` dispatching `keepAwakeActions.initialize`) mounts core's `KeepAwakeProvider` with `expoKeepAwake={ExpoKeepAwake}` (`import * as ExpoKeepAwake from 'expo-keep-awake'`), just inside FeedbackBridge. Handing it the module is what turns the feature on: without it `useKeepAwake` does nothing and hud hides the row. `useKeepAwake` drops both expo-keep-awake rejections (web refuses a wake lock in a frame without the Permissions-Policy, and rejects a release when none is held). The same day's first version (`Settings.keepAwake`, an effect in `GameChrome` and dialog props) was replaced by this before it was committed.
- **It follows the player** like the rest of the Settings sheet: `PlayerSettings` has a `keepAwake` entry, `playerSettingsMiddleware` files and loads it with `sound`/`haptic` (merged over the live value, so a player filed before it existed keeps what is on screen). Pinned by `playerSettingsSlice.test.ts`.
- The row is `@tastic/hud`'s (0.23.0+): `BaseSettingsDialog` reads core's setting itself, with no props, and shows the row (caption "Stop auto-lock mid-match") only when `useExpoKeepAwake()` returns a module and the device is touch-first (native and touch-first browsers), like Lock Orientation. Needs `@rific/core` 0.4.0+.
- `expo-keep-awake` was already installed as a dependency of `expo`, so it is already in existing native builds; it's a direct dependency (`~58.0.3`, via `npx expo install`). No new native build needed.
- Same feature as AirHockey, BoxHockey, LightCycles, Pong and Snake (2026-10-15), and added to ArcheryDuel, Hangman, HexFleet and Solitaire on 2026-10-17. Jay chose to include the turn-based games: the OS auto-lock only counts touches, so a long think on a turn can lock a release build too. The hook's comment is Snake's except for that one clause (Snake names tilt, keyboard steering and watching the CPU).

## Pip fireworks (2026-10-17)

The win's fireworks are bursts of suit pips (Jay, 2026-10-17: it ties the celebration to the game), `components/winning/PipFireworks.tsx`, in place of `@tastic/sprites`' dots (no longer a dependency here). Every game screen renders `{winRevealed && <PipFireworks />}` where `<Fireworks>` was, so nothing about when the celebration shows has changed.
- **The show:** one suit per burst, the four drawn from a shuffled bag so every suit gets its turn; 10 pips at 14pt per burst at the old cadence (a burst every 180-420ms, anywhere in the frame). Each pip pops in, tilts and turns a little and droops as it fades, over 1.1s. One clock per burst (a shared value) drives every pip's style on the UI thread; a pip is an `Animated.View` (sized, never a zero frame) around `@tastic/cards`' `SuitPip`. A burst's spread is rolled when it spawns, outside render.
- **The deck follows the card style:** the tarot deck throws cups, coins, wands and swords (wands and swords turn to their flight angle, so they radiate tip first); every other style throws the standard four.
- **Each pip is drawn the way its card draws it** (Jay: "Could they not be black with a white outline?", "Unless it was dark mode with inverted on"): `@tastic/cards`' `suitInk` (the ink `CardFace` uses for that suit, style and inversion), ringed by `SuitPip`'s `outline` in `cardFaceColor` (the card's face). So the black suits are black with a white ring, and turn light with a dark ring under Invert Dark Mode Colors in dark mode; tarot pips keep their own inks. Invert is `settings.invertDarkModeColors && dark`, as PlayingCard's faces. A first pass lifted the inks for the felt (white spades), which Jay rejected.
- **Needs `@tastic/cards` 0.6.0** (`suitInk`, `cardFaceColor`, `SuitPip`'s `outline`; released 2026-10-17). JS only, so it can ship in an OTA.
- **Sharing it later:** `PipBursts` (the animation, told its deck, inks and ring) knows nothing about Solitaire; `PipFireworks` is the wrapper that reads this app's settings and theme. If another card game wants the bursts, move `PipBursts` into `@tastic/cards` behind its own subpath (Reanimated would become a peer, so keep it off the main entry) and give that game its own wrapper (Jay, 2026-10-17: wait until a second game needs it).
- **Seen** in headless Chrome on the web build: a seeded Klondike one card from won, tapping the last King: the bursts go off around the win card while the cascade bounces. Also a side-by-side preview (since deleted) of standard, tarot and dark-inverted. **Not seen on a device:** the cost of ~40-60 live pips (each its own Svg) mounting and unmounting beside the 52-card cascade on a phone is the thing to check.
- Pinned by `pipFireworks.test.tsx` (one suit per burst, the inks and ring in light, dark and inverted, tarot against every other style); the screen tests mock the component.

## Animated props skip the shadow tree (iOS 2026-10-17, Android 2026-11-15)

Jay felt deals stutter on his phone. `package.json`'s `reanimated.staticFeatureFlags` turns on `IOS_SYNCHRONOUSLY_UPDATE_UI_PROPS` (Reanimated 4.7's way to set a static flag: `pod install` reads it into the Reanimated pod's compiler flags).
- **What it does:** each frame, Reanimated writes non-layout animated styles (opacity, transform, colors, border radii, shadows) straight to the views instead of committing them through the shadow tree. React commits still pick the latest values up from Reanimated's registry, and Reanimated writes its own values again right after every React mount, in the same main-thread pass. With it off, Reanimated skipped its commits from the start of each React commit until that commit mounted, so every ghost in the air held still for a frame or more and then jumped; a deal commits at every landing.
- **Measured** on the iOS 27 simulator with Release builds (arm64, one per flag value, the same JS bundle in both), each frame timed by a test-only magenta bar moved by RN's native driver along the bottom edge: frames mid-deal where every card held still between frames where they moved went from 95 in 24 Klondike deals to 0 in 23, and from 61 in 20 FreeCell deals to 0 in 21. Frames where the whole screen stalled did not change (about 1.6 per Klondike deal either way; FreeCell 27-31, the simulator drawing 52 shadowed faces at about 30fps), so those are not this flag's to fix, and a phone may differ.
- **Hand-offs checked** with a blip detector (a patch that changes for 1-3 frames and then returns exactly, its surroundings still before and after; the recorder's repeated frames dropped), every flag-on flag looked at frame by frame: the 44 deals, scripted presses on every card of seeded Klondike and Yukon boards (moves, shakes, auto-play, draws) followed by 40 undos 96ms apart (peels), Pyramid pairs, Kings and draws, the seeded FreeCell finish into the win cascade and fireworks, a Spider run flying to its slot, a drag and a snap-back. No hole, blank face, flash at an old landing spot or doubled card; every flag was a card moving more than its own size in a frame, a take-off or a firework, and the flag-on runs had fewer flags than flag-off in every case. For the dim fade see the dim-after-deal bullet.
- **What it costs: `measure` lags a slide.** `measure`/`measureInWindow` (and Reanimated's own `measure`) read the shadow tree, which now learns an animated transform only at the next React commit that carries Reanimated's registry, or at its 500ms settled-value sync (`FORCE_REACT_RENDER_FOR_SETTLED_ANIMATIONS`). A probe put the scoreboard toggle up to 55pt off for 0.3-1.6s after its slide while the clock was stopped; with the clock running, a moved press on the lowered scoreboard was still ignored about 10s later. RN's Pressable keeps a press only while the finger stays within 20pt (30 below) of where `measure` put the button at press-in, so a press that moved a little was dropped (Reanimated's docs list this caveat, issue 10121). Reproduced with the simulator tool's touch paths: flag off works, flag on ignored, flag on with the fix works. Fixed by widening the scoreboard's and the footer buttons' `pressRetentionOffset` by their slides (`GameChrome.tsx`, pinned by `gameChromePressRetention.test.tsx`). Nothing else here measures a view under a Reanimated transform except through the card shake (8pt at most); the fan glide is a layout animation, so flight measurements are unchanged, and UIKit picks the touched view from real positions either way. A new Pressable inside a view Reanimated moves needs the same widening (or a gesture-handler press, which hit-tests natively).
- **Seen in both builds, so not this flag:** a plain tap on the lowered scoreboard where Undo sits brought the controls back and also pressed Undo (the simulator tool's tap; a touch path with a little movement didn't). Not looked into, and not checked on a device.
- **Shipping:** it is compiled into the Reanimated pod, so it needs a new native build; an OTA can't carry it. It doesn't change the fingerprint (`@expo/fingerprint` reads only `package.json`'s `scripts`; 1f2cf54 with and without it), so the new build keeps the current runtime version, OTA updates reach old and new builds alike, and nothing fingerprint-based will ask for that build: start it by hand. Not checked on a phone.
- **Android too, since 2026-11-15** (Jay: gameplay and deals weren't smooth on Android): `ANDROID_SYNCHRONOUSLY_UPDATE_UI_PROPS` is on beside the iOS flag. Reanimated's Gradle build reads it from the same `package.json` field and compiles it into its native library. Without it every frame of every flight on Android went through a shadow-tree commit (clone, layout, diff, mount) and held still behind React's own commits, the stutter the iOS flag ended; Reanimated's performance guide recommends it for the same reason. The Android path covers transform and opacity, everything the flights animate, and writes them to the views directly. The caveat is iOS's: `measure` lags a slide, which the widened `pressRetentionOffset`s already cover; Android hit-testing reads the views' real transforms. It needs a new native build, which the fingerprint won't ask for (as above). Not checked on a device or emulator: no Android build of it has been made.
- **No Image fades in on Android** (the same day): React Native's Android Image fades in every bitmap that isn't an Android resource over 300ms (a Metro-served build's, and an OTA update's new images, which expo-updates hands over as `file://` paths), and a face's `onLoad`, which a landed ghost hands over on (`cardFacePaint.ts`), came while the face was still see-through. Every Image passes `fadeDuration={NO_IMAGE_FADE}` (`components/imageFade.ts`), pinned by `imageFade.test.ts`. JavaScript only, so it ships in an OTA.
- **How it was measured, to redo it:** no app code changed for the test. A scratch copy of the repo (node_modules symlinked) got the harness pieces (the clock bar, an auto New Game, scripted presses read from a seeded setting, a `measure` probe), its bundle went through `expo export:embed` and `hermesc -emit-binary -O` into copies of the Release apps, with the copy's assets added at the app root (the symlink changes the node_modules asset paths) and `EXUpdatesEnabled` off in `Expo.plist`, so an OTA could not replace the bundle. The store was seeded before each launch under the `solitaire:` prefix; `store/seed/write-store.py` still writes the old `persist:` keys.

## Every dealt card leaked through its drag gesture (2026-10-18)

Jay: the game "starts to struggle as I keep pressing New Game". It was a real leak: every card a deal unmounted stayed in memory for the rest of the session, with its fiber, its worklets, its shared values and (through its animated ref's `ShadowNodeWrapper`) its native views, about 60 cards and 3.6MB of native memory per New Game in Klondike. Garbage-collection pauses grew with the live heap until play stuttered.
- **The cause:** the card drag gestures (`PileView`'s `DraggableTableauCard`, `DraggableCard`) called `runOnJS(handleStart)` / `runOnJS(handleEnd)` with the component's own callbacks. react-native-worklets keeps a native strong reference to every `runOnJS` target for as long as the worklet's serialized copy lives, and Hermes counts that as a GC root. The copy is cached for as long as the component holds its gesture, and the callback's closure holds the component, so root -> callback -> component -> gesture -> cached copy -> root: nothing in the loop is ever collected. Neither the WeakMap cache nor gesture-handler's `animatedHandlers` shared value is the retainer (patching each, one at a time, changed nothing).
- **The fix:** `callWorkletCallback`/`useWorkletCallback` from `@rific/core` 0.5.0 (they started as this app's own `components/dragdrop/workletCallbacks.ts`, which was deleted once core shipped a superset: any arguments, and the callback need not be stable). A worklet calls only the module-level `callWorkletCallback(key, ...args)`; the component registers its live callback under a `useId` key with `useWorkletCallback(callback)` (in an effect, removed on unmount). **Never hand `runOnJS`/`scheduleOnRN` a callback the same component also reaches its worklet from** (a gesture, an animated style, an event handler it holds). A one-shot animation callback the component doesn't hold (`dimScrimAnimation`'s `onDone`) can't form the loop. Since 2026-10-18 every call is `scheduleOnRN(callWorkletCallback, key, ...args)` from `react-native-worklets` (`runOnJS` is deprecated in Reanimated 4), and `dimScrimAnimation`'s `onDone` is `scheduleOnRN(onDone)` with no key.
- **Libraries fixed the same way (2026-10-18):** `@rific/toaster` 0.9.0 (its dismiss reaction held `handleDismiss` for every toast), `@rific/drawer` 0.8.0, `@rific/scroll-view` 1.1.0 and `@rific/resizable-input` 0.5.0 now use the same helper; this app installs toaster 0.9.0 (JS only, can ship in an OTA). Not verified on a device.
- **Measured** in a Debug build on the iOS 27 simulator, through heap snapshots taken over CDP (Metro's inspector, `HeapProfiler.takeHeapSnapshot`; the socket needs an `Origin: http://127.0.0.1:8081` header) while a harness copy dealt Klondike every 3 seconds: before, the heap grew 183 -> 431MB over 30 deals and the retained card worklets 60 a deal; after, 67.5 -> 80.7 -> 87.3MB at deals 10, 40 and 72, card worklets 26 -> 54 -> 58 (the live board plus the previous one), JS lag flat. In the Release build the leak showed as +18,000 malloc allocations a deal and JS lag rising from 254 to 346ms per second over 40 deals, with GC spikes growing to 900ms per second. The web build never leaked (no worklet runtime). Not checked on a device; the fix is JS only, so it can ship in an OTA.

## Solitaire on @tastic/shell (2026-10-29 to 2026-11-03)

The CardTastic move's step 2 (TODO.md, "Step 2 in detail", done in five steps): Solitaire is a `@tastic/shell` pack, like DuelTastic and PuckTastic. The root, Home, the stats screen, Profiles, the store and the player are the shell's; the eleven play screens are Solitaire's (`withGameProviders`, `GameChrome` and all of `src/games/` and `src/engine/`), and so is everything the shell's screens show. Needs `@tastic/shell` 0.4.0 (0.2.1's swipe-away resume fix, see the "Last active game" bullet, 0.3.0's setup screen on the pack's felt, for RatSlap, and 0.4.0's Home tabs) and `@tastic/hud` 0.27.1 (its Skia-free `/lobby`, which the shell's barrel loads, and the seat panel's options the setup screen needs), plus the shell's other peers: `@tastic/split-screen` 0.5.5 and `@tastic/edge-guard` 0.1.5 (plain JS; edge-guard's config plugin is NOT in `app.json` and must not be), `@rific/auto-paper` 0.13.4, `@rific/toaster` 0.10.1. `@tastic/profile` stays on 0.16.2 (native). The native fingerprint is unchanged by the whole move (iOS `65ef1391...`, Android `5d07ab28...`, the same as 68be3d7; checked 2026-11-03 with `npx expo-updates fingerprint:generate`), so it could ship in an OTA update to 1.2.8 (the plan from 2026-10-29). Jay chose a development build on his phone first instead (2026-11-03, TODO.md's Decisions for Jay 2). Check the fingerprint again if anything native changes before an OTA.

**Where things are**
- **`src/pack.ts`** is the `PackDefinition`: `storeSource` (below), the name and `wordmark` (Home's title and subtitle), `colorCaption` ("Solitaire only"), the eleven games in Home's order, `HeroTitle`, `guestLabel` GUEST and `nullLabel` Guest, `usePickerAlign` (`useFrameAutoAlign`), `SettingsSections`, the stats options, `newProfileColor` (`DEFAULT_SEED_COLOR`) and the root pieces. No cast: every game is a full `GuidelessGameDefinition`.
- **`src/redux/store.ts`** builds the store: `createPackStore(storeSource, storeConfig)`. Not in `pack.ts`, because the pack holds every game's screen and the screens reach the store (`stores/gameInProgress.ts`): built there it would be an import cycle. `storeSource` is `seatColors` (`constants/player.ts`' `SEAT_COLORS`: seat 1 `DEFAULT_SEED_COLOR`, seat 2 Material Blue 500, unused until Ratscrew), `seatTheme` (`components/theme.ts`' `playerTheme`, so a fresh install starts on the muted seed), `reducers` (Solitaire's own keys, `redux/packReducers.ts`) and the eleven game ids, each `resumable` (so the shell mounts its `lastActiveGame` key, the same key, shape and action types Solitaire's own had). `storeConfig`: the `solitaire:` prefix, `migrate` (below), `blacklist` and `serializableIgnoredPaths` `['gameInProgress']` (each game's saved boards keep their own entries) and `middleware: [playerSettingsMiddleware]`. `RootState` is the shell's `ShellState` plus `PackReducersState`, and the store is cast to it (`createPackStore` types its state from the shell's keys alone). Everything imports the store from here, and screen tests swap this module.
- **`src/components/packRoot.tsx`** holds the root pieces' hooks and providers: `SolitaireProvider` (the pack's `Provider`: `GameStatsProvider`, `ProfileThemeSync`, `ReducedMotionBridge`), `useScreenOptions` (the light, rotation-aware status bar, and a crossfade under Reduce Motion; it leaves `animation` out otherwise, or it would undo the shell's Android crossfade), `useFeedbackSounds` (the card and invalid-move sounds) and `packGuide` (the three How to Play cards as the pack's; the seen version stays Solitaire's own `guide` key). The rest of the root is plain values in `pack.ts`: `Backdrop` (`TableBackground`), `foreground` (`CHROME_FG`), `Toaster` (`ThemedToaster`), `portraitWhileKeyboard`, `edgeGuard: false`, `previewColor` (`previewCardBack`), `fonts` (`components/cardFont.ts`' `CARD_FONTS`) and `fontFamily`, `otaVersion`.
- **Each game is `src/games/<id>/definition.ts`** (`SolitaireGameDefinition`, `games/shared/gameDefinition.tsx`): id, title (`GAME_TITLES`), Home blurb, `PlayScreen` (its `<Game>Screen`), `resumable`, `keepStatusBar` (the bar shows upright in a game), `Preview` (`gamePreview`: its `previewLayout.ts` deal, built once, through `GamePreview` on the felt), `Rules` (`gameRules`: `RulesDialog` with its rules and no par) and `StatsSections` (`gameStatsSections`). No seats, no loadout, no How to Play of its own.
- **Routes** (`src/app/`): `_layout.tsx` is the shell's `RootLayout` (handed the pack, `packStore` and the persistor), `index.tsx` its `HomeScreen`, `play/[game].tsx` its `PlayRoute` (with `generateStaticParams` from `games`, for the static web export), `stats.tsx` its `StatsScreen` and `profiles.tsx` its `ProfilesRoute`. The web addresses are `/solitaire/play/<game>` and `/solitaire/stats`; the old `/solitaire/<game>` and `/solitaire/achievements` were let go (Jay).
- **The stats screen's content** is `src/components/SolitaireStats.tsx`: `SolitaireStatsSections` (the pack's `StatsSections`: the calendar, Overall or the game's totals, Activity), `gameStatsSections(id)` (a game's `StatsSections`: a well per rule set), `RecordsTab` (the pack's `statsTabs`: the Best Score | Fastest switch over `RecordsList`), `SolitaireAchievementList` (the pack's `AchievementList`, the game picker hidden there) and `statsReset` (the pack's `resetStats`: one player's stats, achievements and saves, or everyone's on Everyone). With `guestStats: true` (guest's stats are their own, the screen opens on whoever is playing) and `statsGameTitles: false` (each rule set's well already names its game).
- **Settings:** every Settings sheet is the shell's `SettingsDialog`: first the rules of the game it's opened in (the pack's `useSettingsRules`, since 2026-11-10, see "Rules in Settings"), then its own rows (How to Play on a setup screen, Appearance, Blur, Sound, Haptics, Keep Awake, the version; labeled GENERAL under the rules), then Solitaire's Gameplay and Cards (the pack's `SettingsSections`, `components/SettingsSections.tsx`). `components/SettingsDialog.tsx` only re-exports the shell's, so a screen test stands it in with one mock and never loads the shell's barrel.

**What the shell does for Solitaire** (each built from Solitaire's own, and tested in the package)
- The root: the gesture root and safe area, `Fonts` (the 'fonts' gate, a failed load counting as loaded, the web tab title), the store and PersistGate with `FeedbackBridge` (`paper={RNPaper}`), `KeepAwakeBridge` and an `EdgeGuardBridge` that never guards, `Theme` (auto-paper with `expoBlur`, `maskedView`, `statusBar={false}`, `reanimated` and the pack's `fontFamily`; the 'theme' gate and the splash fade), `ProfilesProvider` (the App Group reconcile and sync, `pruneToRoster`) and its 'profiles' gate, `OrientationBridge` (`freezeWhileLocked`, the lock's angle in `appSettings`), the toast provider and the pack's toaster in a turned, pass-through frame, `GuideHost`, the update dialog, and the stack over the pack's `Backdrop` (both navigation layers transparent, the root window kept dark, Android crossfading).
- Home: the one-shot cold-launch resume before the menu mounts (`lastActiveGame`), the pack's How to Play on a first launch (`autoShowPack`, held back while a launch resumes a deal), the turned frame, the upright column and the wide two-pane grid (held while Home is covered), every preview held back a frame, the trophy and cog, seat 1's button with its Color page, and a tile's rules sheet in an in-frame host.
- The play route: sets the resume as a play screen mounts and clears it as it unmounts (the web pop unmounts it too); the game screens and `GameChrome` no longer touch it.
- The stats screen and Profiles: the tabs, the player and game pickers, opening on whoever is playing, the press-away over the header, the turned frame with the column capped at 640pt sideways; Profiles' roster with the swatch placed by the pack's `usePickerAlign` inside that column.

**Decisions to know about**
- **`ProfileThemeSync` stays at the root, on purpose.** The shell's Home applies `seatTheme` itself (`useSeatColors`), but only while it's mounted, and a cold launch that resumes a deal replaces Home with the game. Without the root sync a dark-mode change (or the player's own Appearance) during that game would leave every dialog on the other mode's seed until Home came back. Both write the same `playerTheme`, so neither undoes the other.
- **Narrowed to one game, the Stats tab doesn't show that game's unplayed rule sets.** The shell hands a game's `StatsSections` only the player, not the game picker, so each game shows its default rules plus any played, whichever way the page is narrowed (Solitaire's own screen showed all of a game's rules once narrowed to it). Leaving the rule sets in the pack's section instead would leave each game an empty section, and the page spaces every section (12pt each, 132pt of blank space). Fix in the shell: pass the game picker to a game's `StatsSections` too, then `gameStatsSections` can show every rule set when it's the only game shown. On All Games the rule sets come in Home's order (they came in `VARIANTS` order).
- **The update dialog is the shell's:** it portals into the app-root host and turns itself, where Solitaire's sat in an in-frame host (see the dialogs bullet in the rotation section).
- **Smaller changes from the shell's root:** the toast provider gets expo-haptics while Vibrate is on (only the toaster's history and clear chips tick, and both are off); auto-paper gets Reanimated (its Dialog's `animatedStyle` now does something; nothing here passes one); `GameStatsProvider` mounts before the 'profiles' gate opens (it only records on game events, which come after). Home's tile names and blurbs are Paper's `titleLarge`/`bodyMedium` (`titleMedium`/`bodySmall` wide), a size up from Solitaire's own 20/13, and the tiles are buttons, not `<Link>`s.

**The player**
- Seat 1 of the shell's `profileSelection` is who is playing, and the color picks are its `seatColors` (`@tastic/profile`'s `createSeatColorsSlice`, which began as Solitaire's `tableColorSlice`). `stores/profiles.tsx` is `usePlayer()` (`profileId`, `profile`, `color`, `setColor`, `select`) and a re-export of the shell's `useProfiles` (the roster and its edits). `usePlayer` reads through selectors with an equality check on the profile, not the shell's profiles context: the shell replaces the whole roster on every foreground resume, and every card reads the player. A sibling app's new color for a profile drops its Solitaire pick too (seatColors handles `setAll`), as one made on this app's Profiles screen always did.
- **Lock Orientation** (gone since 2026-11-06, see "The OS turns") was the shell's `appSettings` (`useAppSettings()`). It still followed the player: filed inside each player's `settings` (`playerSettingsMiddleware` watches seat 1 before and after every action: a pick, `clearProfile` after a delete, `pruneToRoster` after a sibling app's delete; a rehydrate isn't a change of player).
- **A deleted profile** is forgotten by the reducers, not by a call: `onProfileRemoved` wraps `achievements` (`forgetProfileAchievements(dropProfileStats)`), `gameInProgress` (each slot through its own reducer, so its entry is rewritten), `recentWins`, `topWins` and `playerSettings`, and the shell's `seatColors` drops the pick itself. They answer `@tastic/profile`'s remove, which only the shell's `deleteProfile` dispatches, never a roster sync. `resetPlayer` stays (`stores/playerData.ts`).
- **`GUEST_PLAYER_ID`** is `constants/player.ts`' own `'guest'`, pinned equal to the shell's by `store.test.ts`: the slices and the saved-games module don't load the shell's whole barrel for one string (a test that mocks part of `@tastic/profile` or `@rific/feedback-press` can't load it).

**Saved data from 1.2.8**
- **The reshape** (`redux/shellMigrate.ts`, wrapping the guide's returning-player migrate, so a pre-Redux player is still grandfathered and the early-write rule still holds): while a saved root has `activeProfile` or `tableColor` and no `profileSelection`, it adds `profileSelection` `{ 1: activeProfile, 2: null }` and `seatColors` (guest's pick as seat 1's guest color, else `DEFAULT_SEED_COLOR`; each profile's pick beside the profile's color from the saved roster, dropping a pick for a profile the roster no longer has or one equal to its own color), drops `activeProfile` and `tableColor`, and drops Lock Orientation (`lockOrientation`, `lockedRotation`) from `settings` and from every player's filed settings (`playerSettings`). Everything else was already the shell's shape or Solitaire's own, `lastActiveGame` included. It runs on every launch (there's no persist version) and acts once.
- **History: the rollback keys (2026-10-29 to 2026-11-06).** For one release the old keys stayed in `solitaire:root` (reducers that only kept what they rehydrated, `activeProfileSlice.ts` and `tableColorSlice.ts`, and a 1.2.8 lock left in `settings`, `LegacySettingsLock`), so an OTA rolled back to 1.2.8 found each player, pick and lock as they were at the move. The build that lets the OS turn the screen (see "The OS turns") has a new native fingerprint, so 1.2.8's JS can't be rolled back onto it: Jay had them deleted on 2026-11-06, and the reshape now drops the old keys and the lock itself. A store a dev build reshaped before then still has the old keys on disk; with no reducer for them, the root reducer drops them on the next write (a one-time "unexpected keys" warning in a dev build).

**Tests:** `store.test.ts` (the keys, the saved boards out of the root entry, a 1.2.8 save reshaped through real redux-persist with a second launch changing nothing; the rollback cases went with the rollback keys on 2026-11-06), `playerSettingsSlice.test.ts`, `stores/profiles.test.tsx` (over the shell's `ProfilesProvider`), `stores/playerData.test.tsx`, `stores/gameInProgress.test.ts`, `settingsSlice.test.ts`, `guideSlice.test.ts` (the wrapped migrate still grandfathers), `pack.test.ts` (the eleven games in Home's order, the same ids as the stats and the store, each resumable with a preview, rules, a play screen and its stats, no seats, loadout or guide of its own, the pack's guide, root pieces and stats options), `heroTitle.test.ts` (the fan's scale in the wide pane), `solitaireStats.test.tsx` (the stats pieces on their own) and `statusBar.test.tsx`'s source scan. What the shell's screens do is tested in the package: Solitaire's Home, Settings-row, profile-panel, resume, stats-screen and Profiles tests went with the screens. The suites ran with a 60s per-test timeout while the machine's load average was in the hundreds (other sessions), where a plain `npm run verify` timed out in a dozen suites; 125 suites, 1,933 tests.

**Seen on the web.** On a `WEB_BASE_URL=/solitaire` export and the web dev server (the Browser pane, 402x874, 375x812 and 874x402, desktop, Sound off but for one stubbed check): a store seeded in 1.2.8's shape opening with its player, pick, lock, settings and saved deal; a fresh store's Home with How to Play over it, Skip writing the seen version; every preview; a tile's rules sheet; a tile opening `/play/klondike`, Home clearing the resume, and a reload at `/` resuming the deal with no guide; both kinds of Settings sheet, How to Play replaying from Home's; picking a profile recoloring the felt, backs, chip and theme live, its menu's Color row captioned; the wide layout; toasts at the top between the corner buttons; a press playing the card sound; `/stats` opening on the player with all three tabs, the pickers and Reset following them; `/profiles`. No console errors once loaded.

**Seen on the iOS 27 simulator (2026-11-03).** 1.2.8's JS (68be3d7) with a 1.2.8 store written in before its first launch (Dad playing, a pink pick over his blue, the lock on at 90, dark, Kid's settings filed) opened sideways as Dad, and a Draw 3 move in Klondike saved a deal. This build installed over it opened straight into that deal at 90, and `solitaire:root` on disk held the reshape (seat 1 Dad, the lock and its angle in `appSettings`, the old keys beside them). Sideways: the wide Home; seat 1's menu opening above its button, on screen; picking Kid (navy felt, orange backs, light, Draw 1, unlocked; on disk Dad's settings filed with his lock); Stats opening on Kid with the three tabs, both pickers and the column capped; Profiles turned. Upright (Kid unlocked, a relaunch): Home; Klondike with its light status bar (`keepStatusBar`); a tap sending an Ace up and the cascade the next; the game's Settings sheet with Kid's rows; Home and back (a native pop and push) with Kid's deal resuming; a cold launch dropping straight into it, and Home from there. Picking Dad again turned the screen to his locked 90 with the status bar hidden, and his 1.2.8 deal resumed sideways in Draw 3. No crash or red box. How, with no native build: an earlier Release build (1.2.4) with the same fingerprint (`65ef1391...`) got each JS bundle (`expo export:embed`, then `hermesc`) with `EXUpdatesEnabled` off, on a throwaway simulator, the store written straight into AsyncStorage's files (`Library/Application Support/<bundle id>/RCTAsyncLocalStorage_V1`: a value over 1,024 characters goes in a file named by the key's MD5, `null` in `manifest.json`), sound off. The simulator tool's taps often show a screen or two late: wait a moment before taking a missed tap for a bug.

**Not checked:** Android, a device, a reset or a profile edit carried out.

## RatSlap (2026-11-04)

The first game here for two players (TODO.md's CardTastic step 3): Egyptian Ratscrew, named RatSlap (Jay). `src/games/ratslap/`, last on Home. Uncommitted and unreleased as of this note; the native fingerprint is unchanged (iOS `65ef1391...`, Android `5d07ab28...`).

**Rules (Jay, 2026-11-04)**, in `engine/rules.ts`, pure functions over one `RatSlapState` (each returns a new state, or null for a move the rules refuse):
- 52 cards, or 54 with the two jokers, split evenly. Seat 1 plays first in a session; a rematch starts with the last match's loser (`createInitialState`'s `firstPlayer`).
- A J, Q, K or A gives the other player 1, 2, 3 or 4 tries to play one of their own; none, and whoever played it wins the pile. An answer turns it round.
- Jokers (house rule): an ordinary card in plain play; during a challenge one cancels it however many tries are used. Two jokers are a double, joker-card-joker a sandwich.
- Doubles, sandwiches and the jokers are each switchable (`RatSlapRules`, `Settings.ratslapDoubles`/`Sandwiches`/`Jokers`), all on by default.
- A good slap wins the pile, mid-challenge too, and still beats a face-card win while that pile waits to be collected (`pileWonBySlap`: a slapped pile is settled). A wrong slap burns the slapper's top card face up under the pile, so everyone sees what it cost (`burned`, apart from `pile`, so burned cards never make a slap and go to the pile's winner; face up since 2026-11-04, Jay: they were face down).
- A won pile goes to the BOTTOM of the winner's deck, turned over, and the winner plays next.
- Out of cards, you can't play (the other plays every turn), but a good slap gets you back in. You lose when the other player collects a pile while you're out, or if you slap wrong with nothing to burn. A challenge whose defender is out is won at once; once neither player has a card, the pile goes to whoever played its top card.

**What the port fixed** in `~/Developer/EgyptianRatscrew`'s engine (its 56 tests ported, the ones pinning the old behavior rewritten): a won pile went on TOP of the hand (the winner replayed the same cards), played cards never turned face up, a burned card plus one played card could make a "double", doubles were always on and the deck always had jokers, a slap could never beat a face-card win, and an empty hand deadlocked the game (nobody could play, nothing ended it).

**The CPU** (`engine/cpu.ts`, pure; run by `useRatSlapMatch`): three levels (`CPU_STYLES`: play pause, slap reaction, a miss chance, and a rare wrong slap on Easy and Medium), picked on player 1's opponent menu in the setup screen (`Settings.ratslapCpu`). It looks at each new pile once (`cpuSlapPlan`) and, meaning to slap, doesn't play over the pile meanwhile. A quick adult reacts in 250-400ms, so Hard (380-600ms) can be beaten and Easy leaves a child time. A simulation of CPU-vs-CPU matches (`cpu.test.ts`) ends every match; Medium against Medium splits about 50/50, and the quicker slapper wins about 98% of matches, so slap speed is nearly the whole game. A match runs about 200 cards at the median (2-3 minutes) and about 550 at the 90th percentile.

**The table** (`SlapTable.tsx`, which `RatSlapScreen.tsx` renders on the round's rules, and Slapjack's screen on its own) is not GameChrome and not `withGameProviders`: it never turns with the phone (it lies flat between two players at its short edges), so it lays itself out in plain screen coordinates (`layout.ts`, sized from the table's own `onLayout`, since the web's window reading can lag a resize) and moves its own cards (`games/table/TableCards.tsx`, shared with the grid games since). `CARD_ASPECT` is a card's width over its height.
- **Every card off the decks is ONE view for its whole time on the table** (2026-11-04, Jay saw pile cards disappear): it mounts over the deck it left (the `origins` map, set by the `played`/`burned` events, read once at mount), turns face up as it moves onto the pile, glides along the fan as newer cards land, and turns face down as it's carried to the winner's deck (`departures`, removed `DEPARTURE_MS` after, by when it lies hidden under that deck). The first version handed a flying ghost to a separately mounted pile card and drew only the top 4, so the 5th card down (peeking out from its tilt) vanished at every play, and a won pile's lower cards vanished at once while one card flew. Never put the pile back on ghosts. Each view is card-sized (never a zero frame), the decks are drawn over the cards (a played card slides out from under its deck, a won pile slides under), and the gestures reach JS only through `callWorkletCallback`. Checked in headless Chrome over CDP: 2,300 frames of a match with plays, slaps, burns and collects, no card leaving the DOM anywhere but over a deck or squared under the fan, and every card first drawn over a deck.
- **The pile fans its top three side by side** (`PILE_FAN`, newest on the right, `pileStep` = the draw-3 waste step plus room for two cards' tilt, so a '10' shows whole), centered on the table, so a double or a sandwich can be read at a glance (Jay: the old tilted heap hid the second and third cards). Cards under the fan lie square under its last card, and `PILE_DRAWN` (5) are drawn, so the one dropping off the bottom is under two others. Tilt is at most 2.5 degrees (`pileTilt`). Burned cards lie face up under the bottom of the pile, raised by `burnedLift` so their corner row shows above it, and dimmed (they never count toward a slap); `tableLayout` sizes cards so that row clears the top deck's challenge line. Only the compact face's top-left index exists, so in a two-player match player 2 reads the pile upside down, as before.
- Player 1 at the bottom, player 2 or the CPU at the top. A tap on your own deck plays your top card; any other tap in your half is a slap. Against the CPU the whole table is yours to slap on. Each person's half says "Slap anywhere on your side" (`SlapLabel`, in the felt between the pile and the deck, `slapArea`; left out where that's under 28pt, and hidden while that side's "Slap!" or "Wrong slap" shows in its place), and a hand grows and fades where each slap landed (the CPU's over the pile), so it's clear where to slap and whose slap counted (Jay, 2026-11-04: he wasn't sure where to slap). Two people get a `GestureDetector` zone each (two fingers never race, PuckTastic's pattern), and the top half's labels, calls and result card face the top player.
- **Zones fire at touch-down (`onBegin`), never `onStart`:** a pan only starts once the finger moves on iOS, so a still tap never played a card there (found on the simulator; the web was fine).
- The deck whose turn it is glows (a breathing halo under the deck, see "The table games' glow" at the end; a ring round it until 2026-11-07); the defender's deck says "Q · 2 tries left". Home and Settings sit at the sides of the midline. A won pile is collected after a beat (`SLAP_COLLECT_BEAT_MS` 550, `CHALLENGE_COLLECT_BEAT_MS` 1100, the window in which a face-card win can still be slapped away). Everything holds while Settings is open or the app is in the background. `useKeepAwake('game')`; the status bar is hidden (`keepStatusBar: false`).
- Jokers have no face bitmap: `PlayingCard` takes any card (`AnyCard`) and draws a joker live (`components/JokerFace.tsx`: JOKER down the corners, a star, `joker-1` red), reporting itself painted at once.

**In the pack:** `definition.ts` (`seatTwo: ['cpu', 'person']`, `twoPlayerSeats` face to face on the web too, `useLoadout`, its own `guide`, `resumable: false`, a live `RatSlapPreview` instead of the baked face layer). Home opens it through the shell's setup screen (`src/app/loadout/[game].tsx`, new): seat 2's menu (the CPU at each level, Player 2, the profiles) and the rules as three switches (`useRatSlapLoadout.tsx`, from hud's `/lobby` only; `hudImports.test.ts` allows it). The match plays on the snapshot taken at Ready (`useActiveRound`); a direct link plays the player's own settings against the CPU. The store gets its `ratslapGuide` key from `redux/store.ts`' `RATSLAP_STORE`, since the store can't import the definition (it holds the screen). `SOLITAIRE_GAME_IDS` stays the eleven.
- **How to Play** (`guideSteps.tsx`, `guide.ts`): up to four cards on the tile's first tap (Tap Your Deck, Face Cards, Slap the Pile, Jokers), following the rules switched on, under the guide copy rules.
- **Rules sheet:** `RulesDialog`'s `scoring={false}` leaves out the moves-plus-seconds paragraph. **Settings sheet:** inside any two-player game (`usePathname()` ends in one of `MATCH_GAME_IDS`) the GAMEPLAY rows for foundations, hints and unplayable cards are left out; Reduce Motion and CARDS stay.
