- **Stats** (`redux/matchStatsSlice.ts`, one entry per two-player game since Slapjack, among Solitaire's own keys, not the shell's `createGameStats`, which has no per-player reset): everyone's and each player's matches against the CPU by level and against people, slaps won and wrong slaps, recorded at the end of a finished match (a match left early isn't). A seat 2 guest keeps nothing of its own. A player's reset, Reset Everything and a deleted profile reach it. Its well on the Stats tab (`RatSlapStats.tsx`, on the shared `games/table/MatchStatsSection.tsx`) names itself; `SolitaireStatsSections` draws nothing when the game picker is on RatSlap; Records, the calendar, the totals and the achievements stay solitaire-only.

**Tests:** `games/ratslap/{rules,slapLogic,deck,cpu,useRatSlapMatch,layout,guideSteps}.test.*` (the simulation is in `cpu.test.ts`), `redux/matchStatsSlice.test.ts`, `pack.test.ts` and `store.test.ts`. **Seen on the web** (402x874): a whole match against a Medium CPU played by a script to "You Won!" with fireworks and the result recorded, Rematch with the CPU first, two players face to face with both slapping in one moment (the first handled wins, the second is ignored without a burn), the setup screen, the tile's How to Play, the Stats well and its game picker, and the Settings sheet. **Seen on the iOS 27 simulator:** today's bundle in the 1.2.4 Release build (same fingerprint): native taps playing cards, the CPU answering a challenge, the pile's tilted cards and the challenge marker. **The setup screen** is the shell's (0.3.0 on hud 0.27.1): on the pack's felt, seat buttons on surface pills, Guest labels, muted seat colors, menus placed by `usePickerAlign` in the one-player row; seen on the web, one player and two. **Not checked:** a device, a two-person match on native, Android.

## Slapjack, Six-Card Golf and Trash (2026-11-04)

Three more two-player games, the same day as RatSlap and on its pieces, last on Home after it (Jay asked for "Trash, golf, slapjack, etc"). Each is for two people face to face or one against the CPU in three levels, picked on the shell's setup screen; nothing is saved mid-match. Uncommitted and unreleased as of this note; no dependency changed, so the native fingerprint is the same.

**Jay's rules choices (2026-11-04):** multiplayer Golf is **Six-Card Golf** (two rows of three, two turned up to start, nothing hidden, so it plays on one phone; solitaire Golf keeps the name "Golf"). **Trash** has Jacks wild (a Jack fills any face-down spot; a Queen or King ends the turn). A wrong slap in **Slapjack** burns a card under the pile, as in RatSlap. Since 2026-11-06 Trash's wild (Jacks or Kings) and Slapjack's penalty (burn, or give the card to the other player) are settings, see "Rule choices and suit marks". The rest are the common rules, picked without asking: Golf's drawn card can be discarded (with no flip after), a pair in a column scores 0 (2s included), the other player gets one last turn after someone has all six up, and the lowest total after 1, 3 or 9 holes wins (ties possible); Trash's round ends the moment someone fills every spot, its winner plays the next with one spot fewer and its loser goes first, and winning a round with one spot wins the match (Ace to 10, or Ace to 5 for a short game).

**What's shared** (`src/games/table/`):
- `cpuLevel.ts` (the levels, labels and setup-menu entries), `players.ts` (`PlayerIndex`, who's human), `geometry.ts` (`Point`/`Pose`/`Rect`, `cardTilt`).
- `TableCards.tsx`: RatSlap's every-card-is-one-view layer, moved here unchanged except a `delayMs` (a deal's stagger) and a `testID` prefix. The grid games use it the same way: a card goes from the stock to the hand to a grid to the discard as one view. A new deal remounts it (`key={deal}`) so every card flies in from the stock.
- `TableChrome.tsx`: `useTableSeats` (seat 1's player or Guest, seat 2's profile, Player 2 or the CPU, and whose results are kept), `SeatBadge`, `TableSideButtons` (Home and Settings at the midline's ends), `TableOverlay` (a result card, one per person each facing them, used for RatSlap's result too), `useAppActive`.
- `LoadoutPills.tsx`: the setup screen's pill row, as switches (RatSlap's rules) or a choice (Golf's holes, Trash's spots). `MatchStatsSection.tsx`: a two-player game's Stats well.
- `redux/matchStatsSlice.ts` replaced `ratslapStatsSlice.ts` (RatSlap was unreleased, so its `ratslapStats` key was dropped without a migration; a browser that had played it logs redux's "unexpected key" once in dev). One entry per game (`MATCH_GAME_IDS`), each with everyone's and each player's record by CPU level and against people (won, lost, tied), plus the game's own `counts` (RatSlap's and Slapjack's slaps, Golf's `holes`, Trash's `rounds`) and `lows` (Golf's `bestHole`). A player's reset, Reset Everything and a deleted profile reach every game.

**Slapjack** is RatSlap's engine under `SLAPJACK_RULESET` (`engine/types.ts`: two new fields, `faceCards` off and `jacks` on, no doubles, sandwiches or jokers; `slapPattern` returns `'jack'`) and RatSlap's table: `RatSlapScreen.tsx` became `SlapTable.tsx`, taking the game, the rules and seat 2, and both screens render it. Its setup screen had only seat 2's menu until its wrong-slap penalty became a pill (2026-11-06). A simulation of CPU matches (`cpu.test.ts`) ends every one.

**The grid games** (Six-Card Golf and Trash) share `table/gridLayout.ts` (each player's rows near their own edge, their name row at the edge, a prompt line toward the middle, the stock and discard across the middle with the card in hand between them nudged toward whoever holds it; face to face, player 2's side is player 1's turned half a turn round the middle; against the CPU it's upright so the person can read it) and `table/TableView.tsx` (`GridTable.tsx` until War and Speed widened it: the cards, a glow under every card the player whose move it is can tap (a ring round it until 2026-11-07), a transparent `Pressable` over each, name rows, prompts, notes like "Hole 1 of 9", Trash's rank chips on face-down spots). Each game's screen decides the cards, targets and lines; its `use*Match` hook holds the state, runs the CPU one step at a time on a timer (a step is skipped if the state moved on), and holds everything while the cards are dealt (`useSettled(deal, dealMs)`). The engines are pure (`engine/rules.ts`, `engine/cpu.ts`); a face-down card's worth to Golf's CPU is the deck's average (71/13), it holds back its last face-down card unless it's ahead (or the hole has run past 30 turns), and Trash's Hard CPU puts a Jack in the spot whose rank it has seen most of. Trash auto-discards a card with no spot after `NO_SPOT_MS` (900), whoever holds it, so it's seen first.

**Pinned by** `games/sixcardgolf/rules.test.ts` and `games/trash/rules.test.ts` (rules, the CPU's choices, and CPU-vs-CPU matches that all end with every card accounted for), `games/ratslap/rules.test.ts`'s Slapjack block, `games/table/gridLayout.test.ts` (four phone sizes), `games/table/guideSteps.test.tsx`, `redux/matchStatsSlice.test.ts`, and `pack.test.ts`/`store.test.ts`. **Seen on the web** (402x874, the browser pane hidden, so animations crawled): each table against the CPU, a scripted one-hole Golf match to its result card with the loss recorded, a scripted Ace-to-5 Trash round to its round card and the next round dealing the winner one spot fewer, Trash face to face (player 2's side turned), Slapjack not slappable on a double, the four Home tiles, Trash's How to Play and setup pills, and the four Stats wells. **Not checked:** a device, the deal and flip animations at full frame rate, a full 9-hole match, Android.

**Home's tabs (2026-11-05):** Home shows the games on two tabs, Solitaire and Two Player (`pack.ts`' `homeTabs`: a game with a seat 2 is a two-player game), each in Home's order, opening on Solitaire. Jay chose tabs over a section heading or a per-tile player count ("does there need to be an indication of players if there are already tabs?"), so the tiles say nothing about players and the four two-player blurbs lost their "Two players." The switch is the shell's (`PackDefinition.homeTabs`, `@tastic/shell` 0.4.0, released 2026-11-05): the stats screen's look, under the player button upright and over the grid wide, the other tab's games hidden rather than unmounted, the tab kept while a game covers Home and across a turn. Pinned by `pack.test.ts` and the shell's `homeTabs.test.tsx`. Seen on the web dev server against the shell's packed tarball (402x874 and 1280x800, dark): both tabs, a switch each way, the wide grid keeping the tab across the resize, no console errors. Not seen on a device.

**Not built:** achievements or calendar days for the two-player games.

## War and Speed (2026-11-06)

Two more two-player games on the same pieces, after Slapjack in the Two Player tab: Speed (played at once, like the slap games), then War, then the turn-by-turn ones. Not released; no dependency changed, so the native fingerprint is the same.

**Jay's rules choices (2026-11-05):**
- **War:** a tie is a war of three cards face down and one face up. The setup screen picks the length: Full Game (until someone has every card, the default) or Short Game (most cards after 26 battles, `SHORT_GAME_BATTLES`; it can end level). Aces are high.
- **Speed:** ranks wrap (a King on an Ace, an Ace on a King or a 2), and a hand refills by itself after every play.
- Since 2026-11-06 War's war (three down or one) and both of Speed's answers are settings, with the other answers offered beside them: see "Rule choices and suit marks".
- **Picked without asking:**
  - War: a player with fewer than four cards for a war plays what they have, the last face up, and one with none left loses the battle. A won battle goes under its winner's deck shuffled, since in order a War deck can go round for ever.
  - Speed: hands of five, draw piles of fifteen, side piles of five, one starter each. A card that fits both center piles goes on the one that leaves the rest of the hand the most to follow it with (`bestPile`). When nobody can play, the side piles turn by themselves after `STUCK_TURN_MS` (1100). Out of side cards, the center piles but their tops are shuffled into new ones. After `DEADLOCK_TURNS` (52) side cards turned with nobody able to play, or with nothing left to turn, fewer cards left wins (a tie when level).

**What changed in the shared pieces:**
- `table/GridTable.tsx` became `table/TableView.tsx`: its layout is any `TableFrame` (card size, center, label and prompt lines, notes, `upsideDown`), empty piles are a list (`emptySlots`), and it takes `rings` without tap targets, `overCards` (drawn over the moving cards: War's decks) and `touch` (a game's own touch zones, under the Home and Settings buttons).
- `table/TouchZones.tsx` is RatSlap's two-finger zones lifted out of `SlapTable.tsx`, which uses it now. It reports a touch at touch-down in table coordinates. React Native's own touch system takes one responder at a time, so two people tapping `Pressable`s at once would drop one; War and Speed use these zones for that reason.
- `table/DeckStack.tsx` draws a face-down deck as art (up to three backs, or an empty slot).
- `MatchStatsSection` takes `levels={false}` for War, whose CPU has no levels. Its matches against the CPU are kept under the middle level.

**War** (`src/games/war/`):
- A tap anywhere on your side plays your part of the step (your card, or a war's four); against the CPU, anywhere on the table.
- The CPU answers the person's card after `CPU_ANSWER_MS` (350). It has no levels, so player 1's opponent menu offers one plain CPU (no `cpuDifficulty`).
- A won battle is collected after a beat (`BATTLE_COLLECT_MS` 900, a war's `WAR_COLLECT_MS` 1400) and flies under its winner's deck as the same card views (`departures`, as in RatSlap).
- The decks are `DeckStack`s drawn over the battle cards. A full game ran 200 simulated matches to their end, a few hundred battles at the median.

**Speed** (`src/games/speed/`):
- A tap on a card in your hand plays it. One that fits nowhere buzzes and plays the invalid sound.
- Every card is a `TableCards` view from the deal on: a draw pile's top card goes to the empty hand slot, a side pile's onto its center pile.
- The CPU plays on its own clock (`SPEED_CPU_PACE`: Easy 1.1-1.6s a card, Medium 0.75-1.1s, Hard 0.48-0.72s). The person's plays don't reset it, so a quick person can't hold it off.
- The prompt line says nothing while you have a card to play; spotting one is the game, so no card glows.

**Pinned by** `games/war/rules.test.ts` and `games/speed/rules.test.ts` (the rules, and whole matches that all end with every card accounted for), `games/table/guideSteps.test.tsx`, `pack.test.ts` and `store.test.ts`. **Not checked:** a device, two people at once on a touch screen, the CPU's pace against a person.

## Gin Rummy (2026-11-06)

The first two-player game whose hands are hidden, so the first pass-and-play one: against the CPU in three levels (one person's table), or two people sharing the phone. Last on Home, after Trash. Not released; no dependency changed, so the native fingerprint is the same.

**Jay's rules choices (2026-11-06):** knock once your deadwood after your throw is 10 or less; gin and an undercut are each worth 25 (no Big Gin); the setup screen picks the target, 100 or 50 (`Settings.ginTarget`, `GIN_TARGETS`); the app marks your melds and shows your deadwood as you play. Later the same day each became a setting with the other answers offered beside it (Oklahoma, Gin Only, Big Gin, the older bonuses, one-hand matches, less meld help): see "Rule choices and suit marks". The rest are the standard rules, picked without asking: the non-dealer may take the first upcard or pass, then the dealer, and if both pass the non-dealer draws from the stock; the card just taken from the discard can't be thrown straight back; Aces are low (Q-K-A isn't a run); the defender lays off on the knocker's melds unless it's gin; the hand is a draw once the stock is down to two cards and the player who drew throws without knocking; the winner of a hand deals the next (so its loser plays first), the same dealer after a draw. No game bonus or line bonuses: the first to the target wins.

**Engine** (`src/games/ginrummy/engine/`, pure):
- `melds.ts`: `candidateMelds` (every set, each three of a four-card set, every run of three or more inside a longer one), `bestArrangement` (an exact search over every way to split the hand into melds, pruned by points, fewest deadwood points, then fewest cards left over), `bestDefence` (the defender's melds and lay-offs together, whichever leaves least: a lay-off goes on a run before a set, and runs grow a card at a time), `deadwoodWithout`.
- `rules.ts`: the state and moves (`takeDiscard`, `passUpcard`, `drawStock`, `throwCard`, `knock`, `nextHand`). A hand's cards are kept in no order; the table arranges them. `lastTurn` is what the last player did as the other could have seen it (where they drew from, the discard card taken, what they threw), which the pass cover and the CPU's line read; `pickups` is the discards each player has taken, which Hard reads.
- `cpu.ts`: every level throws the card that leaves the least deadwood and takes a discard that melds. Easy misses a useful discard and throws loosely 30% of the time, Medium 15% and 12%; Hard never does, takes a low card that cuts its deadwood by 6 or more, weighs its draw outs (`OUT_WORTH` 2 points per card that would meld two of its deadwood), avoids throwing what the other player looks to be collecting, and knocks with less as the stock runs down (10, then 7 under 19 cards, then 4 under 11). Simulated matches to 100 (150 each way): Hard beats Medium about 3 in 4, Medium beats Easy about 2 in 3, Hard beats Easy about 85%. Hard alone, without Medium's looseness, only broke even with a deadwood-minimizing Medium, which is why Medium plays loosely.

**The table** (`GinRummyScreen.tsx`, `layout.ts`): nobody sits across from anyone, so the table is drawn upright for whoever's turn it is (`seated`): their hand across the bottom, the other's face down across the top, the stock and the discard between, the buttons (Pass on the first upcard; Throw, and Knock or Gin, once a card is picked) over the hand. The hand is always arranged by the app: its melds, each with a line under it in the hint ring's color (`MeldLines`), then the deadwood by rank; there's no dragging to arrange it. A card in hand shows 0.52 of its width past the next (`HAND_STEP`, so its rank shows; its suit is marked under the rank, see "Rule choices and suit marks"), which sizes the cards from the width for eleven cards in four groups: 56pt on a 402pt phone. Tapping a card picks it (it rises, and the deadwood in the name row becomes what's left after throwing it); tapping it again puts it back. The hand's tap targets go left to right, the order the cards lie over each other, so a tap on a card's strip goes to that card. `TableView`'s `TableTarget` gained `ring: false` for them. Against the CPU its last turn shows under its hand ("Took the 7♦, threw the K♠"). When a hand ends, both hands lie face up in their melds (the cards the defender laid off in their own group, with a muted line) for `RESULT_MS` before the result card.

**Pass and play:** `holder` is who has the phone (seat 1 to start). When the turn goes to the other person, the table turns to them at once and every card lies face down, under `games/table/PassCover.tsx`: "Pass to Kid", what the last player did that the next one didn't see ("Dad took the 9♦ and threw the J♣.", "Dad passed on the 5♥.", "Hand 2: Kid plays first."), and one Ready, which hands them the phone (`holder`). Android back does nothing while it's up, and nothing else on the table can be reached. The result cards name both players (no "You") and show one card, upright. The setup screen still seats two people face to face on a phone (the shell does that for every two-player game); `twoPlayerSeats.faceToFaceOnWeb` is false, so the web seats them side by side.

**In the pack:** `definition.ts` (`seatTwo: ['cpu', 'person']`, the CPU's level and the target on the setup screen, `useGinLoadout.tsx`), How to Play (`guideSteps.tsx`: Make Melds, Draw Then Throw, Knock to End the Hand, Go Gin with the target, Pass the Phone), `GIN_RUMMY_RULES`, the `ginRummyGuide` store key (`TABLE_GAME_STORES`), `MATCH_GAME_IDS`' `ginrummy` with Hands Won, Gins and Undercuts on its Stats well (`GinRummyStats.tsx`), and a Home tile of a hand in its melds (`GinRummyPreview.tsx`).

**Pinned by** `games/ginrummy/rules.test.ts` (melds, the deal, the upcard, a taken card, a draw, knocking with lay-offs that chain, an undercut, gin, the target, the defender keeping a meld, the CPU's choices, and CPU-vs-CPU matches that all end with every card accounted for), `games/ginrummy/useGinMatch.test.tsx` (the CPU a step at a time after its pause, held while paused or dealing, a refused throw), `games/table/guideSteps.test.tsx`, `pack.test.ts` and `store.test.ts`. **Seen on the web** (402x874 and 1280x800, sound off): a hand against a Medium CPU played to a knock (my deadwood count matching an independent solver at every turn, the CPU taking my discard, the CPU laying off a card on my run, the result card and the next hand with the CPU first), and two people: the pass cover after a pass and after a throw, Ready showing the next hand, and Throw refused for the card just taken. **Not checked:** a device, a whole match to its end in the app, gin and an undercut in the app (only in tests), the How to Play and the Stats well on screen.

## Four Crowns (2026-11-06)

Jay's family's own rummy ("what we call Four Crowns which is just five crowns but with two regular decks of cards"), for two to four players, people passing the phone and CPUs in any mix. Last on Home, after Gin Rummy, in the Two Player tab (a name it outgrows). Not released; no dependency changed, so the native fingerprint is the same. The app calls it Four Crowns, never the boxed game's name.

**Jay's rules choices (2026-11-06):** up to four players; all 104 cards of two decks, no jokers; a card left in hand counts its number (Ace 1), J 11, Q 12, K 13, a wild 20; when someone goes out, every other player has one last turn. Later the same day the deck, the face cards' worth and the last turns became settings, with jokers and the round ending at once offered beside them: see "Rule choices and suit marks". The rest is the boxed game's shape, picked without asking: eleven rounds dealing 3 to 13 cards; the rank equal to the cards dealt is wild (3s, 4s, ... Jacks, Queens, Kings); books are three or more of a rank (suits can repeat, two decks), runs three or more of a suit in a row, Aces low; you go out when every card but your throw melds; the lowest total after eleven rounds wins and a tie shares it; the dealer moves a seat each round; the card just taken from the discard can't be thrown back (as in Gin); an empty stock is made again from the discard, all but its top card.

**Engine** (`src/games/fourcrowns/engine/`, pure):
- `melds.ts`' `bestArrangement(cards, wildRank)` is an exact search, memoized on the cards still to place and the wilds left, placing the lowest card each step: loose, the first card of a book (any of its rank's other cards, wilds to make three, the two decks' twins tried once), or the lowest natural card of a run in its suit (each rank above it the natural card or a wild, ending on a natural card; a wild only while a natural card can still end the run). Wilds left over go on a meld (a book takes any number), three or more alone are a book, and one or two with no meld at all are loose at 20 each. Checked against a brute-force solver (every way to group the cards) on 300 random hands; a 14-card hand takes well under a millisecond in Node.
- `rules.ts`: `goOut` records who went out and the order of everyone else's last turns (`lastTurns`); the last of them scores the round (`endRound`: each hand's best arrangement, `history`, `totals`, `outs`), and after round 11 `winners` holds the lowest total(s). `turns` keeps every turn of the round, for the pass cover and the slots.
- `cpu.ts`: every level goes out when it can, takes a wild or a discard that leaves fewer points, throws the card that leaves fewest, and keeps its wilds until its last turn, counting a loose one as nothing meanwhile (one that threw loose wilds for their 20 points lost 60% of its matches to one that kept them). Easy misses discards and throws loose cards at random often, Medium now and then, Hard never. In 300 simulated two-player matches each: Hard beats Medium about 61% of the time, Medium beats Easy 66%, Hard beats Easy 72%. Weighing pairs and near runs made Hard worse (it kept high pairs), so it doesn't.

**Setup** (`useCrownsLoadout.tsx`): seats 1 and 2 are the shell's; under them, a Player 3 and a Player 4 pill (No Player 3, Player 3: Person, Player 3: CPU; Player 4's once Player 3 is taken; `games/table/extraSeats.ts`, shared with Crazy Eights) and, when seat 2 is a person and a CPU sits in seat 3 or 4, a CPU level pill (with seat 2 a CPU, player 1's opponent menu sets every CPU's level), and above them the rules card (rule pills until 2026-11-08; see "Rules in Settings"). Settings `crownsCpu`, `crownsSeat3`, `crownsSeat4` (`ExtraSeatKind`), following the player. Seats 3 and 4 are "Player 3"/"Player 4" or a CPU (several CPUs are numbered), green and orange, never profiles: that needs `@tastic/shell` to know more than two seats. The dice rolls the CPU level; reset clears seats 3 and 4.

**The table** (`layout.ts`, `FourCrownsScreen.tsx`): drawn upright for whoever's turn it is when that's a person, otherwise for whoever has the phone. The other players sit in slots across the top in turn order (a fan of backs, name, total, last move); the stock and discard in the middle, with "Round 4 of 11" (or who went out) and a crown with "6s Wild" under them; the seated hand along the bottom in its melds with Gin's lines, a crown badge on every wild, and Throw and Go Out over it. The card size fits a 14-card hand across the width (44pt on a 402pt phone). With more than one person, the pass cover (`games/table/PassCover.tsx`) lists every turn since that person's last, one sentence each, adds "This is your last turn." once someone is out, and opens each new round with its wild rank and who plays first. Round over: the piles slide off the left edge, every hand lies face up in a row of its own with "+N" (or "Went out") and the total, and Next Round deals the next. Match over: the result card with the standings, lowest first, and Rematch (the highest total plays first). Stats (`matchStatsSlice`'s `fourcrowns`): a match against the CPU at its level when every other player is a CPU, against people otherwise; seat 1 and seat 2's profile are kept, a shared lowest total is a tie, plus Rounds Gone Out and Best Total.

**Shared pieces:** `games/table/HandRow.tsx` (`cardName`, `handRowPoses`, `MeldLines`, `ActionRow`) came out of Gin Rummy's screen for both games, and `TableView`'s `labels` may leave a seat out (Four Crowns draws its other players itself).

**Pinned by** `games/fourcrowns/melds.test.ts` (scoring, books with repeated suits, runs, wilds in gaps and at the King, the brute-force check, speed), `rules.test.ts` (the deal, a turn, going out and the last turns, scoring, the next round, the reshuffle, the winners, the CPU's choices including keeping wilds, CPU matches for 2, 3 and 4 players with every card accounted for, Hard over Easy), `useCrownsMatch.test.tsx`, `games/table/guideSteps.test.tsx`, `pack.test.ts` and `store.test.ts`. **Seen on the web** (402x874, sound off): the setup rows (with seat 2 a CPU, and with seat 2 a person, when the CPU level row shows); a whole four-player match against three Medium CPUs, played by a script from round 1 to the result card (draws, throws, CPUs going out and everyone's last turns, me going out, every round-over view with the wilds crowned, a 14-card hand in round 11 with every rank showing (the suits did NOT show in the default compact style; fixed later that day with the suit marks), the standings and Rematch); and three players, Guest, Player 2 and a CPU, passing the phone (the cover after my throw, the table turned to Player 2 face down behind it, Ready, and my next cover listing Player 2's and the CPU's turns). Also the table at the pane's desktop size, with 96pt cards. **Not checked:** a device, a 375pt phone's 14-card hand (41pt cards by the layout's sums), Android.

## Rule choices and suit marks (2026-11-06)

Asked which Crazy Eights rules his family plays, Jay answered "This could be a setting? As well as those other questions you had for me about rummy.. Different games have different settings...". So every rule question asked about a table game is now a setting, with the answers offered as its choices; the defaults are what Jay picked when asked. They were pills on each setup screen until 2026-11-08, when every game's rules moved into the Settings sheet, and since 2026-11-10 they are rows in both places (see "Rules in Settings" at the end); the pills below are only seats 3 and 4 now. Not released; no dependency changed.

**One dropdown pill per rule** (`games/table/RulePickers.tsx`): @tastic/profile's `OptionPicker` on the theme's surface, the same pill as the seat buttons beside it, its chip an icon for the kind of rule and its label the choice in effect; the pills wrap in one centered row (one parent, so an open menu stacks over the pills after it). Every table game's setup options use it: RatSlap's three switches (Slap Doubles or No Doubles, and so on), Six-Card Golf's holes, Trash's cards, War's length, and the rummy games' and Crazy Eights' rules and extra seats. The old row of pills (`LoadoutPills.tsx`) is gone: a game with three or more rules as rows of pills ran off a phone held sideways (vs CPU, a 402pt-tall frame). Things to know:
- **A choice's label has about 146px** in the menu (@tastic/profile's menu is 220 wide; the app font at 14px). Measured in the browser: "Gin 25, Under 25" 134, "Draw Until One Fits" 156 (cut off with an ellipsis), "Two Decks, No Jokers" 169. Keep labels under 140 and make each say the rule on its own ("Draw One", "Knock at 10"), since the pill shows only the choice.
- **The shell doesn't lift the options row against the CPU:** the one-person setup stacks the options before the seat buttons (a later sibling) and only lifts them in the two-person layouts (`sharedElevated`), so an open rule menu drew under the seat buttons. `RulePickers` lifts its own row (zIndex 100) while one of its menus is open. Fix it in @tastic/shell (lift `sharedControls` in the stacked zone while `optionsPopoverOpen`), then drop the workaround.
- **Placement:** each menu is placed by `useFrameAutoAlign` (against the window and its safe-area insets). Until 2026-11-06 it took the frame's own `rotation` (the setup screen's two-person halves stayed upright under the drawn turn).

**Gin Rummy's choices** (`GinRules` in the state: `target`, `knock`, `bonus`; `ginHelp` is the table's alone):
- Length: To 100, To 50, One Hand (`GinTarget` 1: the first hand anyone scores wins; a draw deals another).
- Knocking: Knock at 10; Oklahoma (`GinState.upcard` is the hand's first upcard: `knockLimit` is its value, face cards 10, an Ace gin only; a spade doubles the hand, bonuses included, `isDoubled`, `GinHandResult.doubled`); Gin Only (limit 0). The note under the piles says it: "Hand 2 · Knock at 7 · Spades double" (TableView's note box widened to 260 for it).
- Bonuses (`GIN_BONUSES`): Gin 25, Under 25; Plus Big Gin 31 (`canBigGin`/`bigGin`: right after drawing, all eleven cards meld, the hand ends with no throw; ending `bigGin`, counted as a gin in the stats); Gin 20, Under 10.
- Meld help: Melds and Count; Count Only (the hand in one row sorted by suit, then rank, no lines); No Help (no count either; Knock shows for any card that can be thrown and is refused with a reason, "14 left over: knock at 7 or less"; Big Gin is a button tried the same way).
- The CPU knocks only within the limit and goes Big Gin when it can. How to Play follows the knock rule, the bonuses and the length (`GinGuideInput`).

**Four Crowns' choices** (`CrownsRules` in the state: `deck`, `faces`, `lastTurns`):
- Deck (`crownsDeck`): No Jokers (104); With 4 Jokers (108); 3s Up, 4 Jokers (92, no Aces or 2s, so ranks run 3 to King like the rounds). Jokers are `joker-<deck>-<ink>` (an id ending 1 is red, as `JokerFace` reads it), always wild, 50 left in hand; hands are `AnyCard[]` now, a meld takes the round's wilds before a joker, and the banner says "6s and Jokers Wild".
- Face cards: J 11, Q 12, K 13, or Face Cards 10 (`cardPoints(card, rank, faces)`).
- Last turns: Last Turn Each, or Ends at Once (`goOut` scores the round straight away).
- How to Play follows the jokers and the last turns (`CrownsGuideInput`). CPU matches under the other rules end with every card accounted for.

**The earlier games' choices** (2026-11-06, Jay: "Can you go back and make the rules for those other games we just added too?"): the rule questions asked when they were built, each now a setting with its other answer offered. The Six-Card Golf question picked Six-Card over Four-Card Golf (a game of its own, still on the list) and RatSlap's was about controls, so neither became one; RatSlap's rules were switches already.
- **Trash** (`TrashState.wild`, `Settings.trashWild`): Jacks Wild, or Kings Wild (then a Jack or a Queen ends the turn). `isWild`/`spotsFor` take the wild; the CPU reads it; the prompt names it ("Put the King in any face-down spot").
- **Slapjack** (`RatSlapRules.penalty`, `Settings.slapjackPenalty`; RatSlap always burns): Burn a Card, or Give a Card (the slapper's top card goes face down under the other player's deck; out of cards, a wrong slap still loses; a card given to a player who was out puts them back in). The match hook's new `paid` event flies the card from one deck to the other (a `Departure` with a `from`), with the same "Wrong slap" call, sound and buzz.
- **War** (`WarState.down`, `Settings.warDown`): 3 Down, 1 Up, or 1 Down, 1 Up.
- **Speed** (`SpeedState.rules`, `Settings.speedWrap`/`speedRefill`): Ranks Wrap or Aces Low Only (`fits(card, top, wrap)`); Auto Refill or Tap to Draw (a played card leaves its spot empty; a tap on your draw pile fills the first empty spot, `drawCard`; your pile glows and the prompt says "Draw from your pile" while you have nothing to play and a spot to fill; the side piles turn only once nobody can play or draw, `canDraw` in `isStuck`; the CPU draws on its clock when nothing fits, `cpuDraws`).
- Each game's How to Play and rules sheet follow its choices.

**Suit marks on a hand.** Every card style draws the corner suit at the top right (it suits a column fanned downward), so a hand fanned sideways at 0.52 of a card showed only each covered card's rank: a Q♣ and a Q♥ read alike, in Gin and Four Crowns too, whatever their notes said. `HandRow.tsx`'s `suitMarkSpots`/`HandSuitMarks` put the suit under the rank on each covered card's strip, on a patch of the card's face color, in the ink the card draws its suit in (`suitInk`, the player's card style, Invert Dark Mode Colors in dark mode). Not on a card covered so closely that the mark would reach the card over it, not on a joker, and hidden while the cards are dealt; like Four Crowns' crowns, a mark moves at once while its card glides (300ms). Used by Gin Rummy, Four Crowns and Crazy Eights. The real fix is a left-edge index in @tastic/cards' faces.

**Settings added:** `ginKnock`, `ginBonus`, `ginHelp` (and `ginTarget` 1), `crownsDeck`, `crownsFaces`, `crownsLastTurns`, `trashWild`, `slapjackPenalty`, `warDown`, `speedWrap`, `speedRefill`, and Crazy Eights' six (below); a store from before fills them from `DEFAULT_SETTINGS`. Reset on the setup screen puts every rule back to its default; the dice still only roll a CPU level (or, where a game always rolled its rule, that rule).

**Pinned by** `games/ginrummy/rules.test.ts`' rule choices block (Oklahoma limits and doubling, Gin Only, Big Gin, the older bonuses, one hand, the CPU within the limit), `games/fourcrowns/melds.test.ts` (jokers, the round's wild first, faces 10) and `rules.test.ts` (the decks, the round ending at once, faces 10, CPU matches under other rules), `games/trash/rules.test.ts` (Kings wild), `games/ratslap/rules.test.ts` and `useRatSlapMatch.test.tsx` (the give penalty and its event), `games/war/rules.test.ts` (one down), `games/speed/rules.test.ts` (no wrap, drawing by hand, not stuck while anyone can draw, the CPU drawing, whole matches under both), and `games/table/guideSteps.test.tsx` (each guide following its rules, the copy rules under every combination). **Seen on the web** (402x874): every setup screen's pills, Speed drawing by hand (the spot left empty, the CPU drawing into its own, my pile ringed with "Draw from your pile", a tap filling the spot), a wrong slap in Slapjack giving a card (26 to 25 and 27, the card flying to the CPU's deck), a menu opening over the seat buttons with every label whole, Gin's table under Oklahoma and No Help (the note, the suit-sorted hand, no count), Four Crowns with jokers ("3s and Jokers Wild"), and the suit marks in all three hands. **Not checked:** a device, the native menus' placement in a turned frame, Big Gin and a refused knock in the app (only in tests).

## Crazy Eights (2026-11-06)

For two to four players, people passing the phone and CPUs in any mix, on Four Crowns' pieces. Last on Home, after Four Crowns. Not released; no dependency changed.

**Rules.** Jay: the first player out wins (one game, no points); what you do when nothing fits, which cards do something besides the 8s, and how many cards you start with are settings (`EightsRules`, see "Rule choices and suit marks"):
- Nothing fits: Keep Drawing (until a card fits, the default), Draw One, Draw Up to Three (then the turn passes).
- Special cards: Only 8s (the default); 2s Draw Two (the next player draws two and loses their turn); 2s, Queens, Aces (also a Queen skips the next player and an Ace turns play the other way; with two players an Ace is another turn).
- Cards: 7 Cards, 5 for 3+ (the default), 7 Cards, 5 Cards.
- Picked without asking: you draw only while nothing in hand fits, and a drawn card that fits is played (no keeping it); an 8 names any suit, and the next card must be that suit or another 8 (not the rank under it); 2s don't stack; the first card turned up is never an 8 (it goes to the bottom of the stock) and does nothing if it's special; an empty stock is made again from the discard but its top card; if everyone in a row has passed with nothing to draw, the fewest cards win (a tie shares it); a rematch starts with the next player.

**Engine** (`src/games/crazyeights/engine/`, pure): `rules.ts` (`createEightsMatch`, `fits`, `playableCards`, `canDraw`/`draw`, `canPass`/`pass`, `play`, `nameSuit`, `rematch`, `seatAfter`; every turn is an `EightsTurn` with the suit it faced, what was drawn and played, the suit named, a turn lost to a 2 or a Queen, an Ace's reverse) and `cpu.ts`: Easy plays a card at random 60% of the time and names a random suit half the time; Medium keeps its 8s while another card fits, plays the card that leaves the most cards able to follow it, and names the suit it holds most of; Hard also hits a player down to two cards with a 2 or a Queen and leads or names a suit the next player was seen drawing for. It's mostly luck: in 600 simulated two-player games, Hard beats Medium 55% and Easy 60% (63% with the action cards), Medium beats Easy 58%; a Medium-vs-Medium game is about 35 turns.

**The table** (`layout.ts`, `CrazyEightsScreen.tsx`, `useEightsMatch.ts`): drawn upright for whoever's turn it is when that's a person, otherwise for whoever has the phone; the other players in slots across the top (a fan of backs, cards in hand, what they did last: "Drew 2, played 7♥", "Played 8♠, named ♣", "Drew 2, lost a turn"); the stock and discard in the middle with the suit to follow under them ("Hearts", "Clubs named") and, with three or four, "Next: Kid" ("(reversed)" after an Ace). The seated hand is sorted by suit (spades, hearts, clubs, diamonds), then rank, the 8s last, in one row while it fits at 0.52 of a card (9 cards on a phone), then two rows (the back row 0.62 of a card higher, so its ranks and suit marks show over a raised front card), then closer. On your turn the cards that fit are raised; a tap on one plays it, a tap on one that doesn't buzzes; the stock glows when you must draw; an 8 brings up four suit buttons. The prompt says what fits ("Play a heart, a 9, or an 8"), "Nothing fits: draw from the stock", or after a CPU's 2 "You drew two and lost your turn". A turn with nothing to play and nothing more to draw passes by itself after `NO_PLAY_MS` (900), a person's too, but not under the pass cover (`useEightsMatch`'s `holder`); a CPU drawing card after card does so every `CPU_REDRAW_MS` (380). The pass cover lists everything since that person's own last turn ("Dad played the 2♥. Kid drew two and lost a turn."), and on a first turn the card that started the pile. Game over: every hand turns face up, then the result card with everyone's cards left and Rematch. Stats (`matchStatsSlice`'s `crazyeights`): against the CPU at its level when every other player is a CPU, against people otherwise, plus Eights Played.

**In the pack:** `definition.ts`, `useEightsLoadout.tsx` (the game's name and its rules card, then seat pills from `extraSeats.ts`; see "Rules in Settings"), How to Play (`guideSteps.tsx`: Match the Pile, Eights Are Wild, Nothing Fits as the rule has it, Twos Draw Two or Action Cards and Aces Turn It Around when on, First Out Wins, Pass the Phone), `CRAZY_EIGHTS_RULES`, the `crazyEightsGuide` store key, and a Home tile of the pile and a hand with the cards that fit raised (`CrazyEightsPreview.tsx`). Settings `eightsCpu`, `eightsSeat3`, `eightsSeat4`, `eightsDraw`, `eightsSpecials`, `eightsHand`.

**Pinned by** `games/crazyeights/rules.test.ts` (the deal and hand sizes, what fits, the wild 8s and the suit after them, each draw rule, the reshuffle, the stalemate, 2s and the action cards, the CPU's choices, CPU games for 2, 3 and 4 players under every rule with every card accounted for, Hard over Easy), `useEightsMatch.test.tsx` (the CPU after its pause, the pass by itself and not under the cover, the CPU's quicker redraws, held while paused or dealing), `games/table/guideSteps.test.tsx`, `pack.test.ts` and `store.test.ts`. **Seen on the web** (402x874, sound off): the setup pills, a whole three-player game against two Medium CPUs with the action cards played by a script to the result card (a 2 making a CPU draw two, an Ace reversing play, an 8 naming hearts, "You drew two and lost your turn", drawing until a card fit, the result and Rematch with the next player first, the loss recorded against the Medium CPU), and a pass-and-play game (Guest, a CPU and Player 3: the cover with the starting card and two turns, the table turned to Player 3 face down). **Not checked:** a device, a hand long enough for two rows, Android.

## The OS turns (2026-11-06)

`@tastic/core` 0.15 and `@tastic/shell` 0.5 drop the drawn rotation: no accelerometer, no Lock Orientation, no config plugin. Jay's call, recorded in both packages' CLAUDE.md and in the plan (`dueltastic-pucktastic-rotation-plan.md`, side-session notes of the Swirlio session, step 5 for CardTastic): the drawn rotation was "a misguided venture" (janky sensing, and since iPadOS 27 `requireFullScreen` no longer stops an app being resized), CardTastic's phones rotate with the OS, as every tablet does, and its two-player games hold portrait on a phone. The packages' notes have the details; here is what Solitaire changed when it bumped to them (with `@tastic/profile` 0.17, `@rific/toaster` 0.12, `@rific/updater` 0.8 and `@rific/heatmap` 0.10, which needed nothing).
- **`app.json`:** `"orientation": "default"` (it was `"portrait"`), `"@tastic/core"` out of `plugins` (prebuild fails while it's listed), and `UIViewControllerBasedStatusBarAppearance: true` in `ios.infoPlist` (the plugin set it; the stack sets the status bar per screen, see "The status bar is set by the root Stack's screen options"). No `requireFullScreen` any more, so Split View and Stage Manager work, and no `android:appCategory="game"`. Introspected: all four orientations on iPhone and iPad.
- **Gone from the app:** `portraitWhileKeyboard` (pack.ts), the `rotation` the loadout handed `RulePickers` and the one every Settings sheet took (`rotation={0}` in `GameChrome` and the table screens), `useFrameAutoAlign`'s `rotation` option, and `expo-sensors` (a direct dependency and a Jest mock; nothing else used it).
- **Two-player games hold a phone upright** (`components/portraitOnPhones.ts`' `usePortraitOnPhones`, on `expo-screen-orientation`'s `lockAsync(PORTRAIT_UP)`/`unlockAsync()`): the tables are laid out for a portrait phone, and one lying flat between two players must not turn when someone leans on it. Held by the setup route (`app/loadout/[game].tsx`, two-player games only) and the play route for a game with a `seatTwo` (`app/play/[game].tsx`), each wrapping the shell's route; a module counter lets go only when the last holder leaves (the setup screen stays mounted under the play screen). Phones only (the screen's shorter side under 600pt); tablets and the web follow the window, as the tables do on a desktop browser. No config plugin: the module's only sets an initial orientation, and unlocked it allows all but upside down on iOS. This reverses the old "don't add `expo-screen-orientation`" rule, which was about the portrait lock. Pinned by `portraitOnPhones.test.tsx`.
- **Seats:** `twoPlayerSeats` became `sideBySideWhenWide: true` on Gin Rummy, Four Crowns and Crazy Eights (pass and play, nobody sits across); the other two-player games leave it out (face to face in any window).
- **Lock Orientation:** the middleware no longer files a lock, and `reshapeForShell` drops a 1.2.8 lock from `settings` and from every player's filed settings instead of copying it into `appSettings` (since the rollback keys went, the same day).
- **Status bar:** `useScreenOptions` returns a plain `statusBarStyle: 'light'` (core's `useRotationAwareStatusBar` is inert now, and core drops it once nothing calls it).
- **Needs a new native build:** the plugin, the orientation, the Info.plist key, `expo-sensors` out, `expo-screen-orientation` in and `@tastic/profile` 0.17 (native) all change the fingerprint. Not checked: any of it on a device or simulator (the plan's checks: an iPhone in both landscapes, the portrait hold on a two-player game, an iPad in all four orientations and in Split View, a solitaire game turned mid-deal), and a profile named with the keyboard up sideways.
- **The drawn rotation's code is gone too** (2026-11-06, the plan's step 5): `withGameProviders` is a plain `View`, `gameLayout.ts`, `RulesDialog`, `HeroTitle`, `ThemedToaster`, `DealLayer`, `WinCascadeLayer` and `useFrameAutoAlign` read the window and the plain insets, the ghost overlays use measured window points as they are (see "Every ghost overlay uses measured window points as they are" above), `RotatedContentGutter` is `GameContentGutter`, and `overlayFrame.ts`/`overlayFrameMath.ts`, `rotatedScreen.test.tsx` and every test's `@tastic/core` rotation mock are deleted. No source file imports `useRotation`, `useRotatedWindowDimensions`, `FakeLandscapeView`, `rotateInsets` or `rotateDimensions`. The 1.2.8 rollback keys went the same day (Jay): see "Saved data from 1.2.8".

## Seat 2 and seating at a computer (2026-11-07)

From DuelTastic's session the same day (the shell's CLAUDE.md, "Player 1 picks seat 2"), on `@tastic/shell` 0.7, `@tastic/profile` 0.18 and `@tastic/core` 0.16 (none released yet):

- **Player 1 picks seat 2** in every setup screen: an opponent button beside seat 1's lists the CPU (its levels, or one plain CPU) and Player, never a profile, and carries the CPU's Color row. A person in seat 2 gets their own button (GUEST or a profile, never the CPU).
- **Face to face only on a phone or tablet** (Jay: "It should only be face to face if on mobile or web-mobile"). At a computer (the shell's `useAtComputer`: the web without a touch-first screen, at any width) every two-player game's setup screen sits side by side (`sideBySideAtComputer` on all nine), and at the table `useFaceToFace(twoPlayer)` (`games/table/TableChrome.tsx`) is false: RatSlap's and Slapjack's `SlapTable`, Speed, War, Trash and Six-Card Golf draw player 2's side upright (the layout they use against the CPU) while the halves still split the table top and bottom for each person's taps, and `TableOverlay` puts the two result cards side by side, player 1's on the left, both upright. Gin Rummy, Crazy Eights and Four Crowns never turned anything.
- **RatSlap, Slapjack and Speed are only against the CPU at a computer** (2026-11-11, Jay: "there would be no way for each to slap, unless we used keys, which wouldnt be ideal"). They're played in real time and a computer has one mouse, so seat 2's menu offers only the CPU there: each definition's `seatTwo` is a getter over `games/table/realTimeSeats.ts`' `realTimeSeatTwo()`, whose `isAtComputer()` is the shell's `useAtComputer` test without the hook (the definition isn't rendered, and the shell reads `game.seatTwo` as it draws). A saved Player there reads as the CPU (the shell's `seatTwoFor`). War, Six-Card Golf and Trash take turns, and Gin Rummy, Crazy Eights and Four Crowns pass the device, so they keep Player at a computer. The proper home is the shell (a `seatTwoAtComputer` field beside `sideBySideAtComputer`); the getter stands in until then.
- Tests: `src/__tests__/games/table/seating.test.tsx` (`useFaceToFace` with and without a computer, every game that offers Player at a computer sits side by side, the real-time games offer only the CPU there, and `isAtComputer` against touch points and pointer media). Checked on the web dev server at 1100x1300: RatSlap's 2 Player setup screen side by side and its table with nothing turned. **Not checked:** the side-by-side result cards (no match was played to its end), the other tables on screen, a device.

## Ready on the felt (2026-11-07)

Jay: the setup screen's Ready buttons "don't work very well on the felt". They were hud's `ReadyButton` as DuelTastic and PuckTastic draw it on black: an outline and a monospace READY? in the seat's color, which here is the muted card back (Rose's `#a7375d` in light mode), on green felt beside seat and rule buttons that are surface pills in the app's font. Now, over any pack's `Backdrop`, the shell draws every Ready (player 1's, and a person's in seat 2) as a pill on the theme's surface the size of those buttons, READY? black or white in DejaVu Serif Bold, filled with the seat's color once ready with its label in that color's own ink. The code is upstream, nothing in Solitaire: hud's `ReadyButton` `surfaceColor`/`dark` and `PlayerSetupPanel` `readySurfaceColor`, and the shell's `LoadoutScreen` passing the surface over a backdrop (see both packages' CLAUDE.md, "Ready on a felt"). **Released** in `@tastic/hud` 0.28.0 and `@tastic/shell` 0.7.0 (2026-11-07), which `package.json` asks for (until then the three changed files were copied into `node_modules`). Both are JavaScript only, but `@tastic/profile` 0.18.0, released with them, is native, so this goes out in a new build. Seen on the web build at 402x874 (RatSlap's setup screen, 1 Player and 2 Player face to face, READY? and READY ✓, light and dark); not on a device. The pass cover's and the guide's Ready buttons sit on dialog cards and were already the dialogs' contained buttons.

## The table games' glow (2026-11-07)

Jay: "a pulsing background glow would be better than a straight up outline, especially on non uniform piles like the new modes". Every table game marked what the player can tap (RatSlap's and Slapjack's deck, War's deck, Speed's draw pile, Golf's and Trash's grid spots, stock and discard, Crazy Eights' stock) with a 3pt outline 5pt outside the top card, in the hint ring's color. Every stock and deck here fans its under-cards 2.5 and 5pt down and to the right (`DeckStack`, RatSlap's `under1`/`under2`, the grid games' stock), so the outline cut straight across them. Now it is `games/table/PileGlow.tsx`: a card-sized view with no border and no fill, only a `boxShadow` halo in `hintGreen` (`0 0 22px 6px`, `GLOW_BLUR`/`GLOW_SPREAD`), breathing on the solitaire hint's pulse (`startHintPulse`, 1100ms to 0.4 and back). It is drawn BEFORE the cards (TableView puts it after the empty slots and before `TableCards`; `SlapTable` before its `TableCards` and decks), and a box shadow draws only outside its own box, so the cards cover the rest and what shows is a halo round the whole pile, under-cards included, and between a grid's cards it fills the gaps like one glow. One clock per table (`usePileGlowPulse`, one shared value in TableView and one in SlapTable), so every glow on a table breathes together. `TableTarget.ring`/`TableView`'s `rings` are now `glow`/`glows`. The solitaire games' hint ring (`PlayingCard`'s `hintRing`, `RunOutline`) is unchanged. Pinned by `games/table/pileGlow.test.tsx` (one glow per glowing card at its spot, none for `glow: false`, no border, drawn before the cards). Seen on the web build at 402x874 (Six-Card Golf's grid, its stacked stock and the discard, RatSlap's deck, in dark mode, and RatSlap's deck in light mode, where it reads a little softer on the brighter felt; peak and trough held with an injected style), with no console errors. Not seen on a device: the halo depends on React Native's `boxShadow` drawing only outside the box on iOS and Android, as the solitaire hint ring's glow already did. Its size is the solitaire hint's `hintGlowShadow` since the same day (see "The hint glow").

## The hint glow (2026-11-07)

After the table games' glow (above), Jay asked for the same on the solitaire hint. The hint is no longer a ring on the card's edge: it is a breathing halo in `hintGreen` round the card or run to move, drawn so that it shows round the cards and never over a neighbour's face.
- **The halo:** `components/hintGlow.tsx`' `HintGlow`, a view with no border or fill and only a `boxShadow` (`hintGlowShadow(width, color)`: blur 0.24 of a card's width and spread 0.06, never under 18 and 5, so a 96pt card gets 23/6 and a phone's ~50pt cards 18/5), breathing on `startHintPulse`, mounted only while something is hinted. A box shadow draws only outside its box, so it never tints the card it marks. The table games' `PileGlow` uses the same `hintGlowShadow`.
- **Columns** (`PileView`): a hinted run, or a single hinted card, glows in one box from the run's head to its bottom card, drawn BEFORE every card of the column with no zIndex, so the column's own cards cover it and it never spills over the card above the run (`run-hint-glow`). None of the run's cards marks itself. A selected run (more than one card) keeps its ring over the cards (`RunOutline`, now selection only) and wins over a hint, as before.
- **Neighbours cover the halo, not the other way round** (`hintStacking(glowing)`): a pile beside others sits a level over them (zIndex 1) unless it's the one glowing (0). On each column game's column wrapper (the `DropZone` round each `PileView`, in all nine screens: that wrapper, not PileView's own container, is what sits beside the next column), FreeCell's freecells, Canfield's reserve, and StockWaste's waste and stock. Safe because React Native orders zIndex among siblings only and React Native Web gives every view `z-index: 0`, so every view is its own stacking context (checked on the web build: every ancestor of a column has one) and nothing is lifted out of its row. Before, a hinted column's glow tinted the left neighbour's face (seen on the web build: the Q's right edge beside a hinted 8) while the right neighbour covered it.
- **Waste and stock** (`StockWaste`): the hinted waste glows under the draw-3 fan's cards as well as the top card (`wasteGlow`, zIndex 0 under the fan's 1 to 3), so the fanned cards cover it; the waste DraggableCard is no longer `hinted`. The stock still marks itself (`hinted` on its card) and sits under the waste while it glows.
- **Pyramid and TriPeaks:** each hinted, exposed cell's glow is drawn in the triangle before every cell (`cellGlow`), because a card overlaps the cards above it and a halo drawn with it tinted their faces. The cells lost their `hinted` prop.
- **Spider's stock:** one glow round the whole fan of packets, under all of them (`StockFan`); the front packet used to be ringed alone.
- **Everything else** (a card marking itself through `PlayingCard`'s `hinted`: Scorpion's and Aces Up's stock, FoundationCell, a hinted empty slot, which keeps its dashed outline in the slot's own color and glows, `outset` past its border) draws the halo on itself.
- Pinned by `pileViewRunOutline.test.tsx` (the glow's box for a run and a single card, no zIndex, drawn before the first card; the selected run's ring and no glow; no glow while a card of the run is in flight). Seen on the web build at 402x874 in dark mode, glow held at full strength with an injected style: Klondike (a hinted 8 glowing under its column with the Q beside it untinted), Pyramid (a pair in the bottom row) and TriPeaks (the stock under the waste). **Quieter than the ring in tight boards:** where cards sit 2pt apart (columns on a phone, Pyramid's bottom row) the neighbours cover the halo's sides and it shows mostly below the cards; the Hint button's glide still points the way, but Auto Hint has only the glow. `hintGlowShadow`'s minimums are the knob if it reads too quiet. Not seen on a device, nor the waste's glow under a draw-3 fan, a freecell, the reserve or Spider's fan live (only in code).

## Rules in Settings (2026-11-08)

Jay didn't love the rule pills on the two-player setup screens (no game name, a pill showed only the choice, so "ONLY 8S" or "TO 100" meant little, rules and players looked the same, and they wrapped raggedly), and the solitaire games kept theirs in the Settings sheet: "a mixture of both is not that great". So every game's rules went into the Settings sheet, and for two days a setup screen only listed them. **Since 2026-11-10 they're in both places** (Jay: moving them all to Settings "might have been a mistake", with no sign the lines could be changed, and the rules buried under Sound, How to Play and Appearance; he picked "both places" over a Change Rules button): a two-player game's setup screen shows them as the same rows on a card, and every Settings sheet puts them first. Not released; JS only, so the native fingerprint is the same. Needs `@tastic/hud` 0.29.0 and `@tastic/shell` 0.8.0 (both released 2026-11-10 for it).
- **The rows** (`components/RuleRows.tsx`): a game's rules are a section of the sheet named for the game, with "Applies to next game" under the name (`RULES_APPLY_CAPTION`, Jay asked for it), then a row per rule in the sheet's own shape (the 36pt badge, the rule's name, the choice in effect as the caption, a caret). A tap opens its choices in place under the row, a check on the one in effect; picking one closes them. In place, not a popover: the sheet scrolls and fades at its edges, which would clip a menu hung off a row. One rule open at a time. A rule is a `GameRule` (`components/gameRule.ts`: key, name, icon, value, choices, onChange; pure, no UI). Auto Hint is one of these rows too, its badge off while it's Off.
- **Which game, and first in the sheet:** the pack's `useSettingsRules` (`components/SettingsSections.tsx`) finds the game from the route (`games/ruleSettings.ts`' `ruleGameForPath`: `/play/<id>` or `/loadout/<id>`) and returns its section, or null, so a game's play screen and its setup screen show the same section and Home's sheet shows none. The shell hands it to hud's `BaseSettingsDialog` as `leading`, drawn above every shared row; with something leading, hud labels its own rows GENERAL so a label still follows every gap (`@tastic/hud` 0.29.0's `leading`, `@tastic/shell` 0.8.0's `PackDefinition.useSettingsRules`). Until 2026-11-10 the rules were the top of the pack's `SettingsSections`, which the shell draws under its own rows, so they sat below Keep Awake, Sound, How to Play and Appearance. Every game builds its rules in its own `ruleSettings.ts` from the live settings; `GAME_RULE_SETTINGS` lists them by id: Klondike (Draw), Spider (Suits), Golf and TriPeaks (Rank Wrap), Canfield (Draw), Scorpion (Stock), Forty Thieves (Moves), and every two-player game. FreeCell, Pyramid, Yukon and Aces Up have none. GameChrome's `settingsContent` and the seven `*SettingsSection.tsx` files (Paper segments) are gone. The two-player games' names moved to `games/matchGameTitles.ts` so the sheet can name them without loading their screens.
- **"Applies to next game" is true everywhere:** every solitaire screen already reads its rule when it deals. A two-player match plays on the round its setup screen committed at Ready, and Rematch used to deal the next match on the old rules too, so a rule changed in a match's sheet never reached a Rematch. Now every Rematch takes the rules from Settings as they are when it's pressed and commits them as the active round (`games/table/useNextRound.ts`, with each game's `*_RULE_KEYS`), keeping who's playing and the CPU's level; each match hook's `rematch` takes the new rules (the last match's when given none).
- **The setup screen** (`games/table/MatchSetup.tsx`, every two-player game's `options`): the game's name, then its rules on a card (a well: the theme's surface on hud's `WELL_SHADOW`, 360 wide at most), the same `RuleRows` as the sheet, writing the same settings, so the two always agree; no "Applies to next game" there, since the next game is the one being set up. A game without rules would get no card (every two-player game has at least one today). Four Crowns' and Crazy Eights' seat 3 and 4 pills (and the CPU level pill) stay under it (`RulePickers`, testID `<game>-seat-pills`), since they're players. The dice and reset in the two-person band still roll and reset the rules. From 2026-11-08 to 11-10 the card was the choices in effect, a line each, muted, a tap on them opening the Settings sheet (`ruleSummary` and that screen's own `SettingsDialog` went with it).
- **The card's height:** face to face on a short phone the middle band can't hold every row, so the card scrolls past `window height - RULES_CARD_RESERVED_HEIGHT` (420: the 360pt the seat buttons, Ready buttons, action band and title take at 375x667, measured on the web build, plus 30 above and below) less the seat pills' measured height, never under `RULES_CARD_MIN_HEIGHT` (120, nearly two rows), its edges fading while there's more (auto-paper's `FadingScrollView`). At 375x667 face to face Gin Rummy's four rows (264pt) get 247 and scroll a little; with a CPU in seat 3, Four Crowns' and Crazy Eights' three seat pills wrap one to a row (148pt) and the card gets its 120, which keeps both Ready buttons on screen (12pt from each edge; 180 pushed them 19pt off). A rule opened on a phone up to about 812pt tall face to face can scroll its choices under the card's edge. Against the CPU there is room on every phone.
- Pinned by `settingsSections.test.tsx` (no rules on Home and nothing leading there, a game's section by route, one rule open at a time, Auto Hint's choices), `games/ruleSettings.test.ts` (which games have rules; every rule shows a choice in effect and every choice reads back once picked; the routes), `games/table/matchSetup.test.tsx` (the title and the rows, a choice picked right there, no card without rules, seat pills kept, `useNextRound`), hud's `BaseSettingsDialog.test.tsx` and the shell's `packSections.test.tsx` (leading first, GENERAL only under it), and the Rematch cases in `useGinMatch.test.tsx` and Crazy Eights' `rules.test.ts`. Seen on the web dev server (dark): Gin Rummy and Crazy Eights face to face at 402x874 (Knocking opened on the setup screen, Oklahoma picked, the Settings sheet opening on GIN RUMMY with Knocking already Oklahoma, then GENERAL), Gin Rummy and Four Crowns at 375x667 face to face (above), Klondike's sheet (KLONDIKE / Draw, GENERAL, APPEARANCE, GAMEPLAY, CARDS) and Home's (unchanged, no GENERAL). Not seen against the CPU on screen (the seat 2 pick stayed Player), at a computer, or on a device.

## The calendar's hours (2026-11-08)

Jay: the Stats screen's calendar "might be cool if it had the hourly view option like there is on lumber", and "the current day or hour" shouldn't be outlined: "Outlined is just for when one is selected."
- **No outline on today:** the calendar's `todayBorderColor` is `'transparent'` (as Lumber's), so the only outline is the picked day or hour. Today still pulses (left out under Reduce Motion).
- **Days | Hours:** two quiet words under the calendar, right-aligned in the well, DAYS and HOURS in small capitals, the one showing in the theme's primary and the other muted, each a feedback-press `Pressable` with Lumber's finger-sized hit slop (Jay, 2026-11-20: Lumber's look, since it's rarely switched). It was a Paper `SegmentedButtons` until then (the small density at first, which smeared on iOS; see "Segmented switches on iOS"). Not saved: the screen opens on Days, like its other switches.
- **The hourly view** is Lumber's (`LogHeatmap.tsx`'s `HourHeatmap`, rebuilt in `PlayCalendar.tsx`'s `HourCalendar`): @rific/heatmap's `Grid`, 6 hours to a column and 4 columns to a day (`HOURS_PER_COLUMN`, aligned to midnight, 06:00, 12:00 and 18:00, a gap again between days), as many columns as 18pt pitches fit the well, ending with the block under way (the hours after now blank), each day's name ("Sat 7") over its first column. Same 14pt cells, same shades, and the calendar's 144pt height, so switching moves nothing. About 4.5 days on a phone, 8 in the 640pt column. A tapped hour is outlined and shows on the line ("Yesterday 8 PM · 6 deals, 3 won"; `hourLabel`); a day's name picks the day, outlined round its columns; a tap again, or elsewhere in the view, lets go. With nothing picked the line shows the hour under way. A picked day carries over between the views, and a picked hour becomes its day on the way back to Days.
- **The data:** each `DayRecord` gained `hours` (statsTypes.ts): deals and wins by the local hour a deal ended in ('0' to '23'), each game's share too, so the game picker narrows them (`getGameView`). Written by `bumpDay` alongside the day. Only the last `HOUR_LOG_DAYS` (31) days keep theirs: `bumpDay` drops older days' hours as the next deal ends, since the view reaches back 9 days at most and a year of hours would have grown the saved stats (one root entry, Android's ~2MB limit) several times over. Say so if hours should be kept for something else later (a time-of-day stat).
- **Before the log:** a day in view with more deals than its hours hold is from before 2026-11-08, so its hours, up to its first kept hour, are dimmed (the grid's `minTime`, `hoursKeptFrom`) rather than drawn as empty felt. Matters only for the first week after the update.
- Pinned by `playCalendar.test.tsx` (no outline until a day is picked, the switch, an hour and a day name on the line and their toggles, the window and day layout, the data and the dimming, the hour names) and `statsEngine.test.ts` (the hour log, its 31 days, the game view). Seen on the web dev server (402x874 dark and light, 1280x800) with seeded days: both views 144pt tall, the dimmed pre-log day, the outlines, the line. Not seen on a device. JS only, no new dependency, so it can ship in an OTA.

## Segmented switches on iOS (2026-11-08)

Jay's phone showed a gold fade inside the left edge of the calendar's Hours segment, and his screenshot also had a thin line at mid-height inside the stats tabs' Achievements segment. Both are React Native's iOS border drawing under Paper's `SegmentedButtons`: an end segment has uneven corners (square on one side, Paper's 20pt round on the other), so its border is drawn as a small image stretched to fit, and that image needs twice the corner plus a point (41pt). Paper lets the label set a segment's height, which on iOS is a third of a point over a whole number (40.33pt regular, 36.33pt small). At a fractional height short of the image, the left border smears across the segment: a thin line at 40.33, a wide fade at 36.33. The web never shows it.
- **Proven on the iOS 27 simulator** with throwaway probe bundles swapped into the old 1.2.4 Release build (its `main.jsbundle` and `Expo.plist`'s `EXUpdatesEnabled` backed up and put back after): plain bordered views at whole heights were clean even at 36pt with a 20pt corner; Paper's own switch smeared at both densities, at exactly 36.333 and 40.333 measured; a whole height (36 or 40) or a smaller corner was clean. 19pt fits whatever the label's height (its image is 39pt) and the ends still look fully round.
- **The fix** (`components/segmentedButtons.ts`): every SegmentedButtons here takes `theme={SEGMENT_THEME}` (`roundness` 3.8, so Paper's 5 x roundness corner is 19) and the regular density: the calendar's Days | Hours until it became two words, and Records' Best Score | Fastest (whose surface behind it takes `SEGMENT_RADIUS`). A fixed height was the other way out, but a larger text size would overflow it. Pinned by `segmentedButtons.test.ts` (a source scan of every `<SegmentedButtons` tag, and the corner fitting a 40pt segment).
- **Not fixed here, the same bug in packages:** `@rific/auto-paper`'s Appearance and Harmony pickers need the same theme in their package. Not checked on a device; the probes ran on the simulator only.
- **`@tastic/shell`'s Home and Stats tabs got the same theme (2026-11-11, released in 0.8.2),** in the shell's own `src/components/segmentTheme.ts`. The App Store screenshots showed the line on "Two Player" and "Achievements". In the same change, the Stats | Records | Achievements segments became label-wide on iOS and Android (`LABEL_WIDE_TAB` gained `flex: 0`). Before it, Paper's `flex: 1` made Yoga ignore the `flexBasis: 'auto'`, so the three came out equal and cut "Achievements" to "Achievem…" on every phone. Only the web had ever been checked. Both shipped in `@tastic/shell` 0.8.2, which `package.json` asks for. JS only, so no new native build. The 2026-11-11 Home and Stats screenshots were taken with that fix swapped into the simulator build before it was released (`store/README.md`).

## Search and link previews (2026-11-11)

The web export's pages had no title, description or link preview (since SDK 58 `<Head>` only sets the title after hydration, and nothing else was set), so search engines and link previews saw an empty page. Now every page carries them.

- **`src/utils/seo.tsx`** holds the pack's copy (`PACK_DESCRIPTION`, naming eight of the twenty games, the best known of each kind, and `GAME_DESCRIPTION`, added after a game's blurb) and the builders. Each route exports `generateMetadata()`, which Expo Router 58 writes into that page's exported HTML (only the route's own file counts, never `_layout`): the title, description, canonical link, Open Graph and Twitter card tags, with Games-Web's card (`games.infinitetoken.com/images/og.png`, 1200x630, live once Games-Web is released) as the image. DuelTastic, PuckTastic and CardTastic have the same file with their own copy (CardTastic's in `src/utils/`, since its aliases are per folder).
- **Titles read name first** (Jay, 2026-11-11), so a search result that cuts one short keeps the name: Home is `CardTastic: Solitaire & More | Infinite Token Games` (the App Store name, which keeps the word people search for), a solitaire game's page `Klondike Solitaire | CardTastic | Infinite Token Games` (the name people search for it by), and a two-player game's `Gin Rummy | CardTastic | Infinite Token Games`.
- **Game pages:** each game's loadout and play pages carry its own title and blurb, so a search for that game can land on it, and both name the page Home opens (`gamePath`: its loadout, or play when it has none) as canonical, so search engines count the two as one. Stats and Profiles are `noindex`. An id the pack doesn't have (the export's own `[game].html`) gets Home's. Jay wasn't sure about these pages and went with it: drop them to the pack's metadata if they ever cause trouble.
- **Canonical links** are `https://games.infinitetoken.com` plus `EXPO_BASE_URL` (build:web's `WEB_BASE_URL`, which Expo inlines into the static render as well), so they follow the path the build is served under, `/cardtastic` since 2026-11-11 (`/solitaire` before).
- **The tab's title after load:** each route also draws `PageTitle` (`GamePageTitle` on the game routes), a web-only `<Head><title>` drawn deeper than `@tastic/shell`'s own in `Fonts.tsx` (the app's name), so it wins (react-helmet-async takes the last one; checked with expo-router's vendored copy). Without it the tab, and the page Google renders with JavaScript, would read the app's name. Web only, as the shell's is: on iOS Head is Handoff metadata.
- **Left bare:** expo-router's own `_sitemap.html` and `+not-found.html`, which take no metadata.
- **Only two-player games have loadout pages** (`generateStaticParams` there lists games with `useLoadout`), so a solitaire game's canonical is its play page.
- **Checked** on a local export (`WEB_BASE_URL=/solitaire expo export -p web --clear`, written outside the repo): every page's tags, one `<title>` each, `lang="en"` (the default root HTML's). `seo.test.ts` pins the copy, titles and canonical links, and that every route exports its metadata. Not checked in a browser or on the deployed site. Games-Web's sitemap lists only `/solitaire/` so far, not the game pages. JavaScript only: no native change.

## The rename finished (2026-11-11)

Jay: "The folder and the github repo, everything should be CardTastic." What moved, and what can't:
- **Renamed:** the GitHub repo (jayrdeaton/CardTastic; GitHub redirects the old URLs, and its description and topics now cover the two-player games), the folder (`~/Developer/CardTastic`, with this project's Claude memory moved to match), the npm name (`cardtastic`), the URL scheme (`cardtastic`), the Android package (`com.infinitetoken.cardtastic`: never uploaded to Google Play, so this was the last chance, Jay's call), the web path (`games.infinitetoken.com/cardtastic/`, `build:web`'s `WEB_BASE_URL` and output folder), the Vercel project (`cardtastic`, now also at `cardtastic-web.vercel.app`; `solitaire-bay.vercel.app` stays attached), and the store config's GitHub and marketing URLs.
- **Kept:** the iOS bundle ID `com.infinitetoken.solitaire` (the App Store record's can't change, so iOS and Android now differ, and `store/seed/` keeps it), the EAS slug `Solitaire` (EAS can't rename a project, and a new one would cut every existing build off from OTA updates), and the `solitaire:` storage prefix (players never see it; Jay's call). The code's `Solitaire*` names (`SolitaireGameId`, `SOLITAIRE_GAME_IDS`, `SolitaireGameDefinition`, ...) name the kind of game, not the app, and stay.
- **The web move needs a deploy order:** this project's web build first (`vercel --prod`, so `cardtastic-web.vercel.app/cardtastic/` serves), then Games-Web, whose entry is now `cardtastic` with `formerSlugs: ['solitaire']`: every `/solitaire/...` path 308s to the same page under `/cardtastic/` (Jay picked the redirect). Released the other way round, `/cardtastic/` 404s and `/solitaire/` redirects into it. InfiniteToken-WWW, JayDeaton-Web and the jayrdeaton README link `/cardtastic/` too. **Deployed 2026-11-11** (Jay OK'd it) from the uncommitted working tree, the way `deploy.yml` does it on a tag (`vercel pull`, `vercel build --prod`, `vercel deploy --prebuilt --prod`), so production is ahead of every commit and tag until the next release. A stale `web-build/solitaire` export from 2026-10-29 had been sitting in the gitignored `web-build/` and `vercel build` copied it into the output; it was deleted before the deploy, because a pre-shell build served on the same origin would read and rewrite players' `solitaire:` data in its old shape. `vercel build` ships whatever is in `web-build/`, so clear old exports before a local deploy. The DuelTastic release session released the hub right after (Games-Web v1.0.8, with DuelTastic's entry; InfiniteToken-WWW v1.0.16 and JayDeaton-Web v1.0.12 too). Checked through the hub the same day: `/solitaire`, `/solitaire/`, `/solitaire/play/klondike` and `/solitaire/loadout/ratslap` 308 to the same page under `/cardtastic/`, every `/cardtastic/` page and its first bundle answer 200, the home page and sitemap link `/cardtastic/`, and in a browser `/solitaire/` lands on Home at `/cardtastic/` with the origin's `solitaire:` saves still there and no console errors.
- **Native:** the scheme and the Android package are native, so they ride the new build the name change already needs; run `npx expo prebuild --clean` before a local build (the gitignored `ios/` and `android/` still carry the old names).


## The first Android build (2026-11-11)

Jay wants the first official Android build, built beside iOS's production build (`jrd ship build production`, which runs `eas build --profile production` and lets him pick All). What went in for it:
- **The adaptive and monochrome icon** (see "Icons and splash").
- **`android.blockedPermissions`:** `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW`, `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` and `WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, which Expo's template adds and nothing here uses (Lumber blocks the same three). What's left: `INTERNET`, `VIBRATE` and expo-audio's `MODIFY_AUDIO_SETTINGS`.
- **Every dependency current** (a new native build is due anyway): the 13 Expo SDK 58 patches (`npx expo install --fix`), `@rific/auto-paper` 0.15 and `@rific/heatmap` 0.11 (heatmap's fixes the calendar's today never moving past the day the app launched), `@expo/log-box` 57 -> 58 (it had been left on SDK 57's), typescript-eslint 8.72.2 and prettier 3.10. **Held back, both impossible for now:** TypeScript 7 (typescript-eslint 8.72, under the shared ESLint config, supports TypeScript below 6.1) and ESLint 10 (Expo's config pulls in `eslint-plugin-react` 7.37.5, its latest, which supports ESLint up to 9 and throws `contextOrFilename.getFilename is not a function` on 10). The SDK-pinned packages (react, react-dom, async-storage, gesture-handler, svg, worklets) follow the SDK.
- **Checked:** lint (0 errors), typecheck, all 152 suites (2,247 tests; under a load average over 300 the plain run failed 7 suites, every one of which passed rerun on its own and in a full run with `--testTimeout=60000`), Expo Doctor (20 of 20), and the introspected config (the package, the icon, the three permissions marked removed).
- **Already right:** `android.package` `com.infinitetoken.cardtastic`, versionCode from EAS (`appVersionSource: remote`, `autoIncrement`), an app bundle for the production profile, target API 36 from SDK 58, `predictiveBackGestureEnabled: false` like the fleet's games, the splash.
- **Jay's at build and upload time:** the first Android build asks to make the upload keystore on EAS (yes). The app is created in Play Console (the store name "CardTastic: Solitaire & More", package `com.infinitetoken.cardtastic`), and its first `.aab` goes up by hand: Google's API can't make an app's first release, so `submit.production.android` (a service account key and a track) only matters from the second release. The Play listing, graphics, Data safety and content rating are the `app-store-prep` skill's Play reference. **Never run on Android:** no emulator or device has opened this app; a `preview` profile build is an APK the `Pixel_9` emulator can install.

## iPad: every orientation, and room for the window controls (2026-11-12)

Checked on the iPad 13" simulator before the App Store submission (the screenshot simulator, with a current bundle swapped in): in full screen, Home, Klondike and a RatSlap match lay out in all four orientations, and a match re-lays out when the iPad turns mid-match. Dragging iPadOS 26's resize corner put the app in a phone-sized window, where every screen re-laid out too, but the window's controls (close, minimize, tile) sat over the top-left corner: a game's Home, Home's trophy, the stats screen's and Profiles' Back and a setup screen's Back were under them, and a tap there opened the controls, so a game's Home couldn't be reached. **Fixed in `@tastic/shell` 0.8.3** (its CLAUDE.md, "Room for iPadOS's window controls"): on iPad the shell hands every screen the vertically corner-adapted safe area, the same as the regular one in full screen and lowered below the controls in a window, so in a window the whole app sits under them. Nothing in CardTastic changed: GameChrome, the table screens and the toaster already read `useSafeAreaInsets()`. Seen on the simulator with the change copied in (every one of those buttons clear of the controls and working; full screen and an iPhone in both orientations unchanged); not on a device. JavaScript only, no fingerprint change, but build 24 has the old bundle, so the submission needs a new build. Not looked at in a window: the table games' top rows (RatSlap's seat label sat beside the controls, not under them), Gin Rummy, Crazy Eights and Four Crowns.

## The Android window and the update cover (2026-11-15)

Jay saw CardTastic's splash give way to white on Android, and Lumber stay white after an OTA update. The splash gate (the shell's `fonts`, `theme` and `profiles`) is not it: it holds the splash until the felt is drawn, and @tastic/core sets the window black as the JS loads. Two other things show white:
