- **expo-updates' reload cover.** Accepting an update calls `reloadAsync()`, and with no options expo-updates covers the app with a white screen and a spinner while the new bundle loads, on iOS too. `@rific/updater` 0.9.0 (released 2026-11-15, installed here) passes the splash instead: the colors from app.json's expo-splash-screen plugin by itself (`#1E5C47`, `#123F30` in dark mode; a plugin's `ios`/`android` overrides apply on their platform), the art from `setReloadSplash({ image: require('../../assets/images/splash-light.png') })` in `src/app/_layout.tsx` (the dark file is the same). It reaches every `useUpdater`, including hud's update dialog and Settings row. Every app on `@rific/updater` got the same line the same day (Jay: all of them). JS only, so it ships in an OTA update, but only to a bundle that has it: the first update after this one restarts behind the splash. The cover still lifts before the felt: Android takes it down as soon as the new bundle has run, iOS at the first content, and the gates can't hold it (the splash already hid in that launch, and expo-updates' calls to hold its cover are debug-only). So a reload goes splash, a moment of the black window, then the felt. Painting the window the felt instead of black would be @tastic/core's and the shell's change.
- **The Android window.** Nothing set it in app.json, so it was Android's default, white in light mode. It shows before the JS sets it black: a window Android opens for a process it kept alive (the splash's hold flag is process-wide and already off, so the splash drops on the first frame while the profiles load), or a JS failure. `android.backgroundColor` is now `#123F30`, the dark felt (expo-system-ui's plugin writes it as the theme's `android:windowBackground`; one color for both modes). Native: it takes the next build.
- A development build still shows Metro's white loading screen while it downloads the bundle.
- Not seen on a device: no release Android build of today's code was run.

## Quit Match? on the tables (2026-11-15)

A two-player match isn't saved, and until now the table's Home button, Android's back and the browser's back all left one at once. Now each asks Quit Match? while a match is under way (Jay, the same day, after core 0.17, hud 0.30.1 and shell 0.9.0 went in: shell 0.9.0's `useBackBlock` is the block).
- **The pieces** (`games/table/QuitMatch.tsx`): `useQuitMatch()` (the dialog's state), called before each screen's match hook so `quit.asking` holds the table like Settings does (`dialogOpen = settingsOpen || quit.asking`, in `paused` and in every `!settingsOpen` gate); `QuitMatchDialog` (after the match hook, beside `SettingsDialog`), which blocks every way off the table with `useBackBlock` and draws hud's `ConfirmDialog` worded as DuelTastic's and PuckTastic's (Quit Match?, Quit / Cancel, `alert-circle-outline`, not destructive), with "This match won't be saved." where they show the score. Wired into all eight table screens (RatSlap and Slapjack share `SlapTable`).
- **Under way** (`useMatchUnderWay(state, over)`): the match's state object has moved on since it was dealt, and the match isn't over (`state.winner`, `phase === 'matchOver'`, Crazy Eights' `over`). A fresh deal, a finished match and a rematch's deal leave at once, as a match at 0-0 does in the fleet's games. Between hands, holes and rounds it's still under way, so that card's Home asks too.
- **Where Quit goes:** back (`safeBack`, to the game's setup screen) from Android's or the browser's back; Home (`router.dismissTo('/')`) from a Home button. Every Home button on a table (the side buttons and `TableOverlay`'s) is `tableHome`, which marks a module-level flag and navigates as before: the block stops it and `quit.blocked` reads the flag. That's `useBackBlock`'s third argument, `leave`, added for this in `@tastic/shell` 0.10.0 (released the same day with `@tastic/edge-guard` 0.2.0, whose `edgeGuardScreenOptions` the shell's root now imports; CardTastic passes `edgeGuard: false`, so Edge Guard's Android half does nothing here).
- **Also unchanged:** the pass cover still swallows Android's back (`PassCover`'s own listener, the newest); the browser's back over it opens Quit Match?. While Quit Match? is up, Android's back cancels it (ConfirmDialog's listener). On the web the block also asks before the page unloads, mid-match.
- **Face to face** the dialog is upright for player 1, as the Settings sheet is.
- Tests: `games/table/quitMatch.test.tsx` runs the real side buttons and dialog under expo-router's real in-memory router (`jest.unmock('expo-router')`; the app-wide stand-in gained a `usePreventRemove` stub for every other suite): a fresh deal and a finished match leave at once, a played card asks from Home (Quit to Home) and from Android's and the router's back (Quit to the setup screen), Cancel plays on, Android's back closes the dialog, a rematch starts over. Five fail with the block off, and the Home case with `leave` left out. Not checked on a device.

## Web dev server memory (2026-11-16)

The web dev server (`npx expo start --web`) ran out of memory after a couple dozen page loads, the leak HexFleet found (its CLAUDE.md, "Web dev server memory", has the cause and how it was measured). With `"output": "static"`, Expo 58's dev server runs the whole server bundle again for every page request, and `@rific/updater` before 0.9.1 loaded expo-updates on web, which tied each run to the next, so none were freed. 0.9.1 never loads expo-updates on web, and on `^0.9.1` the server holds.

- **Measured** (20 loads of `/` with `curl`, memory as `top -l 1 -pid <pid> -stats mem` reports it, since resident memory swings with macOS's compression and says little): on 0.9.0, 2.2 GB climbing to 5.9 GB; on 0.9.1 it rises and falls between 2 and 3 GB and is back to 1.1 GB idle.
- **No Metro shims:** HexFleet also needed some for Skia; CardTastic has no Skia.
- **Checked in a browser:** the menu loads with no console errors.

## Metro cache key (2026-11-16)

`metro.config.js` sets Metro's `cacheVersion` to a hash of this app's public config (`getConfig(__dirname, { isPublicConfig: true })`). It's the file's only job, so it isn't boilerplate. On the web, Expo writes the app config into expo-constants as it bundles (what `Constants.expoConfig` reads, the web tab title included), but Metro's cache key leaves the config out, and every app on this Mac shares one cache folder (`$TMPDIR/metro-cache`). So an app on the same package versions as another was served the other's config: Swirlio's dev server showed `<title>Labyrinth</title>` on 2026-11-16, and on 2026-10-14 every exported title read "LightCycles". The key keeps this app's cache apart from the others and refreshes it whenever the config changes (a version bump included), at the cost of one cold bundle after each change.

- The same lines are in HexFleet, Lumber, CardTastic, DuelTastic, PuckTastic, Hangman (`metro.config.cjs`) and Swirlio. Drop them once Expo puts the app config in its own cache key.
- Expo's fingerprint doesn't read Metro config, so this changes no runtime version and needs no native build.
- Checked in Swirlio: with Labyrinth's entries in the cache, its dev server got a key of its own and served its own title and config. In the other apps only loading the config was checked.

## Android: a flight leaves in the commit that mounts its ghost (2026-11-17)

Jay: on Android "the cards fly together well, but there is latency between the press (with feedback) and the cards flying". Every flight's ghost stays invisible until its faces report painted (`useGhostPaintGate`, see the take-off paragraph), and that report was each face Image's `onLoad`. On Android the wait bought nothing: React Native's Android Image is Fresco's, which gives a view a bitmap already in its memory cache the moment the view attaches, in the frame it is mounted (`AbstractDraweeController.submitRequest` looks in the cache before it fetches), while the `onLoad` reaches JS on a later frame and what JS changes for it is mounted on the one after. So every tap, draw, drop, undo and auto-play stood still two or three frames, more while JS was busy, over a card already drawn.
- **The fix** (`cardFacePaint.ts`' `noteFaceDecoded`/`isFaceDecoded`, `PlayingCard`'s `CardFaceImage`): on Android, a face bitmap that has loaded once on this run counts as painted in the commit that mounts it: `CardFaceImage` calls `onPainted` from its mount layout effect (through a ref, since a ghost hands in a new callback every render) and reports nothing as loading. So a ghost shows, and its spring starts, in the commit that mounts it; a landed ghost goes in the landing's commit; a card turned face-up shows its face at once; a take-off hold reveals at once; the deal and the win cascade take off without waiting. iOS and the web are unchanged (iOS fills even a cached image in off the main thread).
- **Keyed by source and drawn size:** an OTA update's images are file:// paths, which React Native decodes at the view's size, so their cache entries are per size. The set starts over when the face set changes (card style, or Invert Dark Mode Colors): a bitmap on screen is never dropped from the cache, but one that isn't can be (Fresco's cache is a quarter of the heap; one style's 52 faces are about 25MB). A face counted that was dropped after all would be blank for a frame or two.
- **Still waits:** a bitmap's first load on a run (the first pass through a stock, a card turned up for the first time). Every face seen once is quick from then on, across deals.
- **What is left between press and flight:** the press itself (`onPress` fires on lift-off, as on iOS; the buzz is at touch-down), the move's JS render of the board, and a frame for Android to mount it. expo-audio's Android `play()` blocks the JS thread until the main thread is free (`runBlocking`; feedback-press defers it a tick); not changed.
- **Not checked on Android:** no build of this was run (the emulator has none, and the disk is too full for a local one). JS only, so it ships in an OTA update. Pinned by `playingCardFacePaint.test.tsx`'s Android block (three cases fail with `isFaceDecoded` switched off).

## Played on the win card, par on a hold (2026-11-17)

Chris, a coworker of Jay's who plays a lot, asked for more stats, a clearer par and how many games he'd played. Games Played was already the first row of the Stats screen's Overall well, so he had never found the screen (the trophy on Home); par was only ever seen on the win card, which switches between expert par and the player's own without saying so. The first pass puts both where he already looks. Nothing new is stored.
- **The win card counts the rule set** (`WinBanner`, under "Score N% under your par", the two lines grouped): "112 played · 37 won · 33%", this win counted. It's `useGameStats`' `lastWin.record` (`{ played, won }`), read from the store right after `recordDealWon` records the win (`shownRecord` in `stores/gameStats.tsx`): the player's own bucket while any profile exists (a profile's, or guest's), the phone's totals while none does, as the Stats screen first shows them (guest's own count only started 2026-10-22). One line at 360pt ("played · won · %", not "win rate": 4-digit counts with "win rate" wrapped there). A win reached again after an Undo shows the same record, since it isn't recorded twice. A test's mock without a record shows no line.
- **Holding the score shows par** (Jay didn't want anything more in the bottom band, and a tap already shows and hides the controls): `GameChrome`'s scoreboard is a `FooterPressable` now (feedback-press's own `Pressable` plays 'notification', the invalid-move sound, on a long press) with `onLongPress` at `HOLD_LONG_PRESS_MS`, showing `showParToast` (`games/shared/gameToasts.ts`, the `golf` badge): "Your par is 187 / Middle of last 7 wins", "From your one win so far" with one, "Expert par is 320 / Win one to set your own" before any. Each caption fits one line on a 360pt phone; the first draft's "Middle score of your last 7 wins here" left "here" alone on a second line at 402. Sideways the readout column takes a tap as well as a hold (it hides nothing there; it was `pointerEvents='none'` before). The par is read when asked (`useGameStats`' `getPlayerPar(variant)`, from the store), so no render subscribes to recent wins. `GameChrome` takes the deal's `variant` instead of a `par` prop, and the Rules sheet's Scoring paragraph says "Hold the score to see your par."
- **Not done, offered:** a chart of the last 20 wins with both pars as lines (the data is `recentWins`), the last 30 days' played/won/rate from the day log, and cards to the foundation on a loss (new tracking). Also a Home tile count or a chart icon in place of the trophy, both the shell's.
- Pinned by `winBanner.test.tsx`'s played-and-won describe, `stores/playerData.test.tsx`'s "the win dialog's played and won" (Everyone with no profiles, the player's own with them, guest's own with them) and "a player's par, when they hold the score", and `gameChromePar.test.tsx` (the hold, the three captions, no invalid sound, the tap still toggling, nothing on a won deal, the sideways tap). Seen in headless Chrome over CDP on the web dev server with the screenshot seed (`store/seed/seed.json` written into localStorage): a hold at 402x874 and 375x812 and a sideways tap at 874x402 showed "Your par is 187 / Middle of last 7 wins" on one line with the controls left up, and winning the seeded FreeCell deal showed "12 played · 8 won · 67%" (the seed's 11 and 7 plus this win). The Browser pane was hidden (a 0x0 window, and React Native Web ignores synthetic pointer events), hence CDP's real mouse input. Not seen on a device: a long press on iOS or Android.

## The 2026-11-17 @tastic releases (2026-11-17)

Moved onto the `@tastic` versions published 2026-11-17: core 0.18.0, hud 0.34.0, profile 0.19.0, shell 0.11.0, split-screen 0.6.0, cards 0.6.1, achievements 0.6.1 and edge-guard 0.2.1 (0.x caret ranges, so each needed its range raised). Checked with `npm run verify` (155 suites, 2274 tests; lint's one warning is the gitignored `expo-env.d.ts`, which Expo rewrites without a final newline).

- **No call site changed.** `useCompactDialog()` (RulesDialog, TableChrome, PassCover, WinBanner, LossBanner) was already called with no argument, and `useFrameAutoAlign` no longer imported `PopoverRotation`; only its comment still named hud's `RotatedFrame`, now `ScreenFrame`. Nothing here used the rest of what they removed: core's inert orientation helpers (`OrientationProvider`, `useRotation`, `useRotatedWindowDimensions`, `useRotationAwareStatusBar`, `getViewRotation`, `getFixedZoneRotation`, `getOpposingZoneRotation`), hud's `rotation` props and Lock Orientation row, profile's `loadSharedProfiles` and pickers' `rotation`, and split-screen's `@tastic/core` re-exports.
- **shell 0.11's new `expo-audio` peer** (>=58.0.11) was already a dependency (~58.0.12). Its stats screen, Profiles and toaster now sit in hud's `ScreenFrame` (the guide and `RotatedFrame` bullets above say so).
- **Also current:** `@rific/toaster` ^0.13.1 (a toast's own `duration`, `Infinity` to stay; nothing here sets one), auto-paper 0.15.1, core 0.5.1, feedback-press 0.14.3, gestures 0.1.4, heatmap 0.11.1 and updater 0.9.2, and every other range raised to what's installed (expo ~58.0.26, babel-preset-expo ~58.0.26, RTK 2.14, Paper 5.15.5, RNTL 14.1, test-renderer 1.3.1, typescript-eslint 8.73 and the rest). `npx expo install --fix` moved expo-router to 58.0.32, expo-dev-client to 58.0.21 and jest-expo to 58.0.15; `npx expo install --check` is clean.
- **Needs a new native build before the next OTA update.** The Expo patches change native files (expo, expo-dev-launcher, expo-file-system, expo-router), so the fingerprint runtime version moves and an update published from here won't reach the current store build. That includes the JS-only Android flight fix above: to send it as an OTA update to the current build, publish it from a tree without this bump. The `@tastic` changes themselves are JS only (profile 0.19's native module is unchanged).
- **Still can't move:** TypeScript 7 (typescript-eslint 8.73 still takes TypeScript below 6.1), ESLint 10 and Babel 8, plus the SDK's own pins.

## Daily Deals (2026-11-18)

Online leaderboards start here (Tastic-Server's CLAUDE.md, "Daily leaderboards", has the server's half): each of the eleven solitaire games deals one shuffle a day under its default rules, the same for everyone, and the server replays each win before ranking it. Jay's choices: the Daily lives on a **setup screen for each solitaire game**, not a tab (he didn't want games listed twice) and not New Game (the clock would start without anyone choosing it); a profile's **first Daily win asks once** before it shows on a board, and **Guest never shows** (since 2026-11-20 a guest names a profile before playing, see "A player per profile online"); **Hint and Auto Hint are off** in a Daily (Undo stays, and undone moves still count). Players show as their tag and color, never a name. The server side is live (Tastic-Server v0.0.5, play.infinitetoken.com); nothing here was seen on a device.

- **The rules are `@tastic/cardtastic-engine`** (`~/Developer/CardTastic-Engine`, `^0.2.0`). `src/engine/<file>.ts` (all but `types`, `par`, `topWins`, `run`) and every solitaire `games/<id>/rules.ts` only re-export it, so imports and test mocks stayed put; a game's controller factory is a plain `export const` so a test can spy on it (a re-export's binding can't be replaced). **A rules change goes in the package**, released, then the server, then the app, which must share its `RULES_VERSION` to post (any other gets 426).
- **Every deal keeps its run** (`engine/run.ts`, `useGameSession`): `reset(seed)` starts it, `registerMove(action)` records each counted move or draw at the same line it's counted (25 places, the compiler finds any missed), `registerUndo()` each undo where the undo stack pops, and saves carry it (`SavedGame.run`). Each screen's suite has a `the deal run` test that plays a real seed and replays the save (`testUtils/gameInProgressStore.ts`' `expectSavedRunToReplay`, `movableCardIds`).
- **The setup screen** is the shell's one-player loadout (`games/shared/solitaireLoadout.tsx`, every solitaire definition's `useLoadout`): the name, then who's playing (Jay, 2026-11-18: the picker always comes first; every CardTastic loadout sets `placesSeats`, and `MatchSetup` draws the shell's `seats` there, with one person; two people keep theirs in their halves), then the rules card and `components/daily/TodaysDeal.tsx`, those two side by side in a wide window (`MatchSetup`'s `extra`). Home has no player button any more (the pack's `homePlayerPicker: false`, Jay, 2026-11-18: every game passes a setup screen), so its hero takes that room and Manage Profiles and the Color page are reached from a setup screen's picker. Every play screen's top-left button is a back arrow to its game's setup screen (`GameChrome`, and `useTableBack` on the tables, with Quit Match? while a match is under way; Jay, 2026-11-18), through `router.dismissTo`, which opens the setup screen if it isn't beneath (a cold-launch resume, a reloaded page). A table's result card says Back too. The shell options here came with `@tastic/shell` 0.12.0. Ready starts or continues the regular game. A tap on Today's Deal starts the server's clock (`stores/daily.ts`' `startDailyDeal`) and opens `/play/<game>?daily=<day>`.
- **A play screen in a Daily:** `games/shared/useDailyDeal.ts` reads the route, takes the seed, and sets `stores/dailyScope.ts` during the first render, ahead of the load effect. The scope is what keeps a Daily's save apart (`<owner>#daily`, stamped with its day, so yesterday's never resumes), and what GameChrome (Undo only, `Title · Daily Deal`, no Auto Hint) and WinBanner read, so the screens pass nothing. `useDealSettings` replaces the seven ruled games' rule settings with the defaults. The win goes to the server from an effect the moment `won` flips, since the server's clock runs until it arrives.
- **Each profile's player on the server is `@tastic/profile/online`'s** since 2026-11-20 (see "A player per profile online"). **`redux/dailySlice.ts`** (root store) keeps, per profile, whether they show on the boards (tied to their server player's id) and the last deal started in each game, with its result or the run still unsent. **No name goes to the server** (every player is named `CardTastic`), and the tag and color go only with a yes (`setDailyListed`) and when they change after (`syncDailyLook`).
- **`listed`:** the win card shows "You'd be 3rd today" until the player has said yes, because the run must reach the server when it's won but must not show. The profile's Edit Profile screen (its ONLINE section, once it has a player) turns showing on and off, for any profile, not only the one playing.
- **A relaunch keeps its place:** a game left open by closing the app still reopens by itself, and into the Daily if that's what was open (`dailySlice`'s `playing`, the pack's `resumeHref`), while it's still today's and unfinished.
- **The board** is `/daily/<game>` (`components/daily/DailyBoardScreen.tsx`): today's top 50 from the server, its numbers being the server's seconds, which keep counting while the app is away, unlike the app's own clock.
- **Server address:** `EXPO_PUBLIC_ONLINE_URL`, else `https://play.infinitetoken.com` (`stores/online.ts`). `.claude/launch.json`'s `cardtastic-web-local-server` runs the web dev server against a Tastic-Server on this Mac (port 4100).
- **Pinned by** `stores/daily.test.ts`, `games/shared/useDailyDeal.test.tsx`, `components/daily.test.tsx`, `stores/gameInProgress.test.ts`' Daily case, the eleven `the deal run` tests and `pack.test.ts`. **Seen** on the web dev server against a local Tastic-Server (402x874 and 874x402): the setup screen, starting a deal, a seeded Klondike Daily won through the UI, the server accepting its 136-action run, the first-win question, the yes, and the player on the board. **Not checked:** a device, Android, a whole Daily played from the deal, other games' Dailies through the UI (their runs are covered by the tests), and the Settings section on screen.

## A player per profile online (2026-11-20)

HexFleet's `ONLINE.md` section 9, follow-up 4 (Jay, 2026-11-20: a profile is the person online, and CardTastic and HexFleet work the same way): each profile that plays a Daily Deal has its own anonymous player on Tastic-Server through `@tastic/profile/online` (React-Native-Game-Profile's README, "Online players"; HexFleet's CLAUDE.md, "A player per profile online"), in place of the players and tokens `dailySlice` kept itself. Today's Daily players and their scores start over (Jay: online has only been on TestFlight); their keys were in `solitaire:root` and are no longer read.

- **`stores/online.ts`** makes the players: `createOnlinePlayers` for game `cardtastic`, handed `expo-secure-store` on a phone (the keys in the Keychain, readable after the first unlock) and nothing in a browser (the site's localStorage, under `cardtastic.online.*`), no notifications module (no pushes), and `nameOf: () => 'CardTastic'`, so no one's name reaches the server: its boards show a tag and color, never a name, and CardTastic has no invites that would. Players' `profileName` (the subpath's, from the roster) is what Delete Online Data names them by.
- **A guest names a profile first** (Jay, 2026-11-20, as HexFleet makes a guest's typed name a profile): a guest tapping Today's Deal or Today's Board gets Who's Playing? (`components/daily/NamePrompt.tsx`, hud's ConfirmDialog look with a name field: Cancel and Play, or Continue before the board), and the name makes a profile in the guest's own table color with its first letter as the tag (`tagOfName`), picked as seat 1 before the deal starts or the board opens. On iOS it shows in the other Tastic games' rosters, like any profile. Seen in headless Chrome against the local server: Emma made in Guest's pink with tag E, picked, and her Golf Daily started; Cancel makes nothing.
- **`stores/daily.ts`** makes every call as the profile's player (`online.ensure`, then `online.call`): a Daily is always a profile's, never Guest's (`dailyOwner()` is seat 1's profile or null; a call for Guest throws "Pick a profile to play the Daily", or "... to see today's board"). A key the server refuses is forgotten by the call and the call is tried once more with a new player. Errors are the subpath's `OnlineError`; `dailyErrorText` turns one it couldn't send into "The Daily needs an internet connection".
- **`dailySlice`** keeps per profile `{ player, listed, shownTag, shownColor }` and the deals. `playerJoined` (each call, with the id `ensure` returned) starts a profile over when its player is another than the one on file; `playersKept` (from `followOnlinePlayers`, once the players have loaded and on every change) forgets a profile whose player is gone or replaced: deleted with the profile, by Delete Online Data, or forgotten after a 401. A deleted profile is also forgotten by its reducer (`onProfileRemoved`).
- **Deleting:** the pack's `SolitaireProvider` mounts `OnlinePlayersSync` (`useOnlinePlayersSync` with the shell's roster), so a profile deleted here or in a sibling game takes its player off the server, retried at the next change or launch while the server can't be reached. **A profile's online life is on its own Edit Profile screen** (Jay, 2026-11-20: "delete online, show on public, all of that could be on edit profile"): the pack's `ProfileSections` (`components/daily/ProfileOnline.tsx`) draws the subpath's `ProfileOnlineSection` under Color, once that profile has played a Daily: Show on Public Boards (its tag and the color it plays under in CardTastic go up with a yes; `setDailyListed`'s `owner`), then Delete Online Data for that profile alone, keeping the profile, with CardTastic's message ("Kid's Daily Deal wins come off every board, ..."). Settings has no online section; its Profile row (the shell's, "Profile" over seat 1's name, on Home's and every setup screen's Settings, not a play screen's) opens that screen, and Profiles while seat 1 is Guest (Jay: straight to your own profile, especially with just the one). For a few hours on 2026-11-20 Settings had an ONLINE section of Delete Online Data for every profile at once (and before that, a DAILY DEALS section); `flushDailyDeletions`/`orphans` and the one-player Delete Daily Scores went with the move to the subpath.
- **Native:** `expo-secure-store` (`~58.0.3`, with its config plugin in `app.json`, `faceIDPermission: false` so no Face ID usage string is added; it also keeps the Keychain values out of Android's backups) is a native module, so this needs a new native build, not an OTA update.
- **Packages:** `@tastic/profile` 0.24.0 (the subpath, and `profileName`) and `@tastic/shell` 0.15.0 (`EditProfileRoute`, which every pack needs since profile 0.22 made each profile's edit a screen of its own: `src/app/profile/[id].tsx` renders it), both released 2026-11-20 for this. The move onto Edit Profile came with three more, released the same day: `@tastic/hud` 0.37.0 (Settings' `profilesRow`), `@tastic/profile` 0.25.0 (`EditProfileScreen`'s `sections`, `ProfileOnlineSection`, `EditProfileRow`) and `@tastic/shell` 0.16.0 (`showProfile`, `PackDefinition.ProfileSections`, and its own toaster at one toast).
- **Seen in headless Chrome** (402x874, against the Tastic-Server already running on this Mac): Profiles' New Profile opening the Edit Profile screen; Kid's Klondike Daily making one player named `CardTastic` with its key under `cardtastic.online.*`; Delete Online Data deleting the server player, the keys and the Daily state with a toast; deleting a profile deleting its server player and Daily state. Then, after the move onto Edit Profile (in the Browser pane): a setup screen's Settings showing "Profile · Jay", opening Jay's Edit Profile with ONLINE (Show on Public Boards, Delete Online Data) above Delete Profile. Not on a device: the Keychain, the keyboard over Who's Playing?, Android.
- **Tests:** `stores/daily.test.ts` runs the real `stores/online.ts` (an in-memory `expo-secure-store`) against a stand-in server: one player per profile named `CardTastic`, Guest never online, a refused key making a new player and starting the profile's Daily over, a deleted profile's player deleted with its Daily, Delete Online Data clearing every Daily. `components/daily.test.tsx`' guest describe (the name prompt making and picking a profile, then dealing or opening the board; Cancel) and Edit Profile describe (nothing before a profile plays online; Show on Public Boards for that profile, with the color it plays under; Delete Online Data for that profile alone), and `stores/daily.test.ts`' listing of another profile from its Edit Profile.

## The stats screen's pickers: captions, and Solitaire / Two Player (2026-11-20)

Jay: the stats screen's pills should say what they pick, and its game list (All Games and 20 games) wanted a Solitaire / Two Player split. Both are the shell's (`@tastic/shell` 0.17.0 on `@tastic/profile` 0.26.0, released the same day): the pills read PLAYER and GAME over their choice, and the game pill opens on All Games, Solitaire › and Two Player › (Home's tabs, the pack's `homeTabs`), each a page of an All row (the pill then reads SOLITAIRE or TWO PLAYER) and its games. Jay picked groups inside the one pill over a third pill, which would wrap on every phone.

- **The pack's sections take `gameIds`** (`PackStatsSectionsProps`, null for All Games): `SolitaireStatsSections` draws nothing unless the games picked include a solitaire one (`showsSolitaire`), so Two Player shows only the two-player games' wells, and Solitaire's All shows what All Games' solitaire part always did.
- **Records with a two-player game picked threw** (since the two-player games came: `RecordsList` labels an empty board with `GAME_TITLES[game]`, which only has the solitaire games). `RecordsTab` now says "Records are kept for solitaire games" for a two-player game or the Two Player tab.
- Tests: `solitaireStats.test.tsx` (nothing for the Two Player tab, everything for Solitaire's; Records for a two-player pick). Seen in headless Chrome on the web build (402x874): the captions, the menu's three rows, the Two Player page, its All showing the nine wells under TWO PLAYER, and Records' line.

## A Daily Deals calendar on Records (2026-11-20)

Jay: "Could the records have a heatmap for completed daily challenges deals?" The Records tab now leads with a DAILY DEALS well (`components/daily/DailyCalendar.tsx`): the Stats tab's calendar look (`PlayCalendar`'s `useCalendarLook`, shared), each day shaded by the Dailies won that day, a tapped day's count on the well's title line ("Today · 3 Dailies won"), today's with none tapped. It follows the pickers: one profile's wins, or every profile's added up under Everyone, in the games picked; never for Guest, who never plays one, and not on the Two Player group (Records says they're kept for solitaire games).

- **The history** (`redux/dailyHistorySlice.ts`, its own persisted key `dailyHistory`): each server player's won Dailies by local day, noted by `stores/daily.ts`' `noteWin` whenever the server takes a run (a post, a retry that finds it there, or a start that already has a result). Keyed by the server player's id, not the profile, and pruned on `dailyActions.playersKept`, so a player replaced or deleted (with its profile, or by Delete Online Data) takes its wins with it, as they go off the boards. Its own key so a store from before starts empty, with no code for it. Kept from 2026-11-20: CardTastic kept only each profile's latest Daily per game before, and every Daily player started over that day.
- **Days / Hours** stays the Stats tab's; this calendar is days only.
- Tests: `dailyCalendar.test.tsx` (the slice, the counts by player and game, the calendar's line), `stores/daily.test.ts`' history case, `solitaireStats.test.tsx` (the calendar leads Records, never for Guest). Seen in headless Chrome on the web build with a week of wins seeded for two profiles: under Everyone the well reads "Today · 6 Dailies won" and shades the seeded days.
